# General plan-authoring lessons

_Maintained by `plan-tool review` and `plan-tool detail`. Each bullet is a generalizable rule distilled from a finding. Lessons accumulate; prune by hand when an entry no longer applies._

- **For coordination / prerequisites steps that produce no code, `files: []` from create is correct, but the detail still needs to invent documentation artifact paths under the plan's own `plan-details/` folder.** — Step 1 here had no source files but legitimately produced three new docs (a detail file, a checklist artifact, and a README index). Future create proposals shouldn't be flagged for missing files when the step is purely a handshake. _(e.g. Detail drafted `01-prerequisites.md`, `prerequisites-checklist.md`, and `README.md` under `docs/implementation-plans/plan-details/vendor-sftp-data-integration/`.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:vendor-sftp-data-integration#1</sub>
- **Before drafting a detail under `plan-details/<plan-slug>/`, glob for sibling plan-detail folders and mirror their README + numbered-file convention.** — Conventions live in sibling folders, not in the plan tool's defaults. Discovering them mid-draft (rather than at create time) costs reads. Create-time audit should surface the closest sibling folder as a template hint. _(e.g. `docs/implementation-plans/plan-details/partner-sftp-transfer-family/README.md` was the load-bearing template for this run.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:vendor-sftp-data-integration#1</sub>
- **For partner / vendor integration plans, step 1 should force one decisive design question to writing (e.g. "who hosts the server, us or them?") before any infra work — and the acceptance criteria should make that single answer the gate.** — A single fork at the start of a vendor integration often dictates the entire downstream shape (here: SFTP connector vs Transfer Family user). Plans that bury this in step 4 cause rework. _(e.g. Checklist item 1 (connection direction) determined whether step 4 builds an outbound SFTP connector or adds a user under the existing Transfer Family server.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:vendor-sftp-data-integration#1</sub>
- **Coordination steps have no executable tests; verification should be a filled-in artifact (checklist with `status` and `confirmed by` columns), and acceptance criteria should explicitly forbid code changes in that step.** — Generic "add tests" guidance is harmful here. Reviewers need a binary signal ("every row `confirmed`") and a `git diff` invariant to confirm scope wasn't violated. _(e.g. Acceptance criterion: `git diff amplify/ scripts/` against main returns no changes attributable to step 1.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:vendor-sftp-data-integration#1</sub>
- **Before writing the detail, search the codebase for any existing integration with the same vendor — even on an unrelated surface — and explicitly disambiguate.** — Vendor names collide across surfaces (web scraper vs SFTP feed vs API). Failing to disambiguate leads readers and later steps to grab the wrong existing secret / SSM path / role. _(e.g. `amplify/resources/vendor-scraper.ts` handles Vendor's web console; it shares the vendor name but offers no usable SFTP host. The detail had to call this out so step 4 doesn't reuse `/example/app/{instanceName}/vendor/credentials` for SFTP.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:vendor-sftp-data-integration#1</sub>
- **Detail's "Deferred" section should list each plausible-but-wrong piece of pre-staging work and route it to the correct downstream step.** — Without this, the next implementer either re-litigates scope or pre-stages the wrong shape (e.g. wrong Secrets Manager schema). Naming the deferred items by file + line keeps scope tight and makes the next detail's job easier. _(e.g. Deferred section pointed Secrets Manager placeholder and SG ingress tightening to step 4, schema docs to step 2, cadence to step 3.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:vendor-sftp-data-integration#1</sub>

- **When the plan body names a target directory for new code (e.g. "types belong in `packages/shared/src/types/`"), create should propagate that hint into the step's `files` list as an anchor — even if the exact filename isn't decided yet — instead of leaving `files: []`.** — The directory is the load-bearing decision; the filename is mechanical. Leaving `files: []` makes the step look like pure coordination, which mis-routes complexity and forces detail to re-derive the location from the plan prose. _(e.g. Plan step 2 body said types belong in `packages/shared/src/types/` but create emitted `files: []`. A placeholder like `packages/shared/src/types/<vendor>.ts (new)` would have set the right expectation.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:vendor-sftp-data-integration#2</sub>
- **Steps that produce a canonical contract artifact (file-format spec, API ICD, message schema) are M-complexity minimum and not "unknown" — even when they look like "just docs". The artifact must include version + sign-off block, cross-flow conventions, per-flow column tables, controlled-vocabulary enums, and failure-mode rules.** — These steps gate every downstream implementation step and require structured content the team will cite by version. Treating them as "unknown" or trivial under-budgets review and produces ad-hoc docs that drift. _(e.g. Step 2 here had to specify version, encoding (UTF-8/RFC 4180), date/timestamp (ISO 8601), currency convention (integer cents), file-naming pattern, identifier mapping, and per-row column tables for three flows — none of which the create heuristic surfaced.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:vendor-sftp-data-integration#2</sub>
- **Schema-definition steps for vendor data feeds should keep all flows in a single canonical doc (`file-schemas.md` or equivalent), not split per flow, so cross-flow conventions (encoding, dates, idempotency, identifiers) live in one place.** — Three near-identical "Conventions" sections drift. A single doc + per-flow column-table sections keeps the contract reviewable and prevents partial revisions from missing one flow. _(e.g. Detail consolidated eligibility, program-interest, and program-status into one `file-schemas.md` with shared "Cross-flow conventions" — same pattern step 1 used for its single prerequisites checklist.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:vendor-sftp-data-integration#2</sub>
- **For schema-definition steps, sample/fixture rows belong next to the schema doc under `plan-details/.../fixtures/`, not in the project's test directory. They are spec illustrations; downstream parser/generator steps may copy them into real test fixtures later.** — Placing fixtures under `amplify/test/` or app test directories before any parser exists ties the spec artifact to a runtime layer that doesn't exist yet, and complicates the eventual code-vs-spec ownership split. _(e.g. Step 2 placed `eligibility.sample.csv`, `program-interest.sample.csv`, `program-status.sample.csv` under `plan-details/<plan-slug>/fixtures/`. Step 5 (parser) is free to copy them when it builds the runtime tests.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:vendor-sftp-data-integration#2</sub>
- **Vendor data-feed schemas should require a `rowVersion` (or equivalent monotonic last-modified) column on every row type so the inbound consumer gets free idempotency without a dedup table. Force this into the schema-definition step's acceptance criteria.** — Adding it later requires a schema-version bump and partner re-sign-off; designing it in costs the partner nothing and saves the downstream Lambda meaningful complexity. _(e.g. Step 2's column tables marked `rowVersion` (integer, monotonic) as required on all three flows so step 5's ingest Lambda can skip stale rows on retry.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:vendor-sftp-data-integration#2</sub>
- **When a step produces both a documentation artifact and lightweight code (e.g. strawman types that mirror the doc), the detail's acceptance criteria must require the two to be checked against each other (same enum values, same casing, same column order) — not just that each compiles.** — The whole point of generating the strawman types alongside the doc is to catch "doc says X but code shows Y" drift. Compilation alone doesn't catch enum-value mismatches. _(e.g. Step 2 added an acceptance criterion that the doc's `ProgramStatus` vocabulary matches the TypeScript string-literal union exactly (same values, same casing).)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:vendor-sftp-data-integration#2</sub>

- **When a step wires together infrastructure (schedules + S3/event triggers + IAM + new Lambdas), create-time `files: []` is reliably under-specified — these steps produce a predictable skeleton (one new `resources/<feature>.ts`, N Lambda handler folders, edits to the lambda registry, edits to `resources/index.ts`, edits to the backend orchestration file). Create should propose that skeleton instead of leaving files empty.** — Empty `files` mis-routes complexity rating and forces detail to re-derive the whole layout. The shape is mechanical once you know it's an infra-wiring step. _(e.g. Step 4 here was rated `unknown` with `files: []` but legitimately added `amplify/resources/vendor-sftp.ts`, three handler dirs under `amplify/lambda/`, plus edits to `amplify/backend.ts`, `amplify/lambda/resource.ts`, `amplify/resources/index.ts`, and `amplify/resources/secrets.ts`.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:vendor-sftp-data-integration#4</sub>
- **When a downstream step's shape depends on a Path-A-vs-Path-B fork decided in an earlier step, the detail should describe **both branches** with explicit `delete the unused branch once step N lands` instructions — not pick one and quietly hope.** — Picking arbitrarily commits to a direction the team hasn't decided. Describing both keeps the detail valid regardless of how step N closes, and the implementer's delete-step is mechanical. _(e.g. Step 4 here described Path A (we run an SFTP connector) and Path B (Vendor logs into our existing Transfer Family server) side-by-side because step 1's checklist row 1 hadn't been answered yet.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:vendor-sftp-data-integration#4</sub>
- **Infrastructure-wiring steps need two distinct test surfaces in the Tests section: (a) `Template.fromStack(...)` synth-assertion tests that the right resources/properties exist, and (b) handler unit tests with AWS SDK mocks. Stating only one (commonly only the handler tests) leaves CDK regressions invisible.** — Synth tests catch wiring drift (wrong prefix filter, missing IAM grant, dropped schedule) that handler unit tests can't see, and vice versa. _(e.g. Step 4 added `amplify/test/resources/vendor-sftp.test.ts` for synth assertions alongside per-Lambda handler tests, with the synth test asserting prefix filter + DLQ + SSM outputs explicitly.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:vendor-sftp-data-integration#4</sub>
- **When a step's logic depends on an artifact that's defined in an earlier *not-yet-drafted* step (a predicate, a row schema, a cron expression), ship a stub with an explicit `TODO(step-N)` comment and route the missing dep to a Deferred bullet — don't fabricate the artifact.** — Fabricated artifacts create silent contracts that diverge from the actual step-N output and force re-work. Stubs + Deferred make the dependency loud. _(e.g. Step 4's eligibility predicate stub returns `false` with a `TODO(step-3)` comment; the program-interest generator is a no-op until step 5's `VendorProgramInterest` model exists, and both gaps are listed in Deferred.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:vendor-sftp-data-integration#4</sub>
- **For scheduled-job steps whose downstream rows aren't ready yet, ship the schedule + Lambda as a no-op heartbeat anyway, and call that out in acceptance criteria — it gives the alerting step a real metric stream to attach to.** — Without the heartbeat, the alerting step (typically the next one) has nothing to alarm on and either gets deferred or fakes data. _(e.g. Step 4's `vendorProgramInterestGenerator` fires on cadence and logs `no-op` until step 5 lands the table, so step 6's alarms have a `Duration` + `Errors` stream from day one.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:vendor-sftp-data-integration#4</sub>

- **When step N intentionally leaves stubs (TODO comments, no-op short-circuits, hand-off placeholders) for step N+1 to replace, step N+1's detail MUST enumerate the exact stub locations (file:line) and turn their removal into acceptance criteria (e.g. 'file no longer contains TODO(step-3)').** — Without explicit handoff citations, the next step either misses a stub (silent dead code) or rewrites surrounding logic out of scope. Pinning stub locations and asserting their absence makes the seam between steps reviewable. _(e.g. Step 5 had to cite step-4's `TODO(step-3)` predicate (line 97), `console.warn('… no-op')` short-circuit (line 102), and 'actual row→DynamoDB write is step 5' marker (line 107), then list 'handler.ts no longer contains TODO(step-3)' as an acceptance criterion.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:vendor-sftp-data-integration#5</sub>
- **When step N consumes a frozen contract from an earlier step M (wire types, fixtures, schema doc), step N's acceptance criteria should include a `git diff <artifact-path>` returns no changes invariant against the base branch.** — Contracts drift silently when the consuming step is allowed to edit them. A literal diff-empty assertion locks the boundary and makes 'I had to change the types' show up as a failed criterion rather than a quiet PR comment. _(e.g. Step 5's criterion: `git diff packages/shared/src/types/vendor.ts against main returns no changes — step 5 does not alter the wire types step 2 froze`.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:vendor-sftp-data-integration#5</sub>
- **When two artifacts represent two views of the same data (e.g. a file-row schema and a DB model, an API DTO and a domain type), the load-bearing test is column-list / field-list equality between them — call it out explicitly in the Tests section so schema drift surfaces immediately.** — Most divergence bugs in dual-view data come from one side adding a field. A single test asserting `dbModel.columns === rowSpec.columns` catches every future drift in O(1) reviewer attention. _(e.g. Step 5's mapper test: `userToEligibilityRow produces a row whose columns exactly match eligibilityColumnSpec.map(c => c.name)` — same for the other two mappers.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:vendor-sftp-data-integration#5</sub>
- **`depends_on` in create proposals should capture transitive artifact dependencies (types, fixtures, predicates, controlled vocabularies consumed by the step), not just the immediately-prior sequential step.** — A step that follows step 4 in sequence may still consume types from step 2 and rules from step 3. If depends_on lists only [4], reviewers and downstream steps mis-read the dependency graph and may reorder or parallelize incorrectly. _(e.g. Step 5 declared `depends_on: 4`, but actually consumed step 2's `VendorSchemaVersion` wire types, step 3's eligibility predicate, and step 4's CDK plumbing. Correct value: [2, 3, 4].)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:vendor-sftp-data-integration#5</sub>
- **Steps that introduce new DB models AND new Lambda handlers AND new shared utility modules are L-complexity minimum — never 'unknown'. Compound-surface steps have a known floor regardless of estimation uncertainty in the body.** — 'unknown' under-budgets reviewer attention and lets the step skate past the create-time complexity sanity check. Any one of {new model, new handler, new shared util} is M; combining them is L by construction. _(e.g. Step 5's create row had `complexity: unknown`; the actual detail introduced 4 new DDB models, 1 new SQS-consumer Lambda, 2 new utility modules, and 8 test files.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:vendor-sftp-data-integration#5</sub>

- **Heuristic 'step looks already done' matches on a single short common-English token (≤4 chars, e.g. 'real', 'key', 'core') against generic project docs are almost always false positives — require ≥2-token overlap with the step title, or maintain a stopword list of common English words to skip.** — Three of this review's INFO findings on step 2 fired on the tokens 'real', 'key', and 'LaborDataService' matched against generic headings in `docs/backend-guide.md`, `docs/architecture.md`, and `docs/labor-data-service-runbook.md` — none of which evidence the step being implemented. The noise dilutes signal and trains reviewers to ignore the heuristic. _(e.g. Step 2 (presumably containing tokens like 'API Key' or 'Real-time') matched `### Real`, `## Key`, and `# LaborDataService` in unrelated docs.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via review:app-parameter-standardization</sub>
- **When every plan body section is a `_(TODO)_` placeholder AND zero steps have `detail:` declarations, the plan is a stub — review should collapse this into one finding (`stub plan: run plan-tool detail before re-reviewing`) instead of emitting one body-completeness finding per section plus one detail-coverage finding per step.** — This review produced 13 findings (7 placeholder + 5 detail-coverage + 1 stub-equivalent) for what is essentially a single condition: the author ran `create` and stopped. The noise hides any real signal and makes the lessons-distillation step itself unproductive (no findings reveal anything beyond 'the plan is empty'). _(e.g. Findings for `app-parameter-standardization`: 7 `body-completeness` placeholders covering every section + 5 `detail-coverage` step-missing-detail entries, with no substantive review signal.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via review:app-parameter-standardization</sub>
- **When `plan-tool create` finishes a plan whose body sections are all `_(TODO)_` placeholders, it should print a one-line next-step hint (e.g. "run `plan-tool detail` for each step, or edit the body sections, before requesting review") rather than treating the create step as complete.** — A stub plan that goes straight to review wastes a review pass — every section flags as a placeholder and every step flags as detail-missing, producing no actionable signal. A nudge at the end of `create` would shift the author into the detail/edit loop where the real authoring happens. _(e.g. This plan's review surfaced only stub-state findings; no part of the plan body had been filled in before review was invoked.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via review:app-parameter-standardization</sub>

- **Operability steps (error handling, logging, alerting) for partner / event-driven integrations should always include a heartbeat or watchdog for inbound flows — 'no event arrived' emits no Lambda invocation, so the existing per-Lambda Errors/Duration alarms have zero visibility into a silent feed pause.** — S3-event-triggered (and SQS-event-triggered) Lambdas only emit metrics when something happens; absence-of-file is invisible unless an out-of-band scheduled job emits a presence metric the alarm can evaluate. Easy to miss at create time; expensive to retrofit. _(e.g. Step 6's `vendorCadenceWatchdog` emits `APP/VendorSftp inbound_file_present` on a schedule, alarming with `treatMissingData: BREACHING` (heartbeat semantics — inverse of the per-Lambda alarms).)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:vendor-sftp-data-integration#6</sub>
- **When a structured-log error code is the load-bearing input to a CloudWatch metric filter (or any downstream pattern matcher), the detail must lock that string set as a TypeScript enum / union and call out the coupling explicitly — an `errorCode` typo in a handler silently disables the alarm.** — Free-text log messages drift; the alarm fires only when the filter pattern matches the exact code. This coupling is invisible at review time unless the detail surfaces it as a design invariant, not just an implementation detail. _(e.g. `VendorErrorCode` union in `vendor-logger.ts` is referenced by the `MetricFilter`s in `vendor-sftp.ts`; the acceptance criteria assert both sides cite the same strings.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:vendor-sftp-data-integration#6</sub>
- **Alarm declarations should document the `treatMissingData` choice per-alarm with a one-line reason, because the right answer flips between metric types — `NOT_BREACHING` for sporadic per-Lambda Errors, `BREACHING` for heartbeat/watchdog metrics — and the default is wrong half the time.** — Defaulting all alarms to `NOT_BREACHING` makes watchdogs useless (a dead watchdog never pages); defaulting to `BREACHING` makes per-Lambda alarms page constantly on cadence pauses. The asymmetry is non-obvious. _(e.g. Step 6 sets `NOT_BREACHING` on Lambda Errors but `BREACHING` on `inbound_file_present` — the detail's 'non-obvious design decisions' block calls this out.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:vendor-sftp-data-integration#6</sub>
- **For 'add logging + alarms' steps, `files: []` from create is misleading even though no new business logic is introduced — the step typically touches every Lambda handler produced by prior steps plus the partner's resource file plus new logger/watchdog modules. Create should anchor with `<partner>-sftp.ts (edit)` + `<every prior-step Lambda handler> (edit)` rather than leaving the row empty.** — An empty `files` row routes operability steps to 'unknown' complexity and under-budgets the work; the actual diff in step 6 touches 4 handlers, 1 resource file, adds 2 new modules, and 5 tests. _(e.g. Step 6's create row was `files: (none)` but the detail enumerated edits to four step-4/step-5 handlers plus `vendor-sftp.ts` plus `amplify/lambda/resource.ts`.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:vendor-sftp-data-integration#6</sub>
- **When a later step's scope was created by deferrals in earlier-step details, the later detail's 'Why' section should cite the exact `Deferred` bullet lines (path + line number) it is now picking up. Without this, reviewers can't trace whether the handoff is complete or whether anything fell through the cracks.** — Deferred lists are the only persistent record of scope handoffs between steps; if step N's detail doesn't cite step N-1's deferral, the trace is broken and reviewers must re-read prior steps to confirm coverage. _(e.g. Step 6's 'Why' cites `04-…md:223` and `05-…md:180-186` as the originating deferral lines for alarm wiring and parser failure surfaces.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:vendor-sftp-data-integration#6</sub>
- **Operability/instrumentation steps should explicitly state 'no new control flow' as a scope constraint — the step labels existing branches with structured logs and metrics, it does not introduce new try/catch, retries, or error-recovery paths. Without this guardrail, instrumentation steps blow scope and entangle with refactor work.** — It's tempting to 'while I'm here' add retries, swallow errors, or restructure handlers during instrumentation. That's a different step (resilience) and changing both at once makes failures hard to diagnose. _(e.g. Step 6's Implementation section: 'The handlers do not gain any try/catch they did not already have; step 6's job is to label existing branches, not to add new control flow.')_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:vendor-sftp-data-integration#6</sub>
- **For steps that produce alarms wired to a shared SNS topic, an acceptance criterion should be `git grep` over the new file confirming zero references to any other topic — preventing accidental introduction of a partner-specific topic that fragments the on-call routing.** — It's easy to declare a new `Topic` 'just for this integration' and inadvertently bypass the existing email subscriber. A grep invariant catches this in review. _(e.g. Step 6 acceptance: '`git grep` of `vendor-sftp.ts` returns zero references to any topic other than `config.alarmTopic`.')_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:vendor-sftp-data-integration#6</sub>

- **For steps that add or modify AWS service-level policy primitives (S3 lifecycle, IAM policy evaluation, KMS key policy, CloudFront cache behaviors), the detail must explicitly verify the service's rule-precedence semantics in the official AWS docs rather than assume 'more specific wins' or 'last writer wins'. Many AWS services pick the most restrictive option across overlapping rules, not the most specific.** — Detail-time intuition ("prefix-scoped rule overrides bucket-wide rule") is wrong for several common AWS services and silently produces designs that compile/synth/deploy cleanly but enforce the wrong policy. The cost of being wrong is invisible (S3 deletes files years later) rather than loud (a CloudFormation error). _(e.g. Step 7 here proposed a `prefix: 'vendor/'` rule with `expiration: 2555 days` layered on top of the bucket-wide `expiration: 365 days` rule, asserting the prefix rule would 'supersede' the baseline. S3 actually applies the shorter expiration when rules overlap, so Vendor files would still be deleted at 365 days. Detail should have cited the AWS docs section 'Lifecycle configuration conflicts and resolution' before designing the override.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:vendor-sftp-data-integration#7</sub>
- **For a step that extends multi-step base infrastructure (depends_on chains 2+ deep), the detail's first move should be to audit the base step's resources for *known gaps* — versioning enabled but no noncurrent-version rules, default encryption that's narrower than this step needs, open security groups not yet tightened, etc. — and either fix them in tandem or explicitly call out why they're left open.** — A step that builds on top of a base often inherits a half-finished posture from that base. The detail can't claim 'audit-grade retention' if the underlying bucket leaks noncurrent versions indefinitely. Auditing the base's gaps at detail-time is cheaper than rediscovering them at deploy-time. _(e.g. Step 7 depended (transitively) on step 4's partner bucket from `amplify/resources/sftp.ts`, which had `versioned: true` but zero `NoncurrentVersion*` clauses on its lifecycle rule — a gap step 4's detail didn't surface. Step 7's detail had to widen scope mid-draft to handle noncurrent versions, which should have been flagged at create time as a known dependency-chain gap.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:vendor-sftp-data-integration#7</sub>
- **When `complexity: unknown` and `files: []` come from create for a step whose title contains 'policy', 'retention', 'lifecycle', 'sign-off', 'archival', or 'compliance', upgrade to M-complexity minimum: these steps always require a decision artifact (versioned doc with sign-off block), an IaC change, and an out-of-band SSM/secret population — never just code.** — Policy/retention steps look like docs-only work but actually have three coupled deliverables. Leaving them at `unknown` under-budgets review and makes the detail draft the heuristic that create skipped. _(e.g. Step 7 here was `complexity: unknown, files: []` from create but produced: a new decision artifact (`retention-policy.md`), a new module (`vendor-retention.ts`), a partner-agnostic widening of `sftp.ts`, edits to `backend.ts`, two test files, and two new SSM parameters populated out-of-band — squarely M-complexity.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:vendor-sftp-data-integration#7</sub>

- **For "publish a canonical reference doc" steps, the detail must lock in the exact section list (in order, with H2 anchor titles) because later steps in the plan link to those anchors and verification greps for them. Don't leave section structure to be invented later.** — Without a fixed section list, step 2's corrections can't be written against stable links, and acceptance criteria like "grep '## Pipeline secret propagation'" become brittle. The doc structure is part of the contract this step ships. _(e.g. Step 1 here had to pin seven H2 sections (Scopes, Standard, Inventory, Cross-repo flow, Pipeline secret propagation, Bedrock parameter list, SecureString ownership model) — and acceptance criteria explicitly checked `grep -c '^## '` ≥ 7.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:app-parameter-standardization#1</sub>
- **Inventory tables in a standardization / catalog doc must pin every row to a `file:line` for both the *creator* and the *consumers*, not just "where it lives". Prose paragraphs can't be diffed against the codebase later; pinned tables can.** — Inventory drift is the failure mode for these docs. A row that says "`/foo/bar` — created by some CDK construct" goes stale silently; a row that says "created by `amplify/resources/outputs.ts:47`" produces a CI-greppable invariant. The detail should make the citation format mandatory, not optional. _(e.g. Step 1's section-3 inventory pinned each path to creator (e.g. `amplify/resources/outputs.ts:33-66`) and consumers (e.g. `amplify/bedrock/resource.ts:226-232`), enabling acceptance criteria that grep the codebase for each path.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:app-parameter-standardization#1</sub>
- **When the plan frontmatter declares a `lessons.md` path under `plan-details/<slug>/`, step 1's detail should scaffold that file (empty with a single H1) so future `plan-tool review`/`detail` runs can append without create-file races. Don't leave it as "step N will create it when needed".** — The lessons file is referenced by tooling and may be appended to by later steps in parallel — if step 1 doesn't create it, the first appender has to also handle the create case, and a missing-file no-op silently swallows the lesson. _(e.g. `1-app-parameter-standardization.md:6` declared `lessons_file: .../lessons.md`; step 1's detail explicitly scaffolds it alongside the README and `01-publish-standard.md`.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:app-parameter-standardization#1</sub>
- **When a step inventories multiple naming conventions that *look* interchangeable but aren't (different prefixes, different scope axes, different stores), the detail must list them as separate inventory categories AND explicitly assert non-interchangeability with a one-line justification. Otherwise the next step (or the next contributor) tries to collapse them.** — "Standardization" reads as "pick one and migrate everything". When the right answer is "keep N distinct conventions because they have different lifecycles", the doc has to say so out loud — at the row level (Scope column) and at the prose level. Implicit non-interchangeability gets lost. _(e.g. Step 1 here had to spell out: `/app/*` (runtime config owned by amplify) is NOT interchangeable with `/example/app/*` (credential bundles), instance-scope is NOT interchangeable with environment-scope (AssessmentService is env, LaborDataService is instance — both correct), and the amplify-repo path patterns are NOT interchangeable with the infra-repo flat-dash pattern.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:app-parameter-standardization#1</sub>
- **When a docs step has to record "older sibling docs got X wrong, the correct form is Y", express it as a positive invariant in the new doc ("X uses `apikey`, not `api-key`") with a citation — not as a TODO routed to the next step. Acceptance criteria should then grep both forms (positive AND negative) to enforce the invariant.** — If the new doc just says "see step 2 for corrections", the new doc itself can drift the same way the old ones did. The right pattern is: assert the truth here, citing source code; route the cleanup of *other* docs to step 2. _(e.g. Step 1's acceptance criteria require `grep "advice-service/apikey"` ≥ 1 AND `grep "advice-service/api-key"` = 0 in the new doc — the new doc must be internally consistent before step 2 corrects the other docs.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:app-parameter-standardization#1</sub>

- **UI-feature steps that span web + mobile + a shared component library + ≥1 new AppSync operation are L-complexity minimum, not 'unknown'. A create proposal that emits `files: []` and `complexity: unknown` for such a step is under-budgeting it — the detail will need to enumerate ~15–20 new files across four directories plus 4–6 edits.** — Parallels the existing rule that canonical-contract-artifact steps are M-minimum. UI features have a predictable multi-surface footprint that should drive complexity at create time, so reviewers know what they're approving. _(e.g. Step 9 here landed 20 new files (web pages/modals, mobile screens/sheets, shared cards, 2 Lambdas, 4 test files, README + detail) plus 6 edits (schema, lambda manifest, data resource, backend.ts, web router, mobile tabs layout) from a create row that said `files: []`, `complexity: unknown`.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:vendor-sftp-data-integration#9</sub>
- **When the plan body's prose implies a finer-grained data shape than what an earlier step's detail locked into the schema, the detail for the later step must reconcile the divergence in the 'Why' section *and* park the alternative interpretation in 'Deferred' with the rough cost. Do not silently choose one reading.** — Prose-vs-schema mismatches are landmines: shipping the prose-faithful reading silently invalidates the earlier step; shipping the schema-faithful reading silently underdelivers on the plan. Naming the fork keeps the next implementer (and any reviewer comparing to the plan body) on the same page. _(e.g. Plan body said 'filtered by programs the user is eligible for' but step 5's `VendorEligibility` is keyed on `userID` alone (per-user flag, not per-program). The step-9 detail had to call this out explicitly and defer per-program eligibility as its own model + feed-change story.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:vendor-sftp-data-integration#9</sub>
- **Before drafting a UI-feature step, identify a canonical sibling *feature folder* (web pages + mobile screens + shared components + routes/tabs + tests) and cite it as the template for file layout, naming, and prop shapes. Mirror it exactly — including the test-file count and the shared component split between `packages/frontend/` and `apps/<web|mobile>/`.** — Conventions for where things live in a multi-app monorepo are implicit and discovered via reading other features. Naming the template up front turns the detail from 'invent a structure' into 'mirror this structure', cuts review time, and prevents conventions from drifting feature-by-feature. _(e.g. Step 9 explicitly cited the Mentors feature (`apps/web/src/features/mentors/pages/MentorsPage.tsx`, `apps/mobile/app/tabs/mentors/index.tsx`, `packages/frontend/src/components/mentors/MentorCard.tsx`) as the template, then enumerated the parallel Learning files line-for-line.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:vendor-sftp-data-integration#9</sub>
- **When a later step *replaces* (not extends) a placeholder operation/handler that an earlier step's detail declared, the replacement and the schema-shape rationale must be explicit, with the operation rename + signature change re-justified. Don't paper over the placeholder by leaving the name and pretending to extend it.** — Cross-step amendments are the single biggest source of plan-vs-implementation drift. The earlier step's detail and any reviewer using it as a contract will be wrong if the later step silently changes the signature. _(e.g. Step 5 declared `listVendorProgramsForUser` (no args, returns `VendorProgram[]`, resolver was a `/* AppSync resolver */` comment). Step 9 had to replace it with `searchVendorPrograms({ query, limit, offset })` returning a paginated envelope — the detail justified the rename and noted step 5's was always a placeholder.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:vendor-sftp-data-integration#9</sub>
- **For idempotent state-transition mutations (express-interest, opt-in, accept, RSVP, etc.), the detail should specify a conditional-write pattern (`attribute_not_exists`) plus an `alreadyExpressed`-style boolean discriminator in the response envelope — not a read-then-write check.** — Read-then-write has a race window and forces the UI to handle three result shapes (success, conflict, error). Conditional-write-with-discriminator collapses to two (success-new, success-already) and makes idempotency a server-enforced property, not a client-trusted one. Generalizable beyond DynamoDB — Postgres `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT` is the analog. _(e.g. `vendor-express-interest/handler.ts` issues a `putItem` with `ConditionExpression: attribute_not_exists(userID) AND attribute_not_exists(programId)`; on `ConditionalCheckFailedException` returns `{ success: true, alreadyExpressed: true }` without bumping `rowVersion`.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:vendor-sftp-data-integration#9</sub>

- **Refactor steps that are intended to be behaviorally no-op should include an explicit byte-identical-artifact invariant in acceptance criteria (e.g. `cdk synth` template diff, snapshot diff, build-output diff), not just `lint` + `tsc` + tests.** — Lint and unit tests pass even when a helper accidentally drops a path segment or changes a logical resource name. The whole point of a 'reduce duplication' step is that downstream artifacts must be unchanged — that has to be a checkable acceptance criterion, not an assumption. _(e.g. Step 3 here added `cdk synth` template-diff against `main` as an acceptance criterion because `pnpm lint` + Vitest unit tests on the helpers alone would not catch a regression like `parameterName` flipping from `/app/{env}/assessment-service/api-key` to `/app/assessment-service/api-key`.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:app-parameter-standardization#3</sub>
- **When a step proposes adopting a new helper/utility in N files but the same pattern exists in M >> N call sites elsewhere, the detail must enumerate the un-adopted sites with file:line pointers and route them to a deferred follow-up or a later step.** — Without an explicit out-of-scope list, the next implementer either (a) silently scope-creeps and sweeps everything, breaking the step's complexity budget, or (b) leaves the codebase half-adopted with no audit trail of which call sites still need migration. Detail is the only place that has read the wider surface. _(e.g. Step 3 listed 3 target files but the codebase had ~60 `/app/${instanceName|environment}` occurrences. The detail's 'Out-of-scope adoption' subsection named `amplify/bedrock/resource.ts` (~17 sites), `amplify/resources/auth.ts:88`, `sftp.ts:203-276`, `mentors.ts` and routed them to step 5 — without that, an implementer would have either expanded scope or left a silent gap.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:app-parameter-standardization#3</sub>
- **Steps that introduce a new helper/utility API — even a tiny pure-function one — need the detail to settle API-shape decisions with rationale: signature (variadic vs structured arg), error mode (throw vs accept-and-pass-through), what NOT to include in the first cut.** — Create proposes the helper's name; detail is where the contract lives. If detail doesn't pin signature + error semantics + scope boundaries, the implementer makes those calls ad-hoc and a reviewer can reasonably push back — costing a round trip the detail could have prevented. _(e.g. Step 3's detail explicitly defended variadic `(name, ...segments)` over `(name, subpath: string)`, throw-on-empty over silent-accept, and explicitly excluded both a Secrets Manager variant and an `ssmParameterArn(stack, path)` builder — each with one sentence of rationale. None of this was in the create proposal.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:app-parameter-standardization#3</sub>

- **Cross-platform read-only UI steps (web + mobile + shared) are never `files: []` and never `complexity: unknown` — they're M-minimum and touch at least: a page, a mirror screen, a cross-platform card/component, a shared hook, route registration, and a nav/discovery edit.** — Create proposed `files: []` and `complexity: unknown` for this step, but the legitimate diff spans 7 new files plus 5 existing-file edits across `apps/web/`, `apps/mobile/`, `packages/frontend/`, and the route registry. A pure read view feels small but isn't on a monorepo with cross-platform parity. _(e.g. Step 10 needed `apps/web/src/features/learning/pages/MyProgramsPage.tsx`, `apps/mobile/app/tabs/learning/my-programs.tsx`, `packages/frontend/src/components/learning/ProgramStatusCard.tsx`, `packages/frontend/src/components/learning/ProgramStatusBadge.tsx`, `packages/frontend/src/hooks/use-program-statuses.ts`, plus edits to LearningPage, ProgramDetailPage, `routes/index.tsx`, `_layout.tsx`, and the mobile index.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:vendor-sftp-data-integration#10</sub>
- **Before drafting a UI step that surfaces backend data from a prior step, verify the WRITE path of that data — direct DDB SDK writes (BatchWriteItem, PutItem from a Lambda) bypass AppSync resolvers and therefore bypass `onCreate`/`onUpdate` subscriptions. The detail must then specify focus-refetch + manual refresh, not subscriptions.** — A plan can plausibly say 'real-time updates via subscriptions' without checking whether the producing Lambda goes through AppSync. If it doesn't, the subscription path silently does nothing. This is a class of failure mode where the UI 'works in dev with manual seed' and 'never updates in prod'. _(e.g. Step 5's parser writes `VendorProgramStatus` via direct `BatchWriteItem`, so `client.models.VendorProgramStatus.onCreate().subscribe()` never fires. Step 10's detail explicitly deferred subscriptions and shipped focus-refetch + a manual `refresh()` button as MVP, with a note that the proper fix requires a DDB-stream-driven publisher Lambda.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:vendor-sftp-data-integration#10</sub>
- **When a UI step bridges two backend writes that may not both have completed (e.g. user expressed interest, vendor hasn't echoed back), define the synthetic client-side state explicitly in the hook's TypeScript return type — not buried in implementation prose.** — Synthetic statuses (`PENDING_INTEREST` here) are a real product surface but easy to lose when re-reading the detail later. Putting them in the hook's exported type makes them an API contract a tester or reviewer can grep for, not an implementation detail. _(e.g. Step 10's `ProgramStatusRow.status` union includes `'PENDING_INTEREST'` as a documented client-synthetic value alongside the seven backend enum values; the rule for emitting it (interest row exists, status row does not) is in both the type definition site and the test list.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:vendor-sftp-data-integration#10</sub>
- **For UI steps that depend on a prior step's data model, the detail should cite the exact authorization rule, GSI name, and field declarations by filepath+line from the prior step's detail — not paraphrase them or re-derive them.** — Auth rules and GSI query method names are the load-bearing API the UI consumes. Paraphrasing introduces drift; if the prior step's detail changes shape during review, the citation makes the dependency visible. _(e.g. Step 10 cited `05-…:96` for `allow.ownerDefinedIn('userID').to(['read'])` and `05-…:93-94` for the `statusByUserAndChangedAt` GSI, so a reviewer changing Step 5's auth rule sees Step 10 as a downstream caller.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:vendor-sftp-data-integration#10</sub>
- **Acceptance criteria for read-only UI surfaces must include both the positive case (eligible user sees data) AND the gating case (ineligible user sees the gate), AND a 'data freshness' case that exercises whichever refresh mechanism the step settled on (focus-refetch, manual refresh, pull-to-refresh).** — Read-only views fail in three distinct ways: data missing, gate bypass, stale data. A single 'renders rows' acceptance criterion misses two of them. The freshness case is especially important when subscriptions were deferred — otherwise QA discovers the gap. _(e.g. Step 10's acceptance criteria covered (a) eligible user sees rows, (b) ineligible user hits Step-8's surface, and (c) after expressing interest, the row appears either on focus-refetch OR after clicking 'Refresh' — both must work.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:vendor-sftp-data-integration#10</sub>

- **For migration / replacement steps (X store → Y store, vendor A SDK → vendor B SDK), create-time audit must enumerate every **consumer** of the resource, not just the creator. The proposed `files:` list should list creator + every consumer + every IAM grant site + every operator script that references the old path.** — Migration steps are uniquely allergic to incomplete file lists: missing one consumer leaves a broken Lambda in production. In this run the declared `files:` was 7 files; the detail had to add ~7 'Required addition' files (vendor-scraper.ts/handler, ustrive-client.ts, ustrive-webhook handler, labor-data-service-oauth-manager.ts, labor-data-service-versions-probe handler, bg-agent/labor_data_service_oauth.py, backend.ts) to make the migration complete. Audit heuristic: grep the codebase for the literal resource ID/ARN/env-var pattern and add every match's file to `files:`. _(e.g. Step 5's create proposal omitted `amplify/lambda/utils/ustrive-client.ts` even though that file is the shared SDK wrapper used by all 6 mentor Lambdas — the IAM grants in `mentors.ts` were listed, but the actual `SecretsManagerClient` call site wasn't.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:app-parameter-standardization#5</sub>
- **Migration-step grep must include non-default-language files (`*.py`, `*.sh`, `*.yml`). TypeScript-only audits miss Python consumers and bash provisioning scripts that read the same resource.** — It's easy to glob `**/*.ts` and call it done; the resource being migrated is language-agnostic. In this run, `amplify/bedrock/chat-agent/agent.py` and `amplify/bedrock/bg-agent/labor_data_service_oauth.py` both read the same Secrets Manager entries the TypeScript handlers read, and they would have been missed by a `.ts`-only audit. _(e.g. `agent.py:263-284` reads `/example/app/{instanceName}/advice-service/apikey` via `boto3.client('secretsmanager')`. Easy to miss because the create-time audit defaulted to TypeScript file globs.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:app-parameter-standardization#5</sub>
- **Migration-step details must include an explicit **operator data-migration runbook** (copy live values from source store to target store) as a numbered section, not just code changes. The runbook is the linchpin: deploying the CDK change before the values are copied takes prod down.** — Code-only migration plans imply 'CDK switches; magic copies the values' — operators are left to invent the copy procedure. Standardizing a runbook section in the detail prevents per-environment improvisation and gives reviewers something to validate before approving the deploy. _(e.g. Step 5 §11 specifies a per-env `aws secretsmanager get-secret-value | aws ssm put-parameter` loop and an explicit post-validate `delete-secret --force-delete-without-recovery` step. Without this section the implementer would have done it differently per env.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:app-parameter-standardization#5</sub>
- **When a step proposes deleting a file/construct, the detail must record the grep evidence (search term + result) used to confirm it's dead code, inline in the implementation section. Don't just assert 'unused'; show the proof.** — Reviewers will re-grep anyway; recording the evidence in the detail (a) saves the next reader from re-doing the search, (b) makes the claim falsifiable, (c) catches the case where the author missed a non-default-language call site. _(e.g. Step 5 §5 records: '`grep` for `configureLaborDataServiceAuth` returns only the export line itself and archived-plan references' — this single line lets a reviewer skip the verification step.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:app-parameter-standardization#5</sub>
- **For migration steps with N source items, the detail should produce a per-item **evaluation matrix** (rows = source items, columns = the criteria justifying the migration verdict) as a doc artifact, not just inline prose. The matrix becomes a permanent record in the standards doc for the next time someone asks 'why didn't we use Y for Z?'.** — Future contributors will challenge the migration ('but doesn't Secrets Manager give us rotation?'); having the matrix in a checked-in doc closes the question. Inline prose justifications get forgotten and re-litigated. _(e.g. Step 5 §1 produced a 5-row × 6-column matrix (rotation? cross-account? versioning? shape? consumers? verdict?) and routed it into `docs/secrets-and-parameters.md` Section 7 — the matrix is the artifact, not the migration code.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:app-parameter-standardization#5</sub>
- **Migration steps that span multiple value shapes (JSON-wrapped vs bare scalar) need an explicit per-item shape mapping in the detail. Otherwise the migration looks uniform but isn't, and one consumer will JSON.parse a bare string.** — The default mental model 'all secrets are JSON' is wrong about half the time. Calling out the scalar exception forces the consumer-side code change to be correct on the first try. _(e.g. Step 5 §12 calls out that the uStrive webhook secret is a bare string, not JSON — preserved as-is across the migration. Without that callout, the consumer rewrite in `ustrive-webhook/handler.ts:79-95` would have likely wrapped the value in `JSON.parse(...).webhookSecret`.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:app-parameter-standardization#5</sub>
- **When a step deletes all functionality from a file, prefer **deleting the file** over leaving an empty stub function. Empty stubs invite future contributors to re-fill them with the deprecated pattern.** — An empty `createSecrets(backend, instanceName)` no-op call from `backend.ts` is an invitation: the next person who needs a secret will add a one-liner to the stub. Deleting the file forces the conversation back to the standard. _(e.g. Step 5 §4 decided against leaving an empty `createSecrets` stub, with rationale: 'an empty stub call that future contributors will re-fill the next time they reach for Secrets Manager'.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:app-parameter-standardization#5</sub>
- **For `splittable` migration plans, the detail should recommend a **per-item execution order** with rationale (simplest/lowest-blast-radius first), not just say 'split per-item'. Otherwise the implementer ships the highest-risk vendor first.** — Splittability is the *opportunity*; ordering is the *advice*. Recommending an order in the deferred-items section gives the splitter a default they can override, instead of starting from scratch. _(e.g. Step 5 Deferred recommended: webhook-secret (scalar, simplest) → Advice Service (1 consumer) → Vendor (1 consumer) → uStrive credentials (6 consumers) → LaborDataService (4 consumers + shared OAuth manager). The 1-consumer cases derisk the multi-consumer ones.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:app-parameter-standardization#5</sub>

- **When a prior step's detail explicitly defers a placeholder, stub, or 'muted card' to a later step (e.g. 'Step N+1's deliverable'), the later step's detail must call out the placeholder by file:line and replace it as an explicit edit — not leave a dangling stub.** — Cross-step handshakes are easy to lose. If the later step's detail doesn't list the prior placeholder among its edits, reviewers can't verify the deferral closed, and the placeholder ships to prod as dead UI. _(e.g. Step 10 left a 'muted card placeholder for COMPLETED-state stipend block' at ProgramDetailPage.tsx; Step 11's detail listed that file under 'Existing files (edits only)' with a one-line description of the replacement, citing the 10-…:156 deferral line.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:vendor-sftp-data-integration#11</sub>
- **For UI steps adding a child route under an existing parent route, the detail must check whether the parent currently lives as a single file in a file-based router and budget for converting it to a directory (with the framework's layout file).** — Single-file → directory conversion is invisible to a Glob-only audit and isn't 'a file edit' — it's a structural rename plus a new layout file. Skipping this surfaces as a broken route at smoke-test time, after the rest of the step is already drafted. _(e.g. Step 11 needed `/funding/financial-aid` but `apps/mobile/app/tabs/Funding.tsx` was a single file; the detail had to convert it to `apps/mobile/app/tabs/funding/{_layout,index,financial-aid}.tsx` and note that the parent tab's `Tabs.Screen name="Funding"` registration still resolves to the new directory's `index`.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:vendor-sftp-data-integration#11</sub>
- **UI steps that render numeric data sourced from a vendor feed must specify three things the happy-path spec omits: (a) clamping policy for malformed values, (b) reconciliation when independent fields disagree (which is authoritative + log behavior), and (c) the distinction between 'all-zero values' and 'field absent'.** — Vendor feeds routinely deliver rows where `remaining ≠ allocated − used` or `usedCents > allocatedCents`. Without an explicit policy, the render code either crashes (NaN in a width-% calc), overflows visually, or silently lies. The 'zero vs missing' distinction also drives empty-state copy — a real product state, not a render edge. _(e.g. Step 11's detail spelled out: clamp progress-bar width to `Math.min(100, percentUsed * 100)`; trust vendor's `remainingCents` over the derived value but `console.warn` on disagreement; an all-zero stipend row is kept (signals 'benefit not funded yet') while a row with no stipend object is dropped from the breakdown.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:vendor-sftp-data-integration#11</sub>
- **Eligibility-gated UI features need both route-level gating (the guard wraps the route) and hub-page conditional rendering (the link to the gated page is hidden for ineligible users) — and the original ineligible-user UX should be preserved verbatim, not replaced.** — Route-level gating alone leaves orphan links that 404/redirect when clicked, which feels broken. Hub-page conditional rendering alone leaves the route reachable via deep link. Replacing the ineligible empty state with a new layout is a regression in a separate feature dimension that the step shouldn't be making. _(e.g. Step 11's edit to `apps/web/src/features/funding/pages/FundingPage.tsx` keeps the existing 'Coming soon' copy + icon for ineligible users verbatim, adds an 'Vendor tuition assistance' card link only when `useVendorEligible()` returns true, and the `/funding/financial-aid` route is independently wrapped in Step 8's `VendorEligibleGuard`.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:vendor-sftp-data-integration#11</sub>
- **When a UI step depends on a data hook introduced by the previous step, the detail should reuse the hook unchanged and re-cite the field contract — not propose a new query path 'to save fetches'.** — Two independent reads of the same data drift; keeping all surfaces on one hook means manual-refresh and focus-refetch propagate to every consumer. Per-user row counts in product features are usually bounded, so the 'extra' fetch is negligible. _(e.g. Step 11 explicitly noted that even though the Financial Aid view only needs the status rows (not the catalog join Step 10's hook performs), it reuses `useProgramStatuses(currentUserId)` so that refresh and focus-refetch behavior stays identical between the Learning and Funding surfaces.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:vendor-sftp-data-integration#11</sub>

- **When `plan-tool create` synthesizes a plan, populate every top-level section (Overview, Problem statement, Architecture, Out of scope, Risks, Testing, Acceptance criteria, Deferred) with at least one concrete sentence drawn from the user-provided context — do not emit bare `_(TODO)_` stubs. If a section truly cannot be answered yet, write the specific open question, not a placeholder.** — A plan where every standard section is `_(TODO)_` is unreviewable: it forces a second human pass before `detail` or `review` can produce meaningful signal, and each subsequent review run wastes effort re-flagging the same placeholders instead of finding real issues with the steps. _(e.g. This review emitted 8 redundant MEDIUM body-completeness findings (Overview, Problem statement, Architecture, Out of scope, Risks, Testing, Acceptance criteria, Deferred all `_(TODO)_`), crowding out any step-level findings the review might otherwise have surfaced.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via review:vendor-sftp-data-integration</sub>
- **In `plan-tool review`, when multiple section-level placeholder findings fire for the same plan, consolidate them into a single high-severity finding (e.g. "plan body is unfilled — N of M sections are `_(TODO)_`") rather than one finding per section. Then skip step-quality checks until the body is filled.** — Per-section findings hide the real failure mode (the plan was never written) and bury any genuine step issues under boilerplate. A single rolled-up finding makes the gating problem obvious and the report scannable. Step-quality checks against an empty body waste tokens and produce false negatives. _(e.g. All 8 findings here are the same shape: `## X body is a _(TODO)_ placeholder`. One consolidated finding ("8 of 8 plan-body sections are placeholders — author the body before re-running review") would have been more actionable.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via review:vendor-sftp-data-integration</sub>

- **Every file path mentioned in a detail body must appear in that step's `files:` array (and vice versa). Reconcile both directions before finalizing a detail.** — Files-coherence drift was the dominant finding in this review (40+ MEDIUM items across steps 1, 3, 5). The detail body and the machine-readable manifest are read by different consumers; when they diverge, downstream tooling operates on an incomplete file list while reviewers operate on the prose — the two pictures of the change disagree silently. _(e.g. Step 1's detail cited `amplify/backend.ts`, `amplify/resources/secrets.ts`, `scripts/create-ssm-parameters.sh`, and 9 other paths that weren't in `step.files`; conversely `step.files` listed an entry the detail never mentioned. Author detail and update `files:` as a single atomic edit, not sequential.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via review:app-parameter-standardization</sub>
- **Do not run `plan-tool detail` on a step while the parent plan body still has `_(TODO)_` placeholders in load-bearing sections (Overview, Problem statement, Architecture, Out of scope, Risks, Testing, Deferred). Fill those first.** — Detail authoring grounds its 'Why this matters' / scope / non-goals in the plan body. When the body is empty, detail invents that framing from scratch and the resulting design notes drift from whatever the plan ultimately decides. Cheaper to fill the body once than to reconcile N details against it later. _(e.g. This plan had seven major body sections still as `_(TODO)_` placeholders while details for steps 1, 3, and 5 were already drafted — the details effectively defined the plan's scope rather than executing it.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via review:app-parameter-standardization</sub>
- **The 'looks already done' heuristic firing on a single common token in a docs file is a weak signal — require either (a) a code-side match, or (b) a multi-token title match before treating the step as deferred.** — Single tokens like `real` or a vendor name appearing as a markdown heading in unrelated documentation will trigger the heuristic without indicating any actual implementation. Acting on these wastes review cycles re-confirming nothing happened. _(e.g. Step 2 here was flagged twice — once on `real` matching `### Real` in `docs/backend-guide.md`, once on `LaborDataService` matching `# LaborDataService` in `docs/labor-data-service-runbook.md`. Neither doc heading reflects step-2 work; both are pre-existing prose.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via review:app-parameter-standardization</sub>
- **When a step's only declared file is a sentinel like `(no declared files cited in detail body)`, that's a malformed `files:` entry, not a real path. Treat it as 'files unset' and route the step back to detail authoring rather than logging it as drift.** — Detail rendering currently emits this placeholder when it can't extract real paths, and review then flags it as 'declared but not mentioned'. The signal is noise — the underlying problem is that detail was written without the author committing to a file list, not that a real file is missing. _(e.g. Step 1 here declared `(no declared files cited in detail body)` as its only entry — surfaced as a NIT but really means 'step 1's detail never picked concrete paths, even though the prose cites 12 of them.')_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via review:app-parameter-standardization</sub>

- **Coordination-decision-artifact steps (those resolving named holes in downstream steps — e.g. cadence values, threshold numbers, predicate logic) are M-complexity at minimum, not 'unknown', and produce a structured doc with version + sign-off, per-decision tables, pseudocode for logic, and a controlled-vocabulary cross-link to types declared by an earlier step.** — Like schema-definition steps, these look like 'just docs' but gate specific downstream implementation steps and require structured, citable content. Mis-tagging as 'unknown' under-budgets the work and produces ad-hoc English-only agreements that downstream implementers cannot translate one-for-one. _(e.g. Step 3 here produced cadence-and-eligibility.md with version + sign-off, three cadence tables (each with CDK expression / re-run window / reason), a TypeScript-pseudocode predicate, and a reason-code table mirroring the EligibilityReasonCode union from step 2's vendor.ts.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:vendor-sftp-data-integration#3</sub>
- **When a coordination step exists to unblock named holes downstream, detail-drafting should grep the existing downstream detail files for `TODO(step-N)` markers and 'pending step N' / 'paste the actual X from step N' placeholders — those markers are the literal work items for the coordination step.** — Coordination steps often look like they have unbounded scope. The downstream steps themselves usually already enumerate exactly which decisions they need (as comments, placeholders, or stubs). Treating those markers as the contract makes the step concrete and right-sized instead of drifting into vague 'agree the business logic'. _(e.g. Step 3's two work items came straight from step 4's detail at lines 82 and 97 — a `Schedule.cron(...)` placeholder and a `TODO(step-3)` predicate stub. The acceptance criteria targeted exactly those holes.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:vendor-sftp-data-integration#3</sub>
- **Business-logic predicates captured in a decision artifact should be written as language-flavored pseudocode citing source-line references for every field referenced, not English prose.** — An English predicate ('eligible if active employee enrolled in the program') leaves multiple open questions per clause. Pseudocode forces every clause to name a concrete field, the implementer translates one-for-one, and stale field references show up immediately when a reader follows the citation. _(e.g. Step 3's predicate cited User.status (users.ts:30), User.fyfUserGroup (users.ts:31), and User.externalIDs.yumId (users.ts:15 + update-employee-status/handler.ts:58-69) inline in the pseudocode block.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:vendor-sftp-data-integration#3</sub>
- **Decision precedence (which rule wins when multiple match) is part of the agreement and must be locked in the decision artifact, not deferred to implementer choice or emitted-row shape.** — When multiple predicates can fire on the same input, leaving precedence to the implementer causes the outbound shape to drift (one-row-per-match vs one-row-per-subject) and makes partner-side reporting non-deterministic. The artifact has to pick. _(e.g. Step 3's eligibility predicate documented TACOS_AND_TUITION → FRANCHISE_ENROLLED → EMPLOYEE_ACTIVE precedence in one sentence, so step 4's emit logic is one row per user with exactly one reason code.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:vendor-sftp-data-integration#3</sub>
- **When a downstream step owns wiring (alarms, schedules, threshold-driven resources), split 'decider step' (numbers + cron strings + thresholds in a decision artifact) from 'wirer step' (CDK `Rule`, `Alarm`, `SnsAction`). The acceptance criteria of the decider step should require values that paste verbatim into the wirer step.** — Mixing the two produces decision artifacts with no values ('TBD, see step N') and wirer steps that re-litigate the decision at implementation time. The split also matches how monitoring code is already organized in many codebases (threshold constants separate from alarm constructs). _(e.g. Step 3 fixes the inbound watchdog 'Alarm threshold' as a duration step 6 converts to `Duration.hours(N)`; step 6 owns the `Alarm` + `SnsAction` against the existing `alarmTopic`. Step 3 acceptance requires a populated duration cell.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:vendor-sftp-data-integration#3</sub>

- **Frontend feature-gate steps with `files: []` from create still require a substantial file inventory — typically ≥7 paths (cross-platform context + provider + guard, per-platform wrappers for each app, barrel/index edits, one test file per new module).** — Create treats `files: []` as a coordination signal, but frontend gates routinely fan out across `packages/frontend/`, `apps/web/`, and `apps/mobile/` with mirror-image wrappers. Under-budgeting the file list at create time mis-routes complexity and misleads the detail-time author. _(e.g. Step 8 here declared no files but legitimately landed: `packages/frontend/src/context/VendorEligibilityContext.tsx`, `…/VendorEligibilityProvider.tsx`, `…/components/vendor/VendorEligibleGuard.tsx`, `apps/web/src/shared/context/VendorEligibilityWrapper.tsx`, `apps/mobile/app/contexts/VendorEligibilityWrapper.tsx`, plus three test files and two edits to app entry points.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:vendor-sftp-data-integration#8</sub>
- **When later steps cite a symbol by name (e.g. step N+M imports `<SomeGuard>` or `useSomeHook()` from step N), create should encode that as a `depends_on` from N+M → N — even if there's already a chain through a different step.** — Cross-step symbol citations are the load-bearing prerequisite signal. A symbol that doesn't exist yet but is referenced by downstream code blocks compile. Without the explicit dependency, a scheduler can attempt step N+M before step N lands. _(e.g. Steps 9/10/11 each cited `VendorEligibleGuard` and `useVendorEligible()` as load-bearing imports, but step 8's only `depends_on` was step 5 (the data model). The reverse dependency (steps 9/10/11 → step 8) was implicit.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:vendor-sftp-data-integration#8</sub>
- **When the plan body forbids a behavior present in the closest existing pattern (e.g. "no admin bypass" while the sibling guard supports admin bypass), the detail must include an explicit anti-regression test that mocks the bypass path and asserts denial.** — A future "harmonize the two guards" refactor will silently re-introduce the forbidden behavior unless a test pins it. Documentation alone in the detail body is not enforceable. The test cost is one extra case but the prevention value is large. _(e.g. Step 8's `VendorEligibleGuard` deliberately omits admin bypass while the sibling `AppModeGuard` (`packages/frontend/src/components/app-mode/AppModeGuard.tsx:27-29`) has one. The detail's test plan included `does not bypass for any value of context — even with an admin-like userID`.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:vendor-sftp-data-integration#8</sub>
- **Before drafting a detail that introduces a named symbol, grep the entire repo for that symbol across `packages/**`, `apps/**`, and `docs/implementation-plans/**`. The grep answers three questions in one pass: (1) does it already exist? (2) which sibling patterns should I mirror? (3) which downstream files already import it?** — Doing these as three separate searches duplicates work. The grep-once pattern surfaces downstream callers that inform the API shape (e.g. whether a redirect prop is needed) and reveals plan-doc-only references that haven't shipped yet. _(e.g. Grepping `VendorEligible|useVendorEligible|VendorEligibilityContext|VendorEligibilityProvider` returned zero source matches but multiple plan-doc citations in steps 9/10/11 — confirming step 8 is the originator and revealing the required API shape (fallback + onAccessDenied).)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:vendor-sftp-data-integration#8</sub>
- **When the plan body references files that do not exist on disk (e.g. a sibling pattern claimed to live at path X), the detail must explicitly flag the missing reference and decide whether the current step introduces it or routes around it.** — Silently routing around a missing reference produces a divergent implementation; silently introducing it expands scope. Flagging it in the detail body forces a deliberate choice and warns the next reader that the plan body is out of date. _(e.g. The plan body referenced `apps/mobile/app/contexts/AppModeWrapper.tsx` and `AppModeAdminResolver.tsx` (`docs/implementation-plans/1-vendor-sftp-data-integration.md:136`) but neither file exists; step 8's detail explicitly disregards the citation and documents the mobile parity gap as a separate follow-up.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:vendor-sftp-data-integration#8</sub>

- **When a step's title enumerates the public API surface (e.g. "X dataclass, save/load helpers, hash helpers"), treat that enumeration as the module's contract for the detail — don't expand beyond it unless detail-time discovery surfaces a forcing function, and don't compress below it.** — Title enumerations are a load-bearing scope signal. Expanding them inflates the diff (and the next step's review burden); compressing them leaves callers in later steps blocked. Honoring them keeps complexity ratings accurate. _(e.g. Step 1 title named `Checkpoint dataclass, save/load_if_valid, content-hash helpers`. The detail produced exactly those plus a tiny `discard_checkpoint` (justified by step 2/3 cleanup paths) and called the helper out explicitly rather than smuggling it in.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:resumable-create-detail-execution-context#1</sub>
- **For steps that add a pure-helper module in its own file, the create-time audit should glob for the closest sibling helper module in the same package and surface its path in the step body so the detail can mirror its layout (header docstring, constants, dataclass, path helpers, I/O) without re-discovering the convention.** — Pure-helper modules in a package tend to share an implicit template (constants → dataclass → path helpers → I/O). Without a surfaced sibling, the detail reads several files to reconstruct it and risks drifting. Surfacing the template at create time saves reads and prevents drift. _(e.g. `plan_tool/lessons.py` was the template for `checkpoint.py` — same docstring shape, same `REL` tuple constants, same path-helper layering, same frozen dataclass style. Discovering this mid-draft cost reads that the create proposal could have avoided.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:resumable-create-detail-execution-context#1</sub>
- **For cache / memoization primitives, the load function should collapse every failure mode (missing, malformed, schema-version mismatch, hash mismatch, field-missing / wrong-type) to `None` — never raise. State this contract in the docstring and make each mode an explicit test case.** — Memoization's correctness contract is "a stale or missing cache is indistinguishable from a cold start". A single uncaught exception in the load path turns a cache miss into a hard failure, defeating the point. Enumerating modes prevents quiet regressions. _(e.g. `load_if_valid` has five distinct failure modes; the tests cover each one as a separate function and the docstring lists them. A reviewer can verify the contract in one read.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:resumable-create-detail-execution-context#1</sub>
- **Atomic file writes in Python stdlib require `tempfile.mkstemp(dir=target_parent)` + `os.replace`, not `NamedTemporaryFile`. The latter defaults to `/tmp` and the cross-filesystem rename is not atomic. Plans proposing atomic writes should pin both halves of the pattern in the implementation section.** — This is a stdlib quirk that's easy to get subtly wrong. Plans that just say "atomic write via tempfile" leave the implementer to rediscover the constraint, and a wrong implementation passes tests on the same filesystem but breaks in production layouts (Docker tmpfs, separate disks). _(e.g. The detail explicitly justified `tempfile.mkstemp(dir=path.parent)` with the cross-filesystem caveat. Without that pin, a future maintainer might "simplify" to `NamedTemporaryFile`.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:resumable-create-detail-execution-context#1</sub>
- **When a new module exposes an opaque parameter that callers will populate (e.g. an opaque `name` or `kind` string), the detail's Deferred section must name which downstream step owns choosing the concrete values. Otherwise step 2 / step 3 either over-scopes or re-litigates the design.** — Opaque-on-purpose APIs are a common point of confusion. Without an explicit handoff, the next step's detail either invents a redundant enum here or pre-enumerates strings the current step refused to. _(e.g. `checkpoint_path(name=...)` was left opaque. The Deferred section called out that canonical names (`detail-step-<id>`, `create-initial-steps`, `create-references`) belong to steps 2 and 3, preventing scope creep back into step 1.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:resumable-create-detail-execution-context#1</sub>
- **For frozen-dataclass cache records, an explicit `schema_version: int` field (loaded with a default, compared against a module constant on read) costs nothing now and pre-pays for future on-disk format migrations. Treat it as standard, not over-engineering.** — The alternative — versionless cache files — forces a hard cutover when the shape changes (delete-everything migration or hand-written compat code). A `schema_version` mismatch returning `None` is a one-line addition that lets a version bump invalidate every existing record cleanly. _(e.g. `Checkpoint.schema_version` with `CHECKPOINT_SCHEMA_VERSION = 1` exported publicly so steps 2/3 can future-proof their reads, and a dedicated test covers the mismatch-returns-None path.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:resumable-create-detail-execution-context#1</sub>

- **Don't embed line numbers in step titles or `files` annotations — anchor on function names + section markers (e.g. 'inside the invoke-attempt loop, before the plan-update commit') and offer line numbers only as a parenthetical nav hint.** — Line numbers drift across PRs, refactors, and even across the lifetime of a single plan (later steps edit the same file). When a step title says '~line 717' and by the time detail runs the relevant code is at line 740, the title reads as wrong even though the intent is right. Anchoring on identifiers keeps the title correct as the file evolves. _(e.g. Step 2 title: 'Wire detail.py to checkpoint parsed body between claude success (after _MAX_INVOKE_ATTEMPTS loop, ~line 717) and output_path.write_text (~line 762)' — the `_MAX_INVOKE_ATTEMPTS` and `output_path.write_text` anchors are load-bearing; the line numbers are decoration.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:resumable-create-detail-execution-context#2</sub>
- **For any step that introduces a hash-keyed cache / memoization / resume primitive, the detail must explicitly enumerate near-miss inputs that were considered and rejected from the hash, with one-line reasoning each — not just list the inputs that are included.** — Create's proposal can name the included keys ('id + title + files + depends_on') but it can't reason about what to omit. The omissions are the load-bearing decision — they determine when the cache is too tight (over-invalidating, wasting calls) or too loose (returning stale data). A detail that lists only inclusions invites the next reviewer to re-litigate omissions ad hoc. _(e.g. Step 2's hash deliberately excludes `plan.title/priority/effort`, `step.complexity`, sibling-detail contents, and lessons-file contents. Each exclusion has a one-line reason; otherwise reviewers don't know whether 'we forgot' or 'we decided'.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:resumable-create-detail-execution-context#2</sub>
- **When a step adds a short-circuit path (cache hit, resume, skip) inside a function that drives a progress reporter / counter, the detail must explicitly address how the counter behaves on the short-circuit branch — don't-increment, pre-scan-and-subtract, or report-as-separate-metric — and lock the choice in acceptance criteria.** — Otherwise the detail reads as 'happy path only' and the reporter UX regresses silently. The reporter contract (start_step(i, total, label) → handle.complete(elapsed, bytes)) usually has hidden invariants (heartbeat thread, end-of-run summary) that break if the short-circuit branch calls neither or only one half. _(e.g. Step 2 had to decide whether resumed steps increment `invoke_idx`, decrement `invoke_total`, or appear as labeled-but-uncounted; chose 'don't increment' and explicitly deferred a pre-scan to a later step instead of just adding it.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:resumable-create-detail-execution-context#2</sub>
- **When a step defers a decision to a later step ('I'll keep this inline; step N will extract a factory once there are N call sites'), state both halves of the bargain in writing — what this step does NOT do, and what condition the later step must satisfy to take it on.** — Forward-only deferral notes ('TODO: extract factory') leave the receiving step blind. Bidirectional notes ('step 2 left the timestamp inline; step 3 has 3 call sites and should consolidate') let create's proposal for the receiving step be reviewed against a known obligation, and let the receiving step's detail flag the dependency at the top of its Why section. _(e.g. Step 2 deferred a `Checkpoint.now(...)` factory to step 3, explicitly because step 3 will have multiple call sites and step 2 has one. Step 3's detail will know to budget for the factory rather than re-evaluate inline-vs-factory from scratch.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:resumable-create-detail-execution-context#2</sub>

- **When a create-step description bundles multiple line-number anchors (e.g. "line 584, line 781, line 1328"), de-duplicate them to distinct work units before estimating complexity — anchors often index the same operation through different call-site lenses.** — Three anchors here corresponded to two claude round-trips (inner `invoker(prompt)` calls plus an outer call site). A create heuristic that counts anchors will over-estimate scope and confuse readers about what's being checkpointed. _(e.g. Step 3 listed `line 584, line 781, line 1328`; detail had to clarify these were two distinct round-trips: `propose_steps_from_claude` (caller line 1280, invoker line 584) and `propose_references_from_claude` (caller line 1327, invoker line 781).)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:resumable-create-detail-execution-context#3</sub>
- **When memoizing an LLM call, the cache key must include every input that the prompt template substitutes — not just the obvious user-input field. Read the `_render_*_prompt` (or equivalent) body before deciding the key shape.** — Template-embedded inputs (titles, IDs, env names) silently make the prompt different between runs.
