A cache key that omits them will return stale responses for what is, on the wire, a different prompt. _(e.g. Step 3's references hash had to include `plan_title` (embedded by `_render_references_prompt`) while the steps hash did not (steps prompt doesn't embed it). The create proposal said "keyed by slug + description + output dir hash" — true for steps, incomplete for references.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:resumable-create-detail-execution-context#3</sub>
- **When memoizing LLM output, cache the raw response string and re-parse on every hit; do not cache the parsed structure unless the parser is guaranteed stable across runs.** — Caching parsed results silently returns stale data when validation tightens between runs. Caching raw bytes makes a tightened parser collapse to the normal "unparseable" fail-path, surfacing the issue instead of hiding it. Re-parse cost is almost always negligible compared to a claude round-trip. _(e.g. Step 3 caches the raw `invoker(prompt)` string and re-runs `_parse_steps_json` / `_parse_references_json` on every cache hit; step 2 made a defensible opposite choice for detail.py because its parsed body is followed by an independent validation layer. The asymmetry is worth calling out in both details.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:resumable-create-detail-execution-context#3</sub>
- **The "safe to discard" line for checkpoints is the step that produces the on-disk-and-validated artifact (e.g. a post-write reparse) — not the last call in the orchestrator. Place discard between the durable validation and any subsequent housekeeping (advisory checks, renames, telemetry).** — Failures in housekeeping after the durable line shouldn't keep checkpoints alive; failures before it must. Drawing the line precisely makes the failure-mode matrix small and reasoning-friendly. _(e.g. In step 3 the discard loop runs *after* `parse_plan` succeeds at line 1380 (durable) but *before* `advise_path_issues` (line 1393) and `rename_plan_details_to_slug` (line 1402).)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:resumable-create-detail-execution-context#3</sub>
- **When step N mirrors step N-1's contract on a parallel surface (same primitive, same load-invoke-save shape, same constants), the detail should state the parallel explicitly and call out the deltas — not silently diverge.** — A reviewer reading two adjacent steps benefits enormously from "this is step 2's pattern applied to two more call sites" framing. It also forces the author to justify deltas (here: plan_title in one hash but not the other, raw-vs-parsed cache choice). _(e.g. Step 3's detail explicitly says "mirrors step 2's contract" against `propose_steps` / `propose_references` and identifies each delta from step 2's `propose_detail_step` wiring.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:resumable-create-detail-execution-context#3</sub>

- **When a step's `files:` lists source modules that have test siblings (e.g. `foo.py` next to `tests/test_foo.py`), create should either list the test files explicitly or detail should treat them as implicitly in-scope and surface them in its 'Files' section.** — A 3-file step regularly becomes a 6-file diff once tests are written; readers and reviewers under-budget review effort when the test files aren't named upfront. Detail-drafting also wastes reads re-discovering the test layout. _(e.g. Step 4 listed `cli.py`, `detail.py`, `create.py` (3 files) but required 11 new tests spread across `tests/test_cli.py`, `tests/test_detail.py`, `tests/test_create.py` — a 2x undercount of the touched-file set, missed by create's audit.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:resumable-create-detail-execution-context#4</sub>
- **Steps that introduce a new user-facing CLI flag are M-complexity minimum, not L, even when each individual change looks small. A flag implies help text, parser-level wiring, two-state test coverage, defensive `getattr` at every call site, and propagation through downstream orchestrator signatures — these aggregate quickly.** — create's heuristic tends to see 'add one argparse argument' as trivial. In practice the diff spans parser + 1-2 cmd handlers + 1-2 orchestrators + 5+ tests, which is M-shaped review effort. _(e.g. Step 4 was rated L; the resulting detail spans 3 source files, 11 new tests, a new signature parameter on `generate_detail`, a new helper `_confirm_resume`, and CLI module-docstring updates.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:resumable-create-detail-execution-context#4</sub>
- **When a step adds an interactive prompt, the detail must explicitly specify the non-TTY behavior (CI / piped stdin / `--yes`) as a first-class case in both the implementation section and the acceptance criteria. 'Prompt the user' without this leaves a deadlock risk.** — Calls to `input()` on a closed stdin deadlock or raise `EOFError`; either is a CI-only failure that won't surface in local development. Pinning the non-TTY rule in acceptance criteria forces test coverage. _(e.g. Step 4's `_confirm_resume` short-circuits to 'auto-accept' on `not sys.stdin.isatty()`, and the test plan includes `test_resume_prompt_skipped_when_not_tty`. Without this rule the prompt would silently break automated `plan-tool detail` runs.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:resumable-create-detail-execution-context#4</sub>
- **When step N depends on step M's contract (added parameter, new dir, new helper), N's detail must inline-cite M's exposed surface (function signature line, dir path, key name) rather than assume the reader paged back.** — Detail files are read serially by implementers; flipping back to step M's detail mid-implementation is friction. Inline citation also catches drift — if M's contract changed and N's detail wasn't updated, the mismatch becomes obvious at review time. _(e.g. Step 4's 'Why' section restates that step 2 added `resume: bool = True` to `generate_detail` at `detail.py:598-609` and step 3 did the same for `create_plan` at `create.py:1173-1186`. Without these inline cites a reader would have to grep both files.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:resumable-create-detail-execution-context#4</sub>
- **Before proposing a new function parameter for a user-input callback (prompt_fn, input_fn, etc.), grep for existing interactive-prompt helpers in the same module/codebase. Reusing an existing parameter is preferable to adding a new one.** — Codebases often have an established input-callback convention (e.g. `input_fn` threaded through orchestrators). Adding a parallel parameter with a different name forks the convention and makes future maintenance harder. _(e.g. Step 4 reused `input_fn` in `create.py` (already a parameter at `create.py:1182`) and only added `prompt_fn` to `detail.py` because no equivalent existed there. A less careful detail would have introduced `prompt_fn` to both, fragmenting the convention.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:resumable-create-detail-execution-context#4</sub>

- **Test-coverage steps for a cache/idempotency integration should enumerate the canonical shape explicitly: (a) cache-hit zero-invoke, (b) one cache-miss test per keyed input field, (c) a negative test on a non-keyed field, (d) schema-version mismatch, (e) post-success discard. The non-keyed negative is what pins the design intent rather than the current implementation.** — Cache regressions are silent by construction — a hash that drops a field still produces valid checkpoints, just wrong ones. Only structural per-field coverage catches it. The negative test (proving plan-level fields stay out of the per-step hash, in this run) is the only thing that catches future drift in the *other* direction (someone widening the hash and breaking valid caches). _(e.g. Step 5's TestGenerateDetailResume enumerates cache_miss_on_title_change / on_files_change / on_depends_on_change plus a plan_title_change_still_cache_hits negative test, mirroring the exact keying decision in step 2's `_detail_step_input_hash`.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:resumable-create-detail-execution-context#5</sub>
- **Acceptance criteria for test-suite steps should pair each load-bearing test with the specific code regression it pins: "test X fails if helper Y drops field Z" or "test X fails if call C moves outside loop L". This converts the test list from "here are tests" to "here are guarantees".** — Makes the review step mechanical — a reviewer can check the test asserts against the named regression rather than judging coverage adequacy. Prevents the failure mode where tests pass but the design contract was already broken at draft time. _(e.g. Step 5's acceptance criteria include lines like "test_cache_miss_on_title_change_invokes fails if step 2's `_detail_step_input_hash` drops `step.title` from its input list" and "test_resumed_step_omits_invoke_counter fails if step 2's `invoke_targets` filter regresses to include resumed steps".)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:resumable-create-detail-execution-context#5</sub>
- **When a test-coverage step depends on private (underscore-prefixed) helpers added by earlier steps, the detail must explicitly decide whether to import them or re-derive the logic in tests — and justify the choice once. Don't leave it to the implementer.** — The default ask ("don't import private symbols") would force re-deriving the hash inputs by hand in every test, duplicating the keying contract and making future hash-composition refactors silently leave tests passing on the wrong hash. The cost is a small coupling penalty; the benefit is "the hash function the test seeds with IS the hash function prod uses to look up." Make the call in the detail or it'll get re-litigated in code review. _(e.g. Step 5's "Design note on hash-helper imports" explicitly imports `_detail_step_input_hash`, `_initial_steps_input_hash`, `_references_input_hash` from sibling modules and justifies the decision against the re-derive alternative, citing the existing `_plan_slug` import precedent.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:resumable-create-detail-execution-context#5</sub>
- **CLI-flag propagation deserves its own tiny test class that mocks the orchestrator function reference on the CLI module (`monkeypatch.setattr(cli_module, "func", patched)`) and asserts the flag-present + flag-absent kwarg values as parallel methods. Don't bury flag tests inside end-to-end runs.** — End-to-end CLI tests are slow and conflate flag plumbing with orchestrator behavior. A two-method class (`test_flag_propagates` + `test_default_when_absent`) pins the smallest checkable unit and survives orchestrator refactors that change the integration surface. The flag-absent case (default) is as important as flag-present — it catches a regression where the default flips. _(e.g. Step 5 adds `TestCmdDetailNoResume` and `TestCmdCreateNoResume` with paired `test_cli_no_resume_flag_*` + `test_cli_without_flag_resumes_*` methods, mirroring the existing `test_cli_force_overwrites` precedent at `test_detail.py:1025-1062`.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:resumable-create-detail-execution-context#5</sub>
- **When the detail will introduce a new test fixture / helper, anchor it to the line range of the closest existing analogous helper rather than dumping it at the top of the file. Cite both the new helper's intended location and the sibling it sits next to.** — Test files grow organically — new helpers belong adjacent to siblings doing related work, not in a "helpers" stack at the top. Anchoring keeps diffs small, makes the test file's organization stay coherent, and lets a reviewer find the new helper by reading the file linearly. "Add helper near `_detail_path_for` at `test_detail.py:667-668`" is reviewable; "add helper somewhere in test_detail.py" is not. _(e.g. Step 5 places `_seed_detail_checkpoint` immediately after `_detail_path_for` at `test_detail.py:667-668`, and `_seed_initial_steps_checkpoint` / `_seed_references_checkpoint` after `_single_step_answers` at `test_create.py:62` — each sitting next to the helper it shares responsibilities with.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:resumable-create-detail-execution-context#5</sub>
- **For an integration test step that consumes APIs added by 2+ earlier steps, the detail's "Why" should explicitly list each upstream contract the test pins (one bullet per upstream step) before describing the tests themselves.** — Makes the test step a checkpoint for upstream design contracts: if step 2 ships without `discard_checkpoint` in the right place, the test step's discard test fails — so the test step is also the integration-correctness gate for steps 2/3/4. Reviewers need this explicit so they don't approve test coverage that's actually testing the wrong upstream surface. _(e.g. Step 5's Why section names three concrete upstream regressions — silent zero-invoke cache hit (step 2), cache-miss-on-input-hash semantics (steps 2 + 3), three-way interaction between resume / skip / `--force` (step 2 + existing `force=True` path) — before any test enumeration.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-13 via detail:resumable-create-detail-execution-context#5</sub>

- **When a step introduces a primitive module that several later steps will consume, the detail must explicitly enumerate the higher-order helpers that do NOT belong in this module and where they will land — one bullet each under Deferred, with the why.** — The natural temptation when writing a primitive is to also pre-bake the loop / driver that will use it. Resisting that keeps the dependency graph a tree; without an explicit 'NOT here, lands in step X' list, the next implementer or reviewer will either add the driver here or re-litigate scope. _(e.g. Step 1's Deferred section called out 'no run_iteration_loop(invoker, review_fn, state) driver — each caller's per-iteration body shape differs', routing it to steps 2/3/4. Without that, a reasonable reading of 'pure-logic core' would have included a generic driver.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:iterative-correctness-completeness-passes#1</sub>
- **For stop-predicate, ratio, or threshold logic in a primitive, the detail must call out which denominator (or which side of a comparison) is load-bearing and why — bury this and a reviewer will mis-derive the inverse rule.** — |overlap| / |current| vs |overlap| / |previous| answer different questions ('is claude stuck on the same complaints' vs 'how much did claude resolve'). Both are defensible; the wrong one silently breaks the stop semantics. A one-paragraph justification at decision time is cheaper than a post-hoc bug. _(e.g. is_marginal divides by |current| not |previous| — detail spelled out 'claude resolved 9/10 and introduced 1 new finding' as the canonical case that distinguishes the two.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:iterative-correctness-completeness-passes#1</sub>
- **When a function's caller will log + branch on the outcome, prefer (bool, stable_reason_string) over an enum return. Document the reason set as a closed list in the docstring and have the acceptance criteria assert exactly that set.** — Reason strings are grep-friendly in CI logs and trivially `assert ==`-able from tests without importing enum members; the price (no exhaustiveness check) is acceptable for small reason sets. An enum forces every test file to import the type and reads less well in log scrubs. _(e.g. should_continue returns one of six strings ("disabled", "max_iterations", "deadline_exceeded", "converged", "marginal", "continue") — acceptance criteria pins the exact set.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:iterative-correctness-completeness-passes#1</sub>
- **When a step creates a new module that mirrors an existing primitive's shape, the detail should cite the precedent module by `file:line` for the header docstring, import grouping, and validation pattern — not just describe the shape in prose.** — Prose like 'follow checkpoint.py's pattern' is ambiguous (which pattern? all of it?). A `file:line` citation that points at the docstring or the `__post_init__` block makes the mirror mechanical and forecloses style debates at implementation time. _(e.g. Detail anchored convergence.py's header to checkpoint.py:1-46 ('no claude shell-out, no CLI surface, no first-party plan_tool.* imports') and IterativeBudget's frozen+__post_init__ validation to Checkpoint at checkpoint.py:65-78.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:iterative-correctness-completeness-passes#1</sub>
- **For steps adding new tests under an established test directory, the detail should cite a sibling test file as the structural template (no-class vs class wrappers, docstring shape, section-comment style) rather than re-deriving conventions.** — Test-file conventions are usually implicit (only sibling examples show them) and diverging produces lint-clean but stylistically off code that reviewers fixate on. Citing the template forecloses that. _(e.g. Detail anchored test_convergence.py's shape to test_checkpoint.py:1-37 — top-of-file pact docstring, '# ---------- <name> ----------' section comments, pytest functions without class wrappers.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:iterative-correctness-completeness-passes#1</sub>

- **When a step extends an input hash that already keys an on-disk cache (checkpoint hash, content-addressable store, etc.), the detail must explicitly choose between (a) invalidating existing entries or (b) preserving byte-equality for the default/round-zero case via conditional key inclusion — and the choice belongs in acceptance criteria, not buried in implementation prose.** — Unconditionally adding a new field to the hashed dict invalidates every checkpoint on disk the first time the code lands, breaking resume semantics for users mid-flow. The conditional-include pattern is non-obvious and easy to regress without a pinned-literal hash test. _(e.g. Step 2 here extended `_initial_steps_input_hash` with `iteration: int = 0`; the detail had to specify 'only add `"iteration"` to the payload when `iteration > 0`' and add a test that pins the round-0 hex digest to detect regressions.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:iterative-correctness-completeness-passes#2</sub>
- **When a step adds a loop that calls a previously-once-per-run function N+1 times, the detail must audit the function for filename / artifact collisions and extend any per-name discard / cleanup sweeps in the same step.** — Per-iteration checkpoint or log files silently overwrite each other if the naming scheme is single-shot, and per-name discard tuples leak the new files if not generalized to a glob. Both are invisible from the orchestrator-level diff alone. _(e.g. The post-write loop here had to suffix `create-initial-steps.json` to `create-initial-steps-iter{N}.json` AND generalize the two-element discard tuple at `create.py:1583-1592` to a glob — neither was implied by the original `files:` list.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:iterative-correctness-completeness-passes#2</sub>
- **For steps proposed as 'wire an orchestrator around existing primitives', expand the create-time `files:` list to include the modules holding those primitives (read-only) — not as edit targets, but so the detail-time audit captures their cycle / lazy-import / API-shape constraints.** — The orchestrator's edits are mechanical; the load-bearing decisions (lazy imports, call shape, return-type assumptions) come from the called modules. Listing only the orchestrator under-budgets the detail audit. _(e.g. Step 2's `files:` was `cli.py, create.py, test_create.py`, but the detail had to Read `launcher.py:944` (apply_finding shape), `review.py:2137-2161` (collect_deterministic_findings), and `convergence.py` (step 1's dataclass contracts) to write correctly.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:iterative-correctness-completeness-passes#2</sub>
- **Opt-in feature-flag steps should explicitly add a 'pre-existing call sites still pass with default kwargs' assertion to acceptance criteria — and a test that verifies the disabled path makes zero extra external calls (subprocess, network, LLM).** — A 'disabled by default' loop can still alter call counts, log output, or checkpoint state in ways that break downstream tests. Pinning the call count is the cheapest regression detector. _(e.g. `test_create_plan_with_disabled_budget_is_no_op` asserts the claude invoker is called exactly twice (initial-steps + references) — matching the pre-step-2 count — proving the loop is truly a no-op.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:iterative-correctness-completeness-passes#2</sub>
- **When a step's title bundles three concerns ('wire loop + extend hash key + gate on flag'), the detail should produce three explicit acceptance criteria — one per concern — rather than rolling them into a single 'loop works' assertion.** — Bundled criteria let a partial implementation pass review (e.g., loop wired but hash not extended → cache replays previous iteration). Separate criteria force each sub-concern to be testable in isolation. _(e.g. Step 2 produced separate criteria for (a) `IterativeBudget` import / kwarg threading, (b) round-0 hash byte-equality, (c) round-N hash divergence, (d) discard sweep cleanliness — instead of one 'loop converges' check.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:iterative-correctness-completeness-passes#2</sub>

- **When a per-step iteration loop's body uses an existing in-place editor (e.g. one that edits the artifact file directly), the loop does NOT need to re-render the prompt or re-invoke the proposer between rounds — iteration N+1's review reads N's edit straight from disk. Detail bodies should explicitly contrast this against sidecar-style loops (where the proposer's output is JSON applied separately) so a reader sees why this loop is cheaper.** — Without this distinction, future iteration-loop steps will over-engineer by adding redundant prompt re-renders and per-iteration checkpoints, copying the costlier pattern from the first iteration-loop step they happen to read. _(e.g. Step 3 here loops `collect_findings → apply_finding → collect_findings` with no `render_detail_prompt` call inside the loop; step 2's cmd_create loop re-invokes `propose_steps_from_claude` each round because the steps JSON is a sidecar.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:iterative-correctness-completeness-passes#3</sub>
- **When introducing a new claude-driven review surface, count and inspect existing review prompts before drafting; the new template must reuse the existing findings/output contract (regex + parser) rather than invent its own, and the detail body should cite the canonical existing template by path and length.** — Inventing a new output format forces a new parser, a new lift-into-recommendations path, and a new test surface — none of which add value. Citing the sibling template's line count also gives reviewers a concrete cap on prompt bloat. _(e.g. Step 3's `prompts/detail-quality.md` mirrors `prompts/check.md` (43 lines) and emits the same `- CRITICAL/HIGH/MEDIUM/NIT:` bullets + `_(none)_` marker so `_FINDING_BULLET_RE` + `_claude_recommendations` lift findings with zero new code.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:iterative-correctness-completeness-passes#3</sub>
- **When adding a new keyword argument to a function with many existing callers, default it to a no-op sentinel (`IterativeBudget(enabled=False)`, `extract_lessons=True`, etc.) and add an acceptance criterion asserting that every existing test in the affected test file passes unchanged. The disabled-default kwarg should be exercised in tests by a `…_skips_…` test that asserts the loop's side-effect functions are called zero times.** — Wiring a new feature without a no-op default forces a sweep across dozens of callers and brittle test updates; a `…_skips_…` test proves the loop is truly inert rather than "probably inert". _(e.g. Step 3's `budget: IterativeBudget = IterativeBudget(enabled=False)` kwarg + `test_disabled_budget_skips_iteration_loop` (fake `apply_finding` raises if called).)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:iterative-correctness-completeness-passes#3</sub>
- **Every lazy import inside a function body must be motivated by a documented load-order cycle, and the detail body should name the cycle (`A → B → C → A`). Without that, future readers can't tell which lazy imports are load-bearing vs decorative and may hoist them "to clean up".** — Lazy imports look like clutter and are easy targets for refactors that re-introduce circular import errors at startup time — long after the original PR has shipped. _(e.g. Step 3 lazy-imports `apply_finding` from `launcher` and `_claude_recommendations` from `review` inside the loop body; the docstring cites the `launcher → review → detail` cycle so the next refactor leaves them alone.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:iterative-correctness-completeness-passes#3</sub>
- **Per-step iteration loops should explicitly decide whether intermediate rounds need durable checkpoints, and the detail body should state the decision + rationale ("the on-disk artifact is itself the resume point, no per-iteration JSON needed") so a later reviewer doesn't think checkpointing was forgotten.** — When an earlier sibling step in the same plan DOES add per-iteration checkpoints (because its artifact is a sidecar JSON), a missing checkpoint in a later step looks like an omission rather than a deliberate divergence. _(e.g. Step 3 explicitly says no `create-initial-steps-iter1.json`-style files are produced — the detail markdown on disk is the resume point — because step 2 in the same plan did add iteration-keyed checkpoints for its sidecar JSON.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:iterative-correctness-completeness-passes#3</sub>
- **When a step introduces or relies on content-addressed IDs (full-body hashes of generated text), flag in the Deferred section that small wording changes across iterations produce ID drift, which makes convergence look noisier than it is. The safety net should be named (e.g. an overlap/marginal predicate), and the better-key proposal should be deferred to a follow-up plan rather than scope-crept into this step.** — Content-addressed IDs are the cleanest first cut but always end up needing a normalized key once iteration-loop telemetry surfaces noise. Flagging it upfront in Deferred prevents reviewers from rejecting the step as "incomplete" and prevents future engineers from re-discovering the same noise problem. _(e.g. Step 3's Deferred entry on `_make_rec_id(None, "claude", body)` ID drift, pointing at the `marginal` predicate as the safety net and naming `(severity, normalized_title_first_n_chars)` as a candidate replacement.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:iterative-correctness-completeness-passes#3</sub>

- **When a step title carries a parenthetical 'X lives in Y, not Z' correction, treat it as a signal that create's codebase audit mis-located the function — verify the test file path independently and re-check the step's `files` list for other stale guesses before drafting.** — The correction is load-bearing because it overrides the heuristic's default file mapping; if one location was wrong, sibling locations (test files, related modules) likely weren't audited either. Detail-drafting that trusts the rest of `files:` will cite missing paths. _(e.g. Step 4's title said 'cmd_run lives in cli.py, not run.py'. The `files` field correctly listed `cli.py` and `test_cli.py`, but detail still had to grep to confirm `tests/test_review_walk.py` held the reusable `_basic_plan`/`_force_tty` helpers — a file create never proposed.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:iterative-correctness-completeness-passes#4</sub>
- **For 'wire it now, enable it in step N+1' patterns, extract a one-line factory at the call site (e.g. `_build_X(args)`) even when the body is `return X(enabled=False)`. Tests need a patch surface today, and the next step ships a body change instead of a call-site rewrite.** — Inline-constructed flag values can't be patched without monkeypatching the constructor globally, which leaks across tests. A named factory gives both tests and the follow-up step a single, stable hook. _(e.g. Step 4 introduced `_build_run_budget(args)` returning `IterativeBudget(enabled=False)` so tests could `monkeypatch.setattr(cli_module, '_build_run_budget', ...)`; step 5 then only rewrites that function's body to read from argparse.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:iterative-correctness-completeness-passes#4</sub>
- **Stateful iteration wrappers must seed iteration-0 state from the caller's pre-loop work before entering the loop; otherwise delta-based stop predicates (marginal stagnation, set convergence) compare iteration 1 against an empty baseline and misfire.** — Convergence primitives typically treat 'no previous state' as a special case (often non-marginal by definition), so iteration 1 silently fails to detect a same-finding repeat against iteration 0. The seeding call is invisible from the primitive's API surface. _(e.g. Step 4's loop calls `record_iteration(conv_state, [r.id for r in initial_recs])` before `while True` so iteration 1's `is_marginal` check measures overlap against iteration 0's recs rather than against an empty set.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:iterative-correctness-completeness-passes#4</sub>
- **When a primitive has multiple sibling callers across one plan, each caller's detail should cross-reference the prior caller's detail file (import block lines, call-site shape, default-disabled construction) — not just the primitive's API doc.** — Wiring details (lazy-import vs top-of-file, factory extraction pattern, where the budget is constructed) get refined at the first call site and should propagate verbatim; re-deriving from the API alone causes drift across callers and review churn. _(e.g. Step 4 explicitly cited step 2's `create.py` wiring lines for the import shape (`from plan_tool.convergence import ConvergenceState, IterativeBudget, record_iteration, should_continue`) and noted parity with step 2's `IterativeBudget(enabled=False)` call-site default.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:iterative-correctness-completeness-passes#4</sub>

- **For 'add flag X to N subparsers' steps, the detail should first grep for an existing `add_help=False` parent ArgumentParser; if one is present and the new flag set applies to multiple subparsers, propose a sibling parent rather than per-subparser inline definitions.** — Three separate `add_argument` blocks for the same flag inevitably drift in help text, defaults, and metavar. The parent-parser pattern is usually already established in any non-trivial CLI; missing it leads to a noisier diff and a drift-prone surface. _(e.g. Step 5's detail introduced `iterate_parent` mirroring the existing `yes_parent` at `cli.py:2636-2646` and attached it via `parents=[yes_parent, iterate_parent]` to exactly the three target subparsers.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:iterative-correctness-completeness-passes#5</sub>
- **When a flag is consumed by N handlers, detail should specify a single shared resolver/validator function rather than per-handler inline validation — even if create's file list only names the handler file.** — Per-handler validation drifts on error wording, negative-value rules, and exit codes. Centralization is the same anti-drift argument convergence/refactor steps usually cite for the module under change; it applies one level up to the CLI surface too. _(e.g. `_resolve_iterative_budget(args) -> IterativeBudget` was added once and called from `cmd_create` / `cmd_detail` / `cmd_run`, instead of three near-duplicate `if max < 1: error` blocks.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:iterative-correctness-completeness-passes#5</sub>
- **If a new CLI flag applies meaningfully to only some modes/branches within a handler, detail should explicitly choose between (a) argparse error, (b) soft stderr warning + continue, or (c) silent ignore — and default to the soft warning unless the team has a stated reason otherwise.** — Scripts often tack a flag onto every invocation uniformly; argparse-rejecting it breaks those scripts, silent ignore hides bugs. A one-line stderr warning is the right middle ground and needs to be stated explicitly so the next reviewer doesn't second-guess it. _(e.g. `plan-tool run --mode run --iterate` doesn't hit the reconcile loop, so detail emits a stderr warning ('--iterate only applies to --mode reconcile') and continues, rather than erroring.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:iterative-correctness-completeness-passes#5</sub>
- **Handlers in the same CLI often have distinct error-exit conventions (rc=1 vs rc=2). When detail introduces a shared validator that raises an existing exception type, it must explicitly map the catch to each handler's existing rc, not assume one rc covers all.** — Reusing a single exception class is fine and reduces sprawl, but mismatched exit codes silently change script behavior (CI gates often distinguish rc=1 from rc=2). Spelling out the per-handler mapping in the detail keeps a reviewer from needing to re-derive it. _(e.g. `CreateError` from `_resolve_iterative_budget` is caught and exits rc=1 in `cmd_create` (matching its existing `CreateError` handler) but rc=2 in `cmd_detail` and `cmd_run` (matching their `DetailError` exit convention).)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:iterative-correctness-completeness-passes#5</sub>
- **Steps that 'wire' a value through pre-existing kwargs are lower-complexity than their file list suggests — actual change per call site is typically one line — but detail should still budget for module-docstring touchups in each named file because create's file list often includes the orchestrator file solely for the docstring update.** — Create rates complexity on file count and signature changes; 'docstring touch + kwarg pass-through' files inflate the apparent surface. Naming this explicitly in detail prevents reviewers from expecting deeper edits in those files. _(e.g. `create.py` and `detail.py` appeared in the step's `files:` list but the actual edit was a one-line docstring sentence in each — the signature was already added by steps 2–3.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:iterative-correctness-completeness-passes#5</sub>

- **For 'tests are the work' steps, the Tests section is redundant with Implementation — call this out explicitly and either fold Tests into Implementation or use Tests purely as a flat enumeration with regression-purpose-per-test, not a re-statement of the bodies.** — I duplicated the test enumeration across Implementation and Tests, which made the detail longer without adding information and let an arithmetic slip ('twenty tests total' vs 4+4+10=18) survive the draft. _(e.g. Step 6 here had ~20 tests; the Implementation section already named and described each, then the Tests section re-listed all of them by file. A single source of truth keeps the count honest.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:iterative-correctness-completeness-passes#6</sub>
- **Cross-cutting test steps are not 'more per-step tests' — they exercise user-visible contract (rc codes, byte-equality between CLI flag paths, flag→kwarg propagation), not subcommand internals. When per-step tests already cover internals, the cross-cutting step's value is the contract surface, and the detail should frame each test as 'pins contract X across all N subcommands'.** — Without that framing, the cross-cutting class drifts into duplicating per-step internal coverage and a reviewer can't tell why it exists separately. _(e.g. Step 6's tests like `test_no_iterate_byte_parity_create/detail/run` pin a contract (omitted-flag === explicit --no-iterate, byte-for-byte) that NO per-step test would catch, because each per-step class only tests one subcommand.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:iterative-correctness-completeness-passes#6</sub>
- **When a step's title enumerates 6+ comma-joined scenarios, that's a smell that the step's orientation (the why-it's-one-step) is missing from the title. Create proposals with such titles should be re-titled at detail time to name the unifying concept, even if the scenarios are then enumerated in the body.** — The enumeration-as-title pattern hides whether the step is 'one cross-cutting class per file' or 'six unrelated tests bundled to save a slot'. Reviewers can't size it without reading the body. _(e.g. Step 6's title — 'Cross-cutting tests: cache-hit zero-extra-claude-calls, budget-exhaustion non-zero exit, marginal-stop semantics, --no-iterate parity, closed-stdin auto-accept, --max-iterations 0 equivalence' — should have been 'Cross-cutting contract tests for the --iterate surface' with the six scenarios listed inside.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:iterative-correctness-completeness-passes#6</sub>
- **Test-only steps must list as `depends_on` every prior step whose API the tests reference, including foundation/API-definition steps even when create's heuristic propagates dependencies transitively from leaf consumers. A step that imports `IterativeBudget` and `ConvergenceState` depends on step 1, full stop.** — Create omitted step 1 from `depends_on` because steps 2/3/4/5 already depended on it. But a test step that names `IterativeBudget(...)` in twenty assertions has a direct API contract with step 1 — if step 1's field names change, the test step breaks, not the leaf steps. Transitive isn't enough for tests. _(e.g. Step 6's depends_on was `2, 3, 4, 5` but every test references `IterativeBudget` and `ConvergenceState.stop_reason` from step 1's `convergence.py`. Adding `1` to depends_on would have made the ordering constraint explicit.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:iterative-correctness-completeness-passes#6</sub>
- **Before drafting a test-only step's detail, glob the target test files for module-scope helpers (`_Recording*`, `_queue_*`, `_fresh_*`, `_*_path_for`, `_GOOD_*`, `_*_iso`) and enumerate them in the Files section as 'reusable helpers to import/wrap, not re-roll'. Re-rolling is the default failure mode and bloats tests significantly.** — I spent reads discovering `_RecordingReporter`, `_queue_input`, `_basic_metadata_answers`, `_now_iso`, `_fresh_now`, `_GOOD_BODY`, `_write_orchestrator_plan`, `_ckpt_path_for`, `_find_subparser` mid-draft. The create proposal listed only the three target files; the helpers were the load-bearing context. _(e.g. Step 6's detail had to add 'reuse the existing _RecordingReporter, _queue_input, _basic_metadata_answers, _now_iso helpers' as an inline note for each of three files. A Files section block enumerating these per file would have been the right shape.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:iterative-correctness-completeness-passes#6</sub>
- **Byte-equality tests between two CLI invocation paths must use `Path.read_bytes() == Path.read_bytes()`, not `.read_text()`, and the detail should call out the choice explicitly with rationale. Whitespace-tolerant equality silently allows trailing-newline or line-ending regressions.** — The contract is byte-for-byte — `--no-iterate` and omitting `--iterate` produce identical bytes — and `.read_text()` would mask a regression that introduces a trailing newline difference. _(e.g. Step 6 specified `read_bytes()` for `test_no_iterate_byte_parity_*` in an acceptance criterion. That criterion is what makes the test load-bearing; without it, future maintainers would relax to `read_text()` and the contract erodes.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:iterative-correctness-completeness-passes#6</sub>
- **Boundary tests (e.g. `--max-iterations 0`, empty string input, negative timeout) should flag in their docstring which arm of a contract choice they pin (rejection-with-error vs degenerate-but-valid), so a future implementer changing arms gets a flipped test, not a passing test against the wrong contract.** — Without the explicit contract-arm flag, a test that currently asserts 'rc=0 with disabled-loop semantics' could be silently rewritten to 'rc=2 with error message' and the docstring wouldn't reveal that the contract changed. _(e.g. Step 6's `test_max_iterations_zero_equivalent_to_no_iterate_*` flagged the equivalence arm in the test description; the alternative arm would be 'rc=2 with clean error message'.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:iterative-correctness-completeness-passes#6</sub>
- **Test-only steps that exercise interrupted-then-resumed runs must explicitly require `_fresh_now()` (or equivalent dynamic-timestamp helper) for any seeded checkpoint, and an acceptance criterion should grep against hardcoded ISO timestamps to catch regressions.** — Checkpoint TTLs (often 30 days) silently turn cache-hit tests into cache-miss tests once the wall clock passes the static-timestamp threshold. The test passes on the dev's laptop today and starts failing in CI 30 days later for reasons that look like cache breakage. _(e.g. Step 6's acceptance criteria included 'no hardcoded "2026-01-01..." timestamps in step 6 additions' as a grep-checkable invariant against the `Checkpoint.load_if_valid` 30-day TTL.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:iterative-correctness-completeness-passes#6</sub>

- **Documentation-only steps that introduce a new user-facing CLI/UX surface across multiple docs are M-complexity at minimum — the work is cross-doc invariant maintenance (canonical source + summaries + agent-routing entries), not prose volume. Don't let 'docs-only' default to 'low'.** — Three files × ~40 edits × consistency invariants (exit codes, defaults, off-by-default claim) is real review surface; 'low' under-budgets reviewer time and signals to readers the step is trivial when it actually gates discoverability of the whole feature. _(e.g. Step 7 here was rated `low` but produced a canonical README section, a RUNBOOK quick-ref + workflow + three recovery entries, and a SKILL.md frontmatter + subsection + two decision-tree rows — and required reconciling against the detail bodies of steps 2/3/4/5/6 to keep claims consistent.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:iterative-correctness-completeness-passes#7</sub>
- **When a feature's user-facing semantics span more than one doc, designate exactly one file as canonical for the core explainer (e.g. decision tree, defaults table, exit-code matrix) and have the other docs summarize + link rather than re-state. Without this, mirrored content drifts after the first follow-on edit.** — Three independent decision trees diverge as soon as one is edited in isolation. A single canonical home with cross-links keeps the contract reviewable. _(e.g. Step 7 placed the canonical 'When to use --iterate' tree in README.md, a quick-ref row summary in RUNBOOK.md, and a single agent-routing table row in SKILL.md — all three pointing back to the README anchor.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:iterative-correctness-completeness-passes#7</sub>
- **Acceptance criteria for docs steps should include 'every relative anchor cited in the new sections resolves to a post-edit heading'. Markdown cross-links are fragile and not mechanically tested.** — Detail authors invent new anchors (`#iterating-to-convergence---iterate`) based on the heading text + markdown's anchor algorithm; a single typo silently breaks navigation and reviewers don't notice in a diff. _(e.g. Step 7's acceptance criteria explicitly listed anchor resolution as a check; without it, a reviewer would only catch a broken `#workflow-drive-an-authoring-task-to-convergence---iterate` link by clicking each link by hand.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:iterative-correctness-completeness-passes#7</sub>
- **Tests section for docs-only steps should enumerate specific editorial verification checks (diff documented flags against `--help` output, grep cross-links and confirm anchors, verify shared claims across files agree) rather than 'no tests' or 'manual review'. Treat the editorial checks as the test surface.** — Reviewers reach for the Tests section first; an empty/handwavy one signals 'unverifiable' and the docs ship without anyone confirming flag-list parity or cross-doc consistency. _(e.g. Step 7 listed three concrete editorial checks (CLI parity against argparse, decision-tree consistency across three files, anchor resolution) and named the source of truth for each (step 6's tests for invariants, argparse for flag set).)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:iterative-correctness-completeness-passes#7</sub>
- **Docs steps that land after code-landing predecessors should cite each predecessor's detail by `step N` + line range — the docs and the code must agree on edge cases (exit codes, defaults, halt conditions, hash extensions). The detail body should make the citation, not just `depends_on`.** — A docs step rated by complexity alone looks free, but it's actually a synthesis of N upstream contracts. Explicit citations let a reviewer trace 'where does this claim come from?' to a specific predecessor decision rather than guessing. _(e.g. Step 7 cited 'per step 2, lines 31–41' for create's hash-keying behavior, 'per step 4, lines 32–46' for the `WalkState(yes_to_all=True)` apply-all default, and 'per step 6's TestBudgetExhaustionExit' for the non-zero rc — each docs claim grounded in a code-landing decision.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:iterative-correctness-completeness-passes#7</sub>
- **When a step's `complexity: low` rating is paired with `depends_on: [several earlier steps]`, that's a smell — the dependency count is a proxy for synthesis work that pure prose volume hides. Bump such steps to M unless the docs are literally a one-line addition.** — Complexity heuristics tend to count files-changed or lines-of-prose; they miss 'this step has to reconcile N upstream contracts before writing a single sentence', which is the real work. _(e.g. Step 7 was `low` + `depends_on: [2, 3, 4, 5]` — the dependency list itself was the signal that this was M-shape work even though the file list was three existing docs and the diff was prose-only.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:iterative-correctness-completeness-passes#7</sub>

- **When a step title says 'mirroring `<existing_file>` conventions', create should include line-range anchors for the template's sections (imports / constants / dataclass / I/O primitives) in the step's `files` or notes, not just the bare filename.** — The detail re-derived the layout by reading `checkpoint.py` end-to-end and citing line spans like `checkpoint.py:49`, `:81-99`, `:118-142`, `:145-209`. Pre-baked anchors would have collapsed that into a one-shot read and made the resulting detail's references denser by default. _(e.g. Step 1 cited `checkpoint.py:37-46` (imports), `:49-62` (constants), `:65-78` (dataclass), `:118-142` (atomic write), `:145-209` (fail-safe read) — every one was load-bearing for a parallel section in `run_log.py`.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:run-log-structured-events#1</sub>
- **Treat a parenthetical enumeration in a step title (e.g. 'event dataclasses (RunStart, PhaseStart/End, ClaudeCallStart/End, ...)') as a hard contract: detail must produce a table with one row per item plus its required fields. Create should not collapse such enumerations into 'plus others'.** — The 12 enumerated event kinds drove the entire module shape (discriminator strings, frozen-dataclass per kind, optional token fields on ClaudeCallEnd). Missing even one would have forced step 2/3/4 detail-runs to retroactively patch the API. _(e.g. Step 1's title listed twelve event kinds; the detail produced a 12-row table with `event` discriminator and required fields per row, and an `Event` PEP-604 union alias spanning all twelve.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:run-log-structured-events#1</sub>
- **Constraints stated in the step title (stdlib-only, no edits to existing modules, no third-party deps) must become checkable acceptance criteria — `grep -E '^(import|from)'` for imports, `git diff <dir>` showing exactly N new files for scope — not just prose claims.** — Prose constraints drift; grep/diff criteria don't. The step title here said 'stdlib only' and the detail backed it with `grep -E '^(import|from)' run_log.py` lists `json|os|secrets|tempfile|dataclasses|datetime|pathlib|typing|__future__`. Future create proposals should pre-stage that grep so detail doesn't have to invent the assertion. _(e.g. Acceptance criteria included both the import grep and `git diff .agents/skills/plan-tool/plan_tool/` showing exactly one new file — making 'stdlib only' and 'no edits' falsifiable in one command each.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:run-log-structured-events#1</sub>
- **For foundation steps whose primary deliverable is a public API consumed by later steps, the detail's Deferred section should route each plausible-but-deferred addition to its specific downstream step and name *why*, not just list 'belongs to step N'.** — Without 'why', the downstream-step author re-litigates scope. With it, they inherit a decision. This is the same rule as the partner-SFTP lesson but applies just as strongly to in-codebase foundation steps with no vendor at all. _(e.g. `iter_events` → step 6 because nothing in 2–5 reads the log back; `RunLogWriter` convenience class → step 3 because freezing the shape before `cmd_create` actually uses it would over-fit; module-level active sink → step 2 because that's where `claude_invoke` lives.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:run-log-structured-events#1</sub>
- **When a step's title contains a non-obvious technical choice (e.g. 'atomic JSONL appender'), the detail must cite an authority for the atomicity claim in References, and the implementation must be defensible against the obvious alternative (e.g. `open(..., 'ab').write()` or `fcntl.flock`).** — Atomicity claims that aren't backed by a spec citation rot under review. Pre-emptively naming the ruled-out alternatives saves a review round-trip and protects the implementation from well-meaning 'simplifications'. _(e.g. Detail cited POSIX write(2) for the `O_APPEND` ≤ PIPE_BUF atomicity guarantee, and explicitly argued against `open('ab').write()` (buffered, no per-call-syscall guarantee) and `fcntl.flock` (serializes writers, costs a syscall per event).)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:run-log-structured-events#1</sub>
- **Test-count delta as an acceptance criterion ('1117 → ~1139') forces the test plan to be concrete enough to count. Encourage it for any step adding a new test file.** — A test-count delta is a cheap, automated post-hoc check that catches both 'forgot to add the tests' and 'wrote far more tests than scoped'. Counting also makes the detail author commit to a number, which sharpens the test enumeration. _(e.g. Step 1 listed ~22 tests grouped into five sections and asserted the suite-level delta in acceptance criteria; future plan-tool steps that add a `test_*.py` should do the same.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:run-log-structured-events#1</sub>

- **When a step modifies a module with a 1:1 sibling test file (e.g. `module.py` ↔ `test_module.py`), the create-time `files:` list should include the sibling test file by default; when no sibling exists for the *primary* file being changed, flag 'new test file required' explicitly rather than letting detail quietly invent one.** — Detail had to add `tests/test_shell.py` (not in the declared `files:`) because shell-behavior tests don't belong in `test_create.py`, and `test_create.py`'s fakes inject around `shell.claude_invoke` entirely. Forcing tests into the wrong file either bypasses the new code or leaks shell internals into create's test suite. _(e.g. Step 2 declared `files: [shell.py, test_create.py]`; the detail had to add `tests/test_shell.py` outside the declared list and call it out in the PR description.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:run-log-structured-events#2</sub>
- **For steps that modify a shared helper called by N modules, create should enumerate every call site (file:line) in the step body — and the heuristic should bump complexity by the caller count, not just by line count of the helper.** — The phrase 'keep `(rc, stdout)` tuple stable' only becomes auditable once detail can cite all five call sites; without that enumeration up front, the complexity rating underestimates blast radius and review can't tell whether the contract is actually preserved. _(e.g. Step 2's stability claim covered `create.py:144`, `detail.py:609`, `detail.py:1008`, `jira.py:110`, `reformat.py:439`, `review.py:422` — four discard rc, one surfaces it. The proposal didn't list any.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:run-log-structured-events#2</sub>
- **When a step threads a new optional kwarg through a helper with several existing callers, the detail should default the kwarg so existing call sites compile unchanged, and the step's acceptance criteria should include `git diff` against the caller files as a zero-change invariant.** — Default-keeping is the simplest mechanism to prevent the step from accidentally growing into 'and also touch 6 other modules'. Pinning it with a `git diff` invariant turns 'we didn't break compat' from a claim into a check the implementer can run. _(e.g. `*, phase='claude_invoke', attempt=1` defaults plus the acceptance line `git diff main -- .../{create,detail,jira,reformat,review}.py shows zero changes`.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:run-log-structured-events#2</sub>
- **For CLI-version / feature-availability detection inside a wrapper, prefer an in-band stderr-substring sniff with one retry to an out-of-band `--version` probe at startup; create proposals shouldn't be flagged for omitting a probe.** — A probe-on-every-startup adds latency for the common (modern CLI) case, while the in-band sniff only costs an extra subprocess when the user is on the version that needs the fallback — i.e. the exact case where they're already paying. _(e.g. Step 2's fallback rule: `meta is None and rc != 0 and '--output-format' in stderr → retry without the flag once`.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:run-log-structured-events#2</sub>
- **Vendor JSON envelopes parsed by the project should be consumed with per-field `isinstance` defensiveness and return `None` (not raise) on any shape mismatch; spell this out in the parser's docstring and in the test list.** — Downstream renderers (diff tables, summaries) crash on silent type drift (e.g. a stringified int sliding into a numeric column). Per-field guards are cheap and isolate the risk to the parser. _(e.g. `_parse_claude_json_envelope` guards every `usage.*` field with `isinstance(..., int)` and has a `test_parse_envelope_drops_wrong_typed_fields` regression.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:run-log-structured-events#2</sub>
- **When proposing an event-sink / hook installation point, the detail should specify (a) installer ownership (who calls `set_active_sink` and `clear` in `finally:`), (b) best-effort dispatch behavior on raise, and (c) a regression test that sink-raises does not break the caller — all three, not just (a).** — Sinks that crash the caller they observe are the classic regression for observability hooks. Pinning the contract in tests upfront prevents downstream steps that wire real sinks from having to re-discover this convention. _(e.g. Step 2 specified `try/except Exception` around the sink call with a stderr warning + `test_invoke_sink_exception_does_not_break_caller`.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:run-log-structured-events#2</sub>
- **For duration metrics that get sorted, diffed, or aggregated downstream, the detail must explicitly require `time.monotonic()` (or equivalent), not `time.time()`, and call out the downstream consumer that makes the choice load-bearing.** — Wall-clock can move backwards under NTP. The implementer won't reach for monotonic unless the detail names the downstream sort/diff that breaks if duration goes negative — naming the consumer turns a style preference into a hard requirement. _(e.g. Step 2's `duration_ms = int((time.monotonic() - started) * 1000)` is justified by step 6's `runs diff` using duration as a sort key.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:run-log-structured-events#2</sub>

- **When a step bundles multiple heterogeneous event types in one title (e.g. "emit claude_call_start and claude_call_end from the reporter tee"), the detail must split them by emission site — start events often belong at one layer, end events at another, because only one of those layers has the data the end event needs.** — This run's step title implied both ClaudeCall events came through the StderrReporter tee, but only ClaudeCallStart can — ClaudeCallEnd needs rc/tokens/request_id that only shell.claude_invoke sees. A single tee site for both would either double-count or under-report the meta envelope. _(e.g. Step 3 here: ClaudeCallStart fires from progress.StderrReporter.start_step (it has phase/attempt/prompt_bytes); ClaudeCallEnd fires from step 2's shell-side sink (it has rc/duration_ms/tokens). The Deferred section had to explicitly forbid emission from _StderrStepHandle.complete to prevent later duplication.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:run-log-structured-events#3</sub>
- **Steps that depend on earlier steps in the same plan should treat the dependency's external surface (function name, parameter shape, return type) as a load-bearing assumption and pin it explicitly in the detail — name the symbol the prior step exports, describe the data shape passed across the seam, and note that any divergence updates this step in one place.** — When step 3 wires step 2's sink, the sink's name and meta shape weren't fixed yet. The detail invented `set_active_call_end_sink(...)` and a `_CallMeta` field list (tokens_input, cache_creation_tokens, request_id, …). Without pinning, step 2 could ship a structurally different surface and force step 3 to be re-drafted mid-implementation. _(e.g. Step 3 names the seam explicitly: `shell.set_active_call_end_sink(sink)`, sink receives a `meta` with fields mirroring `ClaudeCallEnd`. If step 2 lands with different naming, only the `install_call_end_sink` body changes.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:run-log-structured-events#3</sub>
- **When introducing a new helper class that two-or-more later steps will also need, keep it private at the first call site and document the promotion criterion ("promote to public module when the Nth caller arrives") — don't pre-promote on the first caller.** — Pre-promoting freezes an API shape before multiple callers have exercised it, which is exactly the mistake step 1 deferred. The first caller's needs are a sample size of one; the right abstraction emerges when the second caller has to reuse it. _(e.g. _RunLogger lives inside cli.py for this step; the Deferred section says "step 4 (cmd_detail) or step 5 (cmd_run) — whichever lands first promotes _RunLogger to run_log.py". This matches step 1's own "Deferred: public RunLogWriter" stance.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:run-log-structured-events#3</sub>
- **Steps that emit lifecycle events (start/end pairs) around an existing function must track the outcome in a mutable local separate from the return value and update it on every exit branch — normal return, caught exception, re-raised exception (especially KeyboardInterrupt), so the on-disk record reflects the actual exit shape.** — A naive try/finally that only records "ok" on success and "error" otherwise loses information about user interrupts (rc=130, outcome="interrupted") and skipped phases (claude returned no parseable output → outcome="skipped", not "error"). The detail has to enumerate every exit path and what it writes. _(e.g. cmd_create rewrite uses `rc, outcome = 1, "error"` as defaults, sets `rc, outcome = 0, "ok"` only on success, sets `"interrupted", 130` on KeyboardInterrupt, then re-raises after finally writes run_end. The phase wrap similarly tracks outcome="skipped" when propose returns empty.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:run-log-structured-events#3</sub>
- **Any new observability/event-emission code that runs alongside a working user flow must use a best-effort write pattern (try/except OSError, log to stderr, continue) — a failing observation must never break the work being observed.** — Run logs, metrics, and traces are not the primary work product. If the disk fills or a permission flips, the user's create/detail/run should still succeed and produce the intended artifact. The instrumentation should announce its own failure but not abort. _(e.g. _RunLogger._emit wraps every append_event in try/except OSError, prints `plan-tool: run-log append failed (...); continuing without persistence` to stderr, and returns. Same pattern for write_in_flight and the unlink at end_run.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:run-log-structured-events#3</sub>
- **When extending the signature of an existing public function whose callers may include tests, monkey-patches, or wrapper code with positional invocations, add new arguments as keyword-only (`*, new_arg=...`) — not as positional with default.** — Positional defaults can collide with monkey-patched fakes that use *args/**kwargs spreads, and they make later removal of an intermediate arg an ABI break. Keyword-only is forwards-compatible. _(e.g. StderrReporter.start_step gained `*, phase=None, attempt=1, prompt_bytes=0`. Existing test callers like `reporter.start_step(1, 1, "label")` keep working because the new flags are after the keyword-only barrier.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:run-log-structured-events#3</sub>
- **Before adding a wrapper that infers a piece of information (e.g. "did this delete succeed"), check whether the underlying helper already returns that information — existing return values are often discarded at call sites and just need to be propagated.** — Easy to write redundant inference (re-check the filesystem after a delete) when the helper already tells you. Wastes a syscall and adds a divergence point if the helper's semantics change. _(e.g. discard_checkpoint already returns bool indicating whether a file existed and was removed (checkpoint.py:212-218); the existing call site at create.py:1583-1592 was throwing it away. The new emit-on-success branch just stopped ignoring it instead of re-stat'ing the path.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:run-log-structured-events#3</sub>

- **When wiring telemetry around an existing retry loop, emit per-attempt events explicitly inside the loop — do not reuse a per-phase reporter/tee that fires when the phase opens.** — A phase-level tee will fire once at the top of the phase, double-emitting on attempt 1 and under-emitting on attempts ≥ 2. The retry-loop case is the structural shape that distinguishes 'instrument this flow' from 'instrument that flow'. _(e.g. cmd_detail's retry loop (detail.py:837-866) runs invoker 1–2× per step; reusing reporter.start_step(phase=…) from step 3 would mis-emit. The fix is explicit emission inside the for-attempt loop, plus a one-line comment next to reporter.start_step explaining why phase= is deliberately omitted (so a future reader doesn't 'fix' it).)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:run-log-structured-events#4</sub>
- **Same low-level operation called from two semantically-distinct sites → one event kind with a `reason` field, not two event kinds.** — Keeps the event vocabulary flat and forces the schema to surface why the operation happened, which is what downstream views need to render. Two near-identical event kinds drift. _(e.g. discard_checkpoint is called from both the declined-resume site (reason: "user_declined_resume") and the post-write cleanup site (reason: "durable_write"). Single `checkpoint_discard` event with a reason field beats `checkpoint_declined` + `checkpoint_cleanup`.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:run-log-structured-events#4</sub>
- **Emit a telemetry event on operation success only; a swallowed/warned-on failure is a logging-system fact, not a user-visible run event.** — Keeps the JSONL a positive record of 'what happened' so that downstream filters (`outcome != success`, `count phase_end`) stay trustworthy. Otherwise the run log becomes a play-by-play of every code path entered, including ones that fizzled. _(e.g. checkpoint_save fires only inside `else:` after `save_checkpoint` returned without raising; the OSError path falls back to stderr only.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:run-log-structured-events#4</sub>
- **Reference large payloads from JSONL events by repo-relative path; never embed the payload in the event line.** — Line-bounded JSONL formats (step 1's 4096-byte ceiling here) force this, but the rule generalizes: dual-location storage (event line + sidecar file) drifts, and large raw responses (claude output, tracebacks, dumps) routinely exceed line ceilings. Repo-relative paths keep the JSONL portable across checkouts. _(e.g. parse_failure carries `path: ".plan-tool/parse-failures/<slug>/<NN>-attemptK-<ts>.txt"` and `raw_bytes`, never the response body itself. The `_log_parse_failure` helper already owns the on-disk archive.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:run-log-structured-events#4</sub>
- **Open the run-log writer AFTER input validation, not before — usage errors that fail before the run starts should leave nothing on disk.** — An unresolvable input is a user mistake, not a run. Emitting a ghost JSONL clutters `runs list`/`runs show` with stub entries that lack any meaningful content, undermining the 'run id == one JSONL file' contract. _(e.g. cmd_detail calls resolve_plan_for_context first; only then does _RunLogger.start open the writer. A bad --name surfaces as `error: unknown plan` with no `.plan-tool/runs/<unknown>/` file created.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:run-log-structured-events#4</sub>
- **Fold rc values into a string `outcome` by what downstream filters will query on, not by what the code structure looks like.** — Telemetry consumers think in 'success / error / cancelled', not in rc=0/1/2. Multiple error rcs collapsing to `outcome: "error"` lets `runs list --outcome error` find them all with one query. _(e.g. cmd_detail's rc=1 (per-step skip failure at cli.py:1163-1164) and rc=2 (DetailError at :1143-1145) both map to outcome="error"; `rc` is captured separately for forensics. Step 5 (cmd_run) should follow the same convention.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:run-log-structured-events#4</sub>
- **When instrumenting fallible call sites with context-manager 'sinks', use a `with` block so the sink uninstalls on exception — and provide a `_nullcontext()` fallback when the logger is absent so the call site stays single-branched.** — A raise mid-attempt without `with` leaves a stale sink installed for the next caller, leading to cross-contaminated events that are nearly impossible to debug later. The nullcontext fallback keeps the `if logger is None: invoker(p) else: with sink: invoker(p)` shape from forking the control flow. _(e.g. `with run_logger.bind_shell_sink(...): raw = invoker(attempt_prompt)` cleanly handles invoker raising in claude-not-installed scenarios; the `else: ctx = _nullcontext()` keeps the test path unchanged.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:run-log-structured-events#4</sub>

- **Instrumentation steps that wire a helper through multiple call layers (entry point + middle layer + leaf) need a "Non-edits worth naming" section in the detail that explicitly lists each function in the call graph that does NOT get the wrap and why.** — Without explicit non-edit callouts, reviewers ask "why isn't symmetric function X wrapped?" and future detail/review runs add the wraps symmetrically, creating churn and double-emission. _(e.g. Step 5's detail had to name review_walk.walk_recommendations, run_all_done_steps, verify_plan, and _dispatch_detail as deliberate non-edits — each for a different reason (shared caller, out-of-scope entry point, redundant frame). Without these callouts, a future plan would re-litigate each one.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:run-log-structured-events#5</sub>
- **When create proposes an instrumentation/observability step, the step title should pre-bake the phase/event-name taxonomy (the actual names + count), not just "emit events". Otherwise the detail spends a large fraction of its body justifying name choices from scratch.** — Phase-name choice is load-bearing for downstream consumers (renderers, dashboards). Locking the taxonomy at create time prevents every detail run from inventing it and forces the design conversation to happen up-front with the user. _(e.g. Step 5 needed five phase names (prompt_build / claude_call / reconcile_walk / apply_finding_dispatch / saved_reconcile_reuse), each mapping to a distinct user question. The detail had to invent and defend this taxonomy with a full table — work that belongs in the plan body.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:run-log-structured-events#5</sub>
- **A step's `depends_on` must include any earlier step whose private helper this step needs to share across modules — not just the schema/scaffolding steps. If the later step has to import the earlier helper from a module that doesn't already depend on the earlier step's home module, that's a hard dependency.** — Layering violations (e.g. leaf module importing entry-point module) become visible only at detail time. Listing the dependency at create time forces the right ordering and prevents the detail from looking like it grew unanticipated scope. _(e.g. Step 5 listed depends_on=[1, 2] but in practice also depended on step 3 (which introduced _RunLogger in cli.py) and step 4 (which fixed the .start() factory shape and the "no phase events for no-work paths" contract). Step 5's detail had to promote the helper to a shared module to avoid launcher.py→cli.py imports.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:run-log-structured-events#5</sub>
- **When create's step title says "budget N integration tests," treat that as the ceiling on E2E coverage only. Multi-layer instrumentation steps additionally need unit tests at each layer the helper is wired through; create should distinguish integration vs unit test budgets explicitly.** — Integration tests cover end-to-end ordering across the JSONL but can't pin per-function contracts (e.g. "run_step(run_logger=None) writes zero files"). Without unit tests at each layer, back-compat regressions surface only in CI failures unrelated to the new feature. _(e.g. Step 5 title budgeted 2 integration tests; the detail added 2 integration + 6 run_step unit + 5 apply_finding unit = 13 total tests. Each layer needed its own back-compat-when-logger-is-None unit test that the integration tests couldn't express.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:run-log-structured-events#5</sub>
- **When a step wires a helper through multiple modules, create's `files` list must include the test file for each module being edited — not just the top-layer test file.** — Forgetting a sibling test file makes the step look like it edits only one module; in practice the unit-test coverage will land in the sibling. Listing both up front sets the right expectation for reviewers and prevents "why are you editing test_X if X isn't in files?" pushback. _(e.g. Step 5's files correctly included both test_cli.py and test_launcher.py because both cli.py and launcher.py were edited. Future instrumentation steps should follow this pattern; create-time audit should glob for the test file alongside each proposed source file.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:run-log-structured-events#5</sub>
- **When a private helper introduced in step N needs to be reused across modules in step M (M>N), step M's detail must include an explicit "promote to public API" subsection naming the new path, the rename, the back-compat alias, and the layering rationale — even when this looks like scope creep.** — Cross-module reuse of a private helper IS the load-bearing decision in a wiring step. Hiding it in implementation prose makes the detail look like it's silently growing scope; surfacing it as a dedicated subsection (with a Deferred entry for the eventual alias removal) keeps the diff defensible. _(e.g. Step 5 promoted _RunLogger (step 3, cli.py) → RunLogWriter (plan_tool/run_log.py) because launcher.py can't import cli.py without creating a layering inversion. The detail named this in its first subsection and added a Deferred entry to drop the back-compat alias once step 3's tests are updated.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:run-log-structured-events#5</sub>
- **For instrumentation steps that touch a high-fan-in entry point with multiple return paths, the detail's acceptance criteria should pin the *outcome* field for each return path (success/error/reverted/interrupted) by-name — not just "emits run_end".** — Each return path is independently testable and independently regressable. Listing the (rc → outcome) mapping by row in acceptance criteria gives reviewers a checklist and gives future implementers a clear contract for what the JSONL should look like under each branch. _(e.g. Step 5's cmd_run has four return paths (saved-reuse exit, dry-run exit, run_step nonzero exit, happy-path advance) plus KeyboardInterrupt. The detail's acceptance criteria spelled out rc=130→outcome=error+rc=130 for the interrupt, rc=1 for critical findings → outcome=error, etc. Without this row-by-row spec, integration tests would only cover the two budgeted shapes.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:run-log-structured-events#5</sub>

- **When a step is framed as 'mirror existing command X but add one new action', complexity is structurally higher than pure copy-paste — the new action carries a contract X doesn't constrain. Create should rate from the new action's surface, not the mirror's shape.** — The mirror gives error-code contract, argparse shape, and traversal hardening for free; the new action (here `diff`) introduces a fresh aggregation, render format, and pairing/skip semantics that no existing test or code anchors. Treating the whole step as 'looks like cmd_checkpoint' under-budgets review and test count. _(e.g. Step 6 was 'mirror cmd_checkpoint' + adds `diff <a> <b>`. The mirror covered list/show/clear in ~one paragraph each; diff needed its own contract (phase pairing, only-in-A/B flagging, dash-vs-zero for tokens, trailing summary line) and 7 of the 26 tests.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:run-log-structured-events#6</sub>
- **Plan titles that say 'X default' or '<value> default' are ambiguous between 'flag defaults to that value when passed bare' vs 'documented-recommended constant the caller should pass'. Detail should call out both readings and pin one with rationale; create proposals should phrase this unambiguously up front.** — These are different implementations (argparse `nargs='?', const=…` vs a module-level constant cited in help text + downstream docs) with different UX. Picking the wrong one mid-implementation forces a re-spec and surprises users who type `clear --older-than` expecting 'default kicks in'. _(e.g. The step title said `--older-than 30d TTL eviction default`. Reading (a) implies bare-flag default; reading (b) implies a recommended-value constant. The detail picked (b) and named the rejected (a) explicitly, but a clearer title would have read `--older-than <DURATION> flag with 30d as the recommended-value constant`.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:run-log-structured-events#6</sub>
- **Destructive cache-management commands (`clear`, `prune`, `evict`) need explicit handling for *live* state files (in-flight markers, lockfiles, sentinel files) separately from inert records. TTL-based eviction should usually preserve live markers; full wipes can remove them.** — Live markers track currently-running invocations or coordination state; using their mtime as an age proxy races with the active writer, and evicting them mid-run breaks the coordination they exist for. This rule isn't in `cmd_checkpoint` because checkpoints have no live-marker analog — it surfaces only when the new cache adds one. _(e.g. Step 6's `clear --older-than 30d` had to preserve `<repo>/.plan-tool/runs/<slug>/in-flight.json` (captures a currently-running invocation), but `clear --all` removes it (semantic full wipe). The detail invented this rule because the create proposal didn't flag the live-marker interaction at all.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:run-log-structured-events#6</sub>
- **When the deterministic 'already-implemented' heuristic matches a symbol the new step is *mirroring* rather than the new feature itself, flag the false alarm explicitly in the detail. Reviewers and downstream agents otherwise read 'matched cmd_X' as 'feature X is already done'.** — Mirror-pattern signals are common when a CLI grows by adding a parallel subcommand. Without an explicit 'this is the template, not the target' note in the detail, the next agent may skip the step or re-confirm the wrong file. _(e.g. The heuristic matched `def cmd_checkpoint` as evidence step 6 might be partially implemented. It wasn't — `cmd_checkpoint` is the template the new `cmd_runs` mirrors. Detail should always disambiguate in a one-liner near the top.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:run-log-structured-events#6</sub>
- **For CLI subcommands that combine multiple filter/scope flags (`--all`, `<positional>`, `--older-than`), enumerate which combinations are legal in the detail before listing tests. Users will combine flags the title implies are mutually exclusive, and the implementation has to either reject or compose them.** — Step titles tend to compress flag-set semantics into 'flag1/flag2 requirement on action'. That phrasing suggests mutual exclusivity, but real CLIs usually want composition (`clear <slug> --older-than 30d` = 'age-evict under one slug'). Resolving this at detail time prevents an extra round-trip in review. _(e.g. Step 6 title read `--all/<slug> requirement on clear, --older-than 30d TTL eviction default`. The detail had to decide: are these three modes, or can they compose? It chose composition (`clear demo --older-than 7d` evicts old runs under `demo` only) and added a test for the combined case.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:run-log-structured-events#6</sub>

- **Step titles that concatenate 3+ distinct contracts with commas/semicolons should be flagged at create time as either 'split into N steps' or 'promote complexity to high'. A single 'medium' rating undercounts the test-organization work even when the file list is short.** — Step 7's title named five separate behaviors (schema-version mismatch, malformed JSONL, path-traversal, --older-than cutoff, baseline preservation). Create rated medium with two files; the detail produced ~17 tests across five named contract groups plus a helper and a meta-test. The work scaled with the title's clause count, not with the file count. _(e.g. Title: 'X causes read-skip not crash; malformed JSONL exits 1; path-traversal exits 2; --older-than respected; baseline stays green' → flag as high-complexity or propose decomposition; reviewer should expect ~3-4 tests per clause.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:run-log-structured-events#7</sub>
- **Phrases like 'existing N-test baseline stays green' or 'regression suite passes' in a step title are verification actions, not tests. The detail must convert them into an automation-friendly proxy (e.g. `git diff --name-only <base> -- tests/` against an allowlist drawn from the plan's own `files:` entries across all steps) — never leave them as manual checks.** — Without a proxy test, the failure mode 'someone edits an existing test to make the regression pass' has no automated guard. The proxy also doubles as scope enforcement — it catches accidental edits to unrelated test files in the same commit. _(e.g. Step 7 added `test_no_existing_tests_were_modified` that asserts every diffed test file lives in the union of `files:` lists across steps 1–7, with `pytest.mark.skipif` for the non-git tarball case.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-14 via detail:run-log-structured-events#7</sub>
- **Boundary tests for any threshold (time cutoffs, count limits, age windows) must pin both sides: `cutoff + ε` and `cutoff - ε`.
