The signal is `depends_on: 1, 2, 3, 4` on a step whose `files:` list is purely tests. _(e.g. Step 5 of `lessons-consolidate-and-dedup`: `files: [test_lessons.py, test_cli.py]` with `depends_on: 1, 2, 3, 4` produced 60+ methods covering 12 distinct contract surfaces (primitive, cache, strict-check, warn-check, JSONL round-trip, writer cap-trim, reader malformed-skip, recurrence counts, recurrence weights, CLI diff/apply/dry-run/headless/plan-scoped/ignore-recurrence).)_  <sub>added 2026-05-18 via detail:lessons-consolidate-and-dedup#5</sub>
- **When an earlier step widens a public function's signature with new keyword-only params (e.g. `strict_check=`, `warn_check=`, `equivalence_invoker=`), the test step MUST land a regression-pin class FIRST — exercising the legacy call shape byte-for-byte — before adding any test that uses the new params. List this pin explicitly in the test step's detail and acceptance criteria.** — The plan's Risks section flagged the regression but didn't bind a test to it. Without a named, first-position pin class, reviewers can't tell at a glance whether the existing-behavior contract is preserved. The pin doubles as the failure-fast signal when a future refactor accidentally rewrites the legacy path. _(e.g. `TestSubstringDedupeRegression` placed before any new-behavior test class in `test_lessons.py`, with three methods exercising the new keyword-only matrix (`strict_check=False, warn_check=False`) and asserting byte-identical output to the pre-step-3 contract — plus an iterable-tuple-unpack pin for the new `AppendLessonsResult` return type.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-18 via detail:lessons-consolidate-dedup#5</sub>
- **Detail bodies for test-only steps should enumerate every `test_*` method by name with a one-line rationale, grouped by test class. The method-list IS the implementation spec; without it, reviewers cannot judge whether the step covers the plan's acceptance criteria, and the implementer has to re-derive coverage from prose.** — Production-code step details get away with prose because the diff itself shows the surface. Test-step diffs are mostly assertions, and assertions only mean something paired with their target contract. A named test method per behavior makes the coverage matrix legible at review time. _(e.g. Step 5's Tests section enumerated 60+ methods grouped under 12 named classes (`TestParseEquivalenceResponse`, `TestFindLessonEquivalences`, `TestAppendLessonsStrictCheck`, `TestAppendLessonsWarnCheck`, `TestLessonsWarning`, `TestAppendWarning`, `TestLoadWarnings`, `TestRecurrenceCounts`, `TestRecurrenceWeightFromCounts`, `TestCmdLessonsConsolidate`, `TestLessonsHelpSurface`, `TestSubstringDedupeRegression`), each method carrying one sentence of intent.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-18 via detail:lessons-consolidate-dedup#5</sub>
- **Acceptance criteria for test-only steps should include a grep-able invariant for the step's anti-pattern (e.g. "no test calls live `claude_invoke`" or "no new tests use `unittest.mock.patch` to stub the production module"). State the exact grep that proves it.** — Test steps drift toward convenience-anti-patterns (over-mocking, stubbing production internals, shelling out to real services) faster than production-code steps, because the test author optimizes for green over signal. A grep-able invariant in acceptance criteria gives both author and reviewer a binary check. _(e.g. Step 5's acceptance criteria included `grep -rn "claude_invoke\b" .agents/skills/plan-tool/tests/ | grep -v "shell_equivalence_invoker"` returning no new hits — pinning the rule that every claude interaction in tests goes through an injected fake.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-18 via detail:lessons-consolidate-dedup#5</sub>
- **When a test step shares helper fixtures across multiple test files (e.g. `_FakeInvoker` used in both `test_lessons.py` and `test_cli.py`), the detail should pick one canonical home and explicitly say the other file imports it, rather than letting the implementer redefine it. Call this out in the Files section.** — Two divergent fakes is a foot-gun — they drift, and a test that should fail on one path passes because the wrong fake had the wrong default. Naming the canonical location at detail time prevents the duplication entirely. _(e.g. Step 5 designated `_FakeInvoker` lives in `test_lessons.py` and `test_cli.py` imports it via `from tests.test_lessons import _FakeInvoker`. The detail noted the alternative (redefine in each file) was considered and rejected.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-18 via detail:lessons-consolidate-dedup#5</sub>
- **For steps that produce only tests, the Files section should explicitly split "new files" from "existing files (edits only)". Even when `files:` lists only existing paths, restating "no new files" prevents the implementer from creating a `conftest.py` or `helpers.py` that the step didn't budget for.** — Test steps invite "just one helper file" scope creep, especially when prior steps added a lot of new public surface. An explicit "new files: none" line in the detail blocks that creep at review time. _(e.g. Step 5's Files section opened with `**New files:** _none._` followed by a per-existing-file edit description, anchoring the rule that helpers land alongside the tests that use them.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-18 via detail:lessons-consolidate-dedup#5</sub>

- **When a step touches 3+ documentation files, treat it as 'doc-fan-out' and rate it medium, not low — each surface has its own conventions, anchor slugs, and insertion points that the detail must reconcile.** — Per-file diffs are surgical, but the coordination cost (anchor slugs that must match across files, table-cluster placement, cross-reference targets) is what makes the step non-trivial. A 'low' rating under-budgets the detail's planning load. _(e.g. Step 6 here was rated low and touched README.md, RUNBOOK.md, lessons/general.md, and BACKLOG.md — four distinct conventions (fenced CLI block, clustered quick-reference table, canonical bullet shape, stable backlog numbering) that the detail had to specify individually.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-18 via detail:lessons-consolidate-dedup#6</sub>
- **Doc-update steps that follow shipping steps should reference flag names / file paths / function signatures by indirection ('match what step N wired'), not assert literals — the as-shipped code is authoritative and the detail can be drafted before steps 1–N actually land.** — Detail drafts are written before implementation; asserting literal flag names risks drift if the implementer renames during step N. Indirection keeps the doc-step detail correct regardless of cosmetic choices in upstream steps. _(e.g. Step 6's README CLI line was specified as 'match the exact flag set step 2 wired into p_lessons_consolidate' rather than committing to --min-confidence vs --confidence; the implementer reads the as-shipped subparser and matches.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-18 via detail:lessons-consolidate-dedup#6</sub>
- **When a doc-update step adds a new H2 section that other surfaces will cross-link, pre-decide the anchor slug in the detail and use the same slug in every cross-reference — don't leave it as 'TBD'.** — GitHub-flavored markdown auto-slugs H2 titles deterministically (lowercase, spaces→hyphens, punctuation stripped). Specifying the slug up-front lets the detail write concrete inter-file links; leaving it implicit forces a second pass once the title is finalized. _(e.g. Step 6 fixed #workflow-consolidate-lessons-files and #lessons-consolidation slugs in the detail so the README→RUNBOOK and RUNBOOK→README cross-references could be specified as literal anchor URLs, not placeholders.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-18 via detail:lessons-consolidate-dedup#6</sub>
- **Shipping plans should bundle 'remove the corresponding backlog/TODO entry' into the same final step that updates user-facing docs — not defer it as follow-up cleanup.** — Backlog files decay unless their preamble's 'remove when shipped' rule is enforced in the same PR that ships the feature. Deferring leaves the doc internally inconsistent (feature documented as shipped in README, still listed as future work in BACKLOG) for an unbounded window. _(e.g. Step 6 paired README/RUNBOOK updates with BACKLOG.md removals of #35 and the bundled Bucket D entry in a single step, closing both loops in one PR.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-18 via detail:lessons-consolidate-dedup#6</sub>
- **The deterministic already-implemented signal matches symbol presence, not entry presence — when it flags a doc file because a section heading matches, the detail must still verify the specific entry is absent rather than concluding the work is done.** — The heuristic can detect 'this file has a `## CLI` section' but not 'the CLI section is missing the new subcommand row'. Treating the flag as 'already implemented' would skip needed edits; treating it as 'section exists, append within it' is the correct read. _(e.g. Step 6's signal flagged README.md via `## CLI` matching `## CLI usage`, but the lessons consolidate line was not in the fenced block — the detail correctly read this as 'append to existing section', not 'skip the README edit'.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-18 via detail:lessons-consolidate-dedup#6</sub>

- **When create lists a barrel/index file in `files:`, the detail must either edit it or explicitly confirm 'no edit needed' with the reason — silently dropping a listed file makes reviewers assume the step is incomplete.** — Reviewers see a listed file with no diff and ask 'where's the change?'. One sentence ('the root index already does `export * as utils from "./utils"`, so no edit needed') resolves the ambiguity cheaply. _(e.g. Step listed `packages/shared/src/index.ts` and `packages/shared/src/constants/application-areas.ts` but neither needed edits — detail explicitly stated this and the reason for each.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-19 via detail:ai-chat-deep-linking#1</sub>
- **Create-time, when proposing a new module under a domain folder with a sibling `index.ts` barrel (`constants/index.ts`, `utils/index.ts`), add that barrel to the step's `files:` automatically — otherwise the new module is unreachable and detail must surface the addition as out-of-scope-list mid-draft.** — Easy heuristic to bolt on (glob for `index.ts` next to any `new` file proposal) that eliminates a recurring friction point. Without it, detail either adds the re-export silently (scope creep) or has to write a PR-description carve-out. _(e.g. Step proposed `packages/shared/src/constants/chat-deep-link-catalog.ts (new)` but omitted `packages/shared/src/constants/index.ts`. Detail had to call out the added `export * from './chat-deep-link-catalog'` as a 'deliberate scoped extension'.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-19 via detail:ai-chat-deep-linking#1</sub>
- **Already-implemented signals that match on generic identifiers (`const chat`, `const data`, `const result`, `function handle`) should be marked low-confidence in the prompt or suppressed entirely — they are almost always false positives and cost detail a paragraph to refute.** — The signal must justify the read cost it imposes. A match on `const chat` against the literal pattern `const chat` carries near-zero information; a match on a function name containing the step's domain keyword carries real signal. Filter by specificity, not just substring. _(e.g. Heuristic flagged `const chat` inside `apps/mobile/app/aiChat/[id].tsx`'s `MessageBubble` adapter as possibly being an existing resolver wiring — it was an unrelated local variable in a `messages.map` block.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-19 via detail:ai-chat-deep-linking#1</sub>
- **When a step touches markdown rendering, the detail must cite the rendering library's link-interception API exactly (e.g. `react-markdown` `components` map, `react-native-markdown-display` `onLinkPress` return semantics) — these APIs differ between platforms and getting them wrong silently breaks interception.** — Returning `false` vs `true` vs `undefined` from `onLinkPress` changes whether the OS handler fires; component overrides vs renderer hooks are different mechanisms. Without spelling this out, the implementer guesses and the interception fails open. _(e.g. Detail had to specify `onLinkPress={(url) => { …; return false }}` for mobile to suppress `Linking.openURL`, and a separate `components={{ a: ChatLink }}` prop for web `react-markdown` — two different APIs for the same conceptual change.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-19 via detail:ai-chat-deep-linking#1</sub>
- **When a step edits a constants/route map that contains stale `// TODO:` markers, the detail should fix only the markers the plan body names and explicitly leave the rest, citing the plan's 'Out of scope' clause — otherwise reviewers will ask why the other TODOs weren't addressed.** — Editing two of seven TODO rows without explanation looks arbitrary. A one-line carve-out per untouched TODO category prevents 'while you're in there' scope creep at review time. _(e.g. Detail fixed `WebDeepLinks.MENTOR` and `OPPORTUNITIES` but explicitly left `EXPLORE`, `FUNDING`, `AI_CHAT`, etc. unchanged with a pointer to the plan's 'Out of scope' section.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-19 via detail:ai-chat-deep-linking#1</sub>
- **When a step ships a public API (resolver, builder, normalizer) with multiple exports, the detail should enumerate each export's signature in numbered list form with its return type and one-line behavior — not bury them in prose.** — Implementers and reviewers both read the export surface as a contract. A numbered, signature-typed list collapses a half-page of prose into a scannable contract; ambiguity in return types ('returns null vs throws') becomes obvious. _(e.g. Step 1's `deep-link-resolver.ts` declared five exports as a numbered list — `isAppLink`, `resolveAppLink`, `applicationAreaFromAppLink`, `buildAppLink`, `tryNormalizeInAppHref` — each with full discriminated-union return type spelled out.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-19 via detail:ai-chat-deep-linking#1</sub>

- **When create's `files` list labels an artifact as `(generated)`, the detail must invent both the generator script and the build-script wiring, and flag both as scope additions. Create should propose the generator script as an explicit file when it tags any output `(generated)`, or omit the tag.** — A `(generated)` file is not self-creating; without a script, the on-disk file diverges from its declared source the moment anyone hand-edits it. Inventing the script mid-detail is unbudgeted scope creep that reviewers may push back on. _(e.g. Step 2 declared `packages/shared/src/constants/chat-deep-link-catalog.json (generated)` with no generator. Detail had to add `packages/shared/scripts/generate-chat-deep-link-catalog-json.ts` and wire it into `pnpm build:shared`, both flagged as scope additions in the PR description.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-19 via detail:ai-chat-deep-linking#2</sub>
- **When a step requires the same canonical data in multiple sources of truth (e.g. TS catalog + JSON file + foreign-language literal), the drift gate test is the load-bearing artifact — not a nice-to-have. Detail must enumerate every mirror site and assert each pair-wise in the same test file.** — The 'single source' framing in step titles is often aspirational — the design ends up with N mirrors kept in lockstep by CI. Naming each mirror up front prevents a future contributor from updating two of three and shipping a silently-broken agent. _(e.g. Step 2's drift test asserts four equalities: TS↔JSON byte-exact, JSON↔Python literal list, catalog↔resolver round-trip, and agent.py prompt section ⊆ catalog. Missing any one of these leaves a real drift path uncovered.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-19 via detail:ai-chat-deep-linking#2</sub>
- **When a step inserts new sanitization / transformation passes into an existing pipeline, the detail must document the ordering invariants between passes (which runs first, why, what breaks if reversed) — even when the current implementation makes order incidental.** — Future refactors of one pass (e.g. tightening a redaction regex) can silently break the contract that a sibling pass relied on. The ordering rationale belongs in the detail body, not in a comment that may not survive a refactor. _(e.g. Step 2 inserts `injectDeepLinks` → `sanitizePersistedChatLinks` → `filterAgentResponse` and explicitly notes that today's redaction regex doesn't match markdown link syntax, but a future tightening could, so all link-shaped transforms must finish before redaction runs.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-19 via detail:ai-chat-deep-linking#2</sub>
- **For steps that wire a shared-library helper into a service that is bundled, packed, or content-addressed for deploy (Docker images, Lambda layers, Bedrock agent runtimes), the detail must check the bundle/hash configuration before proposing 'load from disk at runtime' designs.** — Deploy caches keyed on a file-hash list will not invalidate when an out-of-list file changes, silently shipping stale code. Discovering this mid-implementation is expensive; calling it out at detail time forces an explicit design choice (in-file literal vs. add-to-hash-list). _(e.g. Step 2 keeps `CHAT_DEEP_LINK_CATALOG_APP_URLS` as a Python literal inside `agent.py` (already hashed) rather than reading the shared JSON at runtime, because `computeAgentHash` in `amplify/bedrock/resource.ts` only hashes four explicit files.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-19 via detail:ai-chat-deep-linking#2</sub>

- **When a step's `files:` list contains source files but the step is verification-only (manual QA, observability check, ops dry-run), the detail must label them 'observation targets — do not edit' and route any defect found to the originating step's PR. Create should ideally tag observation-only files at proposal time to prevent the next implementer from mutating them.** — Without the explicit label, an implementer reads `files: [foo.tsx]` as 'edit foo.tsx', which converts a doc-only QA step into a code change and violates the step's `git diff` invariant. Reviewers also lose the ability to use the file list as a coverage checklist. _(e.g. Step 3 listed `apps/mobile/app/aiChat/[id].tsx`, `apps/mobile/app/secondaryScreens/aiChat/[id].tsx`, and `apps/web/src/shared/components/ChatScreenStyled.tsx` so reconcile could confirm every chat surface was exercised — but the detail had to explicitly say 'do not edit; defects belong to step 1's PR'.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-19 via detail:ai-chat-deep-linking#3</sub>
- **Manual QA matrices for cross-platform persistence features are M-complexity minimum, not 'low'. They require: a Builds-under-test table (with same-backend-env invariant), one scenario table per platform for single-platform parity, a separate matrix for cross-device read/write directions, negative cases for adjacent schemes/paths, and a sign-off block. Treating them as 'low' under-budgets review and produces ad-hoc checklists.** — The cross-device read acceptance criterion ('start on platform A, open on platform B') is observational by construction and lives below the unit-test boundary — it gates the feature. Misrating it 'low' lets it ship as a stub. _(e.g. Step 3 here looked like 'just a checklist' but needed Scenarios A–E, 33 confirmed cells in Scenario A alone, and a builds-table invariant pinning iOS/Android/web to the same backend env so cross-device reads were meaningful.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-19 via detail:ai-chat-deep-linking#3</sub>
- **Any QA acceptance criterion stated as a percentage (e.g. '≥95% of replies satisfy X') must come with a minimum sample size per build in the verification artifact. Without it, 'sample some' is unfalsifiable and the criterion is effectively unverified.** — Reviewers cannot confirm a percentage threshold without knowing the denominator. Sample size also has to be per-build, not aggregate, when the property varies by platform. _(e.g. Scenario E required ≥20 sampled AI replies per build (iOS / Android / web) before the ≥95% link-density compliance figure could be recorded.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-19 via detail:ai-chat-deep-linking#3</sub>
- **Cross-device persistence verification steps must hard-pin every build under test to the same backend environment (sandbox identifier, dev, qa) and call out that a mismatch invalidates the matrix.** — Cross-device reads depend on the same underlying records being readable from all clients. Without the pin, testers will mix sandbox-A mobile and sandbox-B web and get false greens because they're reading different rows. _(e.g. Step 3's Builds-under-test table marks the matrix invalid if any row's `Backend env` differs — the cross-device AC depends on the same `SessionLog` rows being readable from all three clients.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-19 via detail:ai-chat-deep-linking#3</sub>
- **For features that persist a single rendered string (markdown, HTML, plaintext) into shared storage and re-display it on heterogeneous clients, the plan must include negative cases for both write-path sanitization regressions and read-path normalization regressions — not just happy-path round-trips.** — Same-string, multi-client features have two failure surfaces: (1) the write path persisted the wrong shape, (2) the read path failed to normalize a legacy/pre-rollout shape. Tests at only one boundary miss the other. _(e.g. Scenario C tested pre-rollout `SessionLog` rows (read-path normalization of legacy hrefs) and Scenario D's '[ghost](app://nope)' row tested write-path sanitization defense-in-depth — both were necessary.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-19 via detail:ai-chat-deep-linking#3</sub>
- **When a manual-QA step has a `Catalog version` / `Schema version` / `Contract version` that lives in code, the QA artifact header must include the version and the acceptance criteria must require it to equal the literal value in code. This makes 'is this matrix still valid?' a one-line check after later commits.** — Manual matrices go stale silently when the underlying contract changes. A version-equality AC turns a future rebase into an automatic re-run signal. _(e.g. The doc header pins `Catalog version: mvp-1` and the AC requires it to equal `CATALOG_VERSION` (TS) and `CHAT_DEEP_LINK_CATALOG_VERSION` (Python) — a bump invalidates the prior run.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-19 via detail:ai-chat-deep-linking#3</sub>

- **Review's symbol-presence heuristic should treat extremely generic tokens from the step title (e.g. `chat`, `prompt`, `user`, `message`, `agent`, `handler`) as stopwords — matching `const chat` or `const prompt` against any file in an AI/chat codebase produces near-guaranteed false-positive 'already done' signals that add noise without evidence.** — Both findings in this review fired on `chat` and `prompt` matching trivial declarations in files that the plan was about to modify — the matches told us nothing about whether the step's actual work (deep-linking behavior) had been implemented. A reviewer reading the report has to manually discount every such hit, eroding trust in the heuristic. _(e.g. Step 1 ('looks already done') matched `const chat` in `apps/mobile/app/aiChat/[id].tsx` — but that file is the *target* of the deep-linking work, not evidence it's complete. Step 2 matched `const prompt` in `amplify/lambda/ai-chat-invoke/handler.ts` for the same reason.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-19 via review:ai-chat-deep-linking</sub>

- **When a plan's slug or title contains generic domain tokens that saturate the target codebase (e.g. `chat`, `prompt`, `user` in an AI-chat feature plan), step-title symbol searches will collide with the very implementation the plan is extending. Detail should preempt this by listing existing extended files in step 1 as a baseline, so later token-collision findings can be disambiguated as 'expected extension surface' rather than 'possible prior implementation'.** — Both findings on this review were low-confidence hits on `chat` and `prompt` in an AI chat deep-linking plan against an AI chat codebase — pure domain-token noise that a baseline-of-existing-files in step 1 would have neutralized. _(e.g. Step 1 of `ai-chat-deep-linking` should have explicitly named `apps/mobile/app/aiChat/[id].tsx` and `amplify/lambda/ai-chat-invoke/handler.ts` as the existing surfaces being extended; the heuristic then has a baseline to subtract from.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-20 via review:ai-chat-deep-linking</sub>

- **When a plan's domain matches the project's primary domain (e.g. an AI-chat plan in an AI-chat app), title-token symbol search produces mostly false-positive hints — reviewers should anchor on distinctive identifiers named in the step body (specific route params, deep-link schemes, query keys, function names) rather than generic title tokens.** — Tokens like `chat`, `prompt`, `user`, `auth` will match dozens of unrelated declarations in a domain-aligned codebase, drowning real signal in noise and tempting status flips based on collisions. Both findings in this review were exactly this pattern. _(e.g. Step 1's `chat` token matched `const chat` in `apps/mobile/app/aiChat/[id].tsx` and step 2's `prompt` token matched `const prompt` in `amplify/lambda/ai-chat-invoke/handler.ts` — neither is evidence of deep-linking work, just incidental use of common nouns.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-20 via review:ai-chat-deep-linking</sub>

- **Inventory/standard docs should cite source-of-truth symbol names (function, exported constant, env-var key, sh-script section heading), never line numbers — and the acceptance criteria should encode this.** — Line numbers drift on every nearby commit and invalidate the doc; symbols stay grep-able and let reviewers and future plan-tool runs verify rows by a single grep. _(e.g. Step 1 anchored 'AssessmentService parameter owner' to the 'AssessmentService Integration Parameters' block in scripts/create-ssm-parameters.sh rather than a line range, so re-ordering the script won't silently break the inventory.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-20 via detail:app-parameter-standardization#1</sub>
- **For 'publish the standard' steps that gate downstream correction/migration steps, the acceptance criteria must explicitly preserve invariants the later steps will be measured against — not just enumerate what the doc says.** — Without locked-in invariants, a later step can 'harmonize' the standard by analogy and silently change the contract before the corrections that depended on it land. Naming the invariants here turns the doc into a stable target. _(e.g. Step 1's acceptance criteria pin 'AssessmentService paths must render with {environment}, NOT {instanceName} by analogy with LaborDataService', because step 5 might otherwise migrate by lookalike.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-20 via detail:app-parameter-standardization#1</sub>
- **Steps that produce a single canonical doc should enumerate stable H2 section anchors by name in the acceptance criteria, in fixed order.** — Downstream steps link into the doc by section anchor; if the section list or order drifts, every link breaks silently. Counting sections by name in acceptance criteria locks the structure. _(e.g. Step 1 named the seven H2 sections (Scopes, The Standard, Inventory, Cross-repo flow, Pipeline secret propagation, Bedrock parameter list, SecureString ownership model) in order so step 2's correction targets can link to them.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-20 via detail:app-parameter-standardization#1</sub>
- **Detail should record known oddities (dead permissions, deprecated paths, doc-vs-code mismatches, unused parameters) inline in the canonical artifact as flagged entries with cleanup explicitly deferred to a separate step.** — Turns the doc into a registry of known issues without expanding current scope. Stopping to fix each oddity bloats the step; ignoring them lets them rot. A flagged-and-deferred entry is the minimum viable record. _(e.g. Step 1 flagged the dead /app/${instanceName}/bedrock/* read permission in createBedrockStack's runtimeRole (no creator exists anywhere) and explicitly deferred cleanup to a future step.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-20 via detail:app-parameter-standardization#1</sub>
- **When the create step proposes a single new doc but the plan declares a sibling `lessons.md` path in frontmatter, the detail must include that lessons file in 'Files (creates vs edits)' even though it's an empty stub.** — Future plan-tool review/detail runs append to the path declared in frontmatter; if step 1 doesn't create it, every later run has to handle the 'first lesson' bootstrap case. Easy to omit because it's not in `files:`. _(e.g. Step 1's files block included docs/implementation-plans/plan-details/app-parameter-standardization/lessons.md as a one-line H1 stub matching the plan's `lessons:` frontmatter field.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-20 via detail:app-parameter-standardization#1</sub>

- **The deterministic 'already-implemented' heuristic produces high false-positive rates when its match pattern is a single common English word or a generic feature name; detail should treat these flags as 'look here once' pointers and not as evidence the step is partially done.** — This run's heuristic flagged `docs/backend-guide.md` because `### Real` matched pattern `real`, and `docs/labor-data-service-runbook.md` because `# LaborDataService` matched pattern `LaborDataService` — neither indicates any prior implementation work; both are just normal doc headings. _(e.g. Compare to a useful signal like a path pattern (`/example/app/{instanceName}/advice-service/apikey`) appearing verbatim in two files — that would be load-bearing. Single-word matches against doc headings are noise.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-20 via detail:app-parameter-standardization#2</sub>
- **For doc-correction steps that fix path/identifier mismatches, the detail must verify each value against the source-of-truth symbol (construct, constant, or env-var assignment), not against another doc — and the list of symbols to check should appear in the detail's Why section, not be re-derived per fix.** — Docs drift in matched pairs (both deployment.md and runbook.md can be wrong the same way), so cross-checking one wrong doc against another wrong doc fails silently. The construct/handler is authoritative; the docs are downstream. _(e.g. Verifying Advice Service's slug required reading `AdviceServiceApiKeySecret` in `amplify/resources/secrets.ts`, two IAM grants in `amplify/bedrock/resource.ts`, AND `get_advice_service_api_key()` in `agent.py` — all three agreed on `apikey`, which beat the two docs that agreed on `api-key`.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-20 via detail:app-parameter-standardization#2</sub>
- **When a step encodes multiple concrete corrections plus new-content additions in its title, the detail should preserve that bundle (don't split mid-detail) but should explicitly enumerate each correction with its current/correct/source-of-truth triple at the top of Why.** — Bundled scope titles signal the create heuristic saw a shared canonical anchor (here: step 1's standard doc). Splitting would fragment cross-links; not enumerating leaves a reviewer guessing which fix is which. _(e.g. Step 2 here had three distinct fixes — Advice Service slug, LaborDataService scope, sandbox-vs-pipeline VPC callout — all anchored to `docs/secrets-and-parameters.md`. Treating them as one step kept the cross-links consistent.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-20 via detail:app-parameter-standardization#2</sub>
- **Doc-only steps need a grep harness as their Tests section: exact ripgrep patterns with expected hit counts (zero hits for what was wrong, ≥1 hit for what should now be there). Generic 'reviewer reads the diff' is insufficient — corrections regress quickly and CI can run grep.** — Path/scope corrections are mechanical and binary; a grep harness gives a reviewer a one-line pass/fail signal and lets CI catch regressions if the wrong path creeps back in. _(e.g. Step 2's harness: `grep -n 'advice-service/api-key' <3 docs>` must return zero hits; `grep -n 'advice-service/apikey' docs/deployment.md` must return ≥1 hit.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-20 via detail:app-parameter-standardization#2</sub>
- **When multiple docs cover overlapping ground (deployment guide, runbook, architecture doc), the detail must declare one canonical doc and demote the others to cross-linkers — otherwise the same fact drifts into N places and the next correction repeats the same chase.** — The reason a doc-correction step exists at all is that earlier authors duplicated rather than linked. The detail must not perpetuate the same drift it's fixing. _(e.g. Step 2 demoted `docs/backend-guide.md`'s 'use paths like /app/{environment}/...' sentence to a one-line link to `docs/secrets-and-parameters.md`, rather than rewriting the path pattern inline.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-20 via detail:app-parameter-standardization#2</sub>
- **When a step's prose calls for a 'callout' or 'preface' subsection in an existing doc, the detail should commit to an exact heading text, a target parent section, and a relative insert position — not leave 'add a subsection about X' open.** — Without a heading + parent + position, the next implementer either invents the structure (drift) or stalls asking. The grep harness also needs a stable heading to assert against. _(e.g. Step 2 specified heading `### VPC parameters: sandbox vs pipeline`, parent `Backend Environment Variables`, position 'appended', so the test `grep -n 'VPC parameters: sandbox vs pipeline' docs/deployment.md` is well-defined.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-20 via detail:app-parameter-standardization#2</sub>

- **When create proposes a 'make X the canonical/documented path' step on a live working artifact (script, config, schema), the detail's acceptance criteria must include diff invariants that mechanically prove no behavior changed — e.g. unchanged call count, byte-identical flag parsing, untouched defaults — so a reviewer or CI grep can catch accidental behavior creep in a doc-only step.** — Doc-and-comment edits to a working artifact silently invite scope drift (reorderings, 'while I'm here' refactors). A diff invariant is the one cheap mechanical check that catches it before review. _(e.g. Step 4 added 'git diff scripts/create-ssm-parameters.sh against main shows only header-comment lines added/changed' and 'Grep of create_parameter against the script returns exactly twelve hits' to acceptance criteria.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-20 via detail:app-parameter-standardization#4</sub>
- **When a plan title or body contains a parenthetical about existing state ('X already present at lines N-M', 'Y already configured'), the detail should treat it as an audit claim to validate via Grep/Read first, not as gospel — and record the audit conclusion in the Why or a 'Pre-implementation audit' subsection so reviewers see the basis for any 'no new entries needed' decision.** — Parentheticals from create are a heuristic snapshot. If they're stale, the detail silently inherits the staleness; if they're accurate, surfacing the audit makes the decision defensible. Either way it changes the detail's first task from drafting to reconciliation. _(e.g. Plan title said '(Bedrock entries already present on lines 91-95)' — detail's Implementation §0 audited the full inventory before concluding no new create_parameter calls were needed, then re-stated that conclusion as a diff invariant in acceptance criteria.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-20 via detail:app-parameter-standardization#4</sub>
- **For doc-only steps that point readers at a live source artifact, cite the artifact's internal section headings, function names, or echo strings verbatim — not paraphrased — so future grep-based navigation from the doc to the source still works.** — Paraphrased cross-references rot the moment someone renames the source section. Verbatim citations are self-healing: as long as the source name exists, the link works; if it's renamed, the doc visibly breaks at the same time and gets fixed. _(e.g. Detail cited `SNS Push Notification Parameters:` / `AssessmentService Integration Parameters:` / `Bedrock Model ID Parameters:` / `Build Configuration Parameters:` verbatim from the script's existing `echo` statements as the cross-reference vocabulary between the standard's table and the script body.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-20 via detail:app-parameter-standardization#4</sub>
- **Detail's Implementation section should enumerate explicit 'do NOT change' rules (signatures, ordering, defaults, flag names) for steps that touch a working artifact alongside the 'do change' list — otherwise the next implementer treats adjacent improvements as in-scope.** — When the scope is 'document existing behavior', proximity to the file invites unrelated tidying. Listing 'no flag changes', 'no reordering', 'no default-value changes' as explicit non-changes is cheaper than relitigating in review. _(e.g. Detail's 'Non-obvious decisions' under Implementation §1 said: no flag changes (no --instance-name yet), no reordering of the four blocks, no default-value changes to the Bedrock model IDs. Each one closed a plausible scope-creep door.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-20 via detail:app-parameter-standardization#4</sub>

- **When a step renames or removes a CDK env-var binding (`addEnvironment('FOO', ...)`), every Lambda handler that reads `process.env.FOO` is an implicit edit. Create should grep both sides and list the consumers in `files:`.** — Step 5 declared 7 files but required edits to 7 additional Lambda handlers (uStrive client, webhook handler, LaborDataService OAuth manager, Vendor scraper handler, versions probe, Python `labor_data_service_oauth.py`, plus `backend.ts`). Detail had to discover each consumer by grepping `USTRIVE_API_KEY_SECRET`, `LaborDataService_CREDENTIALS`, etc. — work create could have done up-front. _(e.g. Renaming `USTRIVE_API_KEY_SECRET` → `USTRIVE_API_KEY_PARAM` in `amplify/resources/mentors.ts` forces edits in `amplify/lambda/utils/ustrive-client.ts`, `amplify/lambda/ustrive-webhook/handler.ts`, etc.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-20 via detail:app-parameter-standardization#5</sub>
- **When the same logical responsibility exists in multiple languages (TS Lambda + Python agent), credential / config / API-client migrations must touch every language implementation. Create should grep the class or function name across `.ts`, `.py`, `.js`.** — Detail discovered `LaborDataServiceOAuthTokenManager` lives in BOTH `amplify/lambda/utils/labor-data-service-oauth-manager.ts` AND `amplify/bedrock/bg-agent/labor_data_service_oauth.py`. A migration that updated only the TS version would have left the Python agent broken on next cold start — a defect the CDK build wouldn't catch. _(e.g. Step 5's `files:` named only `amplify/bedrock/chat-agent/agent.py` for Python work; `bg-agent/labor_data_service_oauth.py` was discovered during detail drafting.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-20 via detail:app-parameter-standardization#5</sub>
- **When a migration retires a CDK resource/configurator, the detail must explicitly decide "delete the file" vs "leave an empty stub" and justify it. Default to delete unless the file carries load-bearing comments worth preserving.** — An empty `createSecrets` no-op invites the next contributor to refill it, defeating the standardization. Detail had to write this decision into a `Non-obvious design decisions` section because create proposed listing the file as edits only. Future create should propose deletion (with the call-site + index re-export removals) when the function would become a no-op. _(e.g. Step 5 deleted `amplify/resources/secrets.ts` rather than leaving an empty `createSecrets` — and that required also touching `amplify/resources/index.ts` and `amplify/backend.ts`, which were not in `files:`.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-20 via detail:app-parameter-standardization#5</sub>
- **Removing a CDK resource file touches three places, not one: the file itself, the barrel re-export (`resources/index.ts`), and the call site in the backend entrypoint. Create should list all three together.** — Detail had to add `amplify/backend.ts` (and implicitly `amplify/resources/index.ts`) to the edit list. Listing only the resource file makes the step look smaller than it is and lets a careless implementer leave dangling imports. _(e.g. Removing `amplify/resources/secrets.ts` required removing `export * from './secrets'` from `amplify/resources/index.ts` and the `createSecrets(backend, instanceName)` call from `amplify/backend.ts`.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-20 via detail:app-parameter-standardization#5</sub>
- **When a step is annotated `splittable`, the detail's Deferred section should propose a specific PR order (simplest first), with one-line justification per slice. "Splittable" alone gives the implementer no decision support.** — Detail had to invent the order (scalar-secret → single-consumer credentials → shared-helper credentials → multi-language credentials) because the splittable flag conveys *possibility* but not *ordering*. Future plans for splittable migrations should bake the order into the detail. _(e.g. Step 5 Deferred ordered: webhook-secret → Advice Service → Vendor → uStrive credentials → LaborDataService credentials. Each is independently deployable because old + new can coexist during cutover.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-20 via detail:app-parameter-standardization#5</sub>
- **When a step closes a previously-accepted security finding, the detail's acceptance criteria must require updating the finding's doc in the same step (or explicitly defer it). Otherwise the finding stays "accepted" in perpetuity.** — Detail had to add an acceptance criterion that `docs/security/findings/CRITICAL-3.md` reflect the migration; create did not flag the finding doc as load-bearing. Security-finding docs are easy to forget because they live outside the changed code's directory. _(e.g. Step 5 migrates the `SecretValue.unsafePlainText` placeholder pattern that `CRITICAL-3.md` accepts. Acceptance criteria call out the doc update; full "accepted → resolved" transition is deferred.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-20 via detail:app-parameter-standardization#5</sub>
- **For credential-source migrations (e.g. Secrets Manager → SSM SecureString), the detail should default to: rename env vars to force-fail-loud at deploy + an operator-side data-copy runbook + no dual-read fallback. Bundling two SDKs and a fallback ladder in every consumer is more expensive than a one-shot cutover.** — Detail had to argue this choice in §12 because create provided no opinion. Future credential-migration steps benefit from this being the codified default. _(e.g. Step 5 §11 documents `aws secretsmanager get-secret-value | aws ssm put-parameter --type SecureString` per env, then a single CDK deploy per env. Force-failure via env var rename catches missed migrations.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-20 via detail:app-parameter-standardization#5</sub>
- **Migration steps that move data between AWS stores need an operator-side runbook (copy live values, validate, then delete the old store entries) — it isn't code, but it gates the deploy. Detail should include it as an Implementation subsection, not as Deferred.** — Without the runbook, a code-only PR leaves prod broken on deploy (placeholders go live, real values stay orphaned). Create's complexity heuristic should account for this when rating migration steps. _(e.g. Step 5 §11 ("Operator-side data migration") is required for the migration to be safe; omitting it would have produced a code-correct, prod-broken PR.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-20 via detail:app-parameter-standardization#5</sub>

- **After drafting a detail body, reverse-sweep it for backticked file paths and reconcile every cited path with step.files: add real new/modified files to the manifest, and strip body mentions that are only ambient context (cross-references, examples) so the manifest stays a true edit-list.** — Detail bodies naturally accumulate many path citations (anchors, examples, cross-refs) that aren't actually edited; the review's files-coherence check flags every one as drift, drowning real signal. Doing the sweep at detail-write time is cheap; doing it during review wastes a review pass. _(e.g. This review surfaced 60+ INFO files-coherence findings across steps 1-5 because details cited paths like `amplify/backend.ts`, `docs/secrets-and-parameters.md`, and `scripts/create-ssm-parameters.sh` that weren't in step.files. Many were legitimate edits missing from the manifest; others were just anchor references that should have been omitted from `files:`.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-20 via review:app-parameter-standardization</sub>
- **Sentinel placeholders that `create` emits when no real files are known (e.g. `(no declared files cited in detail body)`) must be removed from step.files before the detail is saved — they are tooling artifacts, not paths, and they pollute later coherence/heuristic checks.** — A literal English sentence in `files:` is never a real path, and leaving it there means every future `review` flags it as 'declared but not mentioned in detail'. It also misleads downstream tools (status heuristics, grep-based audits) into searching for nonsense tokens. _(e.g. Step 1 here had `(no declared files cited in detail body)` as its sole files entry, which produced a NIT files-coherence finding and otherwise contributed nothing.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-20 via review:app-parameter-standardization</sub>
- **When `plan-tool review` reports that core plan body sections (`## Overview`, `## Problem statement`, `## Architecture`, `## Risks`, `## Testing`, `## Deferred`, `## Out of scope`) are still `_(TODO)_`, treat it as a `create`-time defect that blocks meaningful step review — fix the body first, then re-run review, rather than reviewing steps against an empty plan frame.** — Step-level review depends on the plan's stated scope, risks, and out-of-scope boundaries to judge whether a step's `files`/acceptance criteria are right-sized. Reviewing details against a TODO-only body lets steps drift without a backstop, and stacks up repeat MEDIUM findings that aren't actionable until the body is filled. _(e.g. This plan returned seven MEDIUM body-completeness findings (Overview, Problem statement, Architecture, Out of scope, Risks, Testing, Deferred all `_(TODO)_`) — none can be resolved by step-level edits.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-20 via review:app-parameter-standardization</sub>

- **When a plan modifies the tooling itself (plan-tool, build scripts, lint rules, the review heuristics), expect title-symbol token-collision hints to fire on the tool's own source files. Weight those hints lower than usual — the tool's vocabulary necessarily matches the plan's vocabulary, so matches are near-certain even when nothing is implemented.** — Self-referential plans are a known false-positive class for the prior-implementation heuristic. Reviewers (and the model summarizing findings) should not over-index on these matches, and create/detail should not treat them as evidence the work is partially done. _(e.g. Plan `reconcile-subcommand-split` triggered hints on tokens `Direction`, `Decision`, `reconcile`, `Intent` — all matched against `.agents/skills/plan-tool/` files (review.py, apply-finding.md, RUNBOOK.md, SKILL.md), which are exactly the files the plan proposes to modify.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-20 via review:reconcile-subcommand-split</sub>

- **For 'mark superseded / retire / archive' steps in any plan-managing tool, read the status enum source BEFORE drafting — the obvious literal (`superseded`, `archived`, `retired`) is often absent, forcing supersession to be encoded as `blocked` + a free-form note. Detail should justify the encoding explicitly so a reviewer doesn't propose a 'cleaner' schema edit mid-review.** — Create proposed a low-complexity one-file edit, but actually executing it required schema archaeology to discover that the natural vocabulary doesn't exist. Surfacing this in the detail prevents a downstream reviewer from re-litigating the choice. _(e.g. `plan_tool/schema.py: PlanStatus` has no `superseded` value, so the migration pointer rode `status: blocked` + a per-step `status_note`.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-20 via detail:reconcile-subcommand-split#1</sub>
- **When the target file's schema is Pydantic with `extra="forbid"`, the detail must call out which existing free-form field carries any new metadata (and forbid inventing new frontmatter keys) — otherwise the next implementer will silently add a field and break parse.** — `extra="forbid"` is invisible from the file itself; you only see it by reading the schema. Without an explicit callout, a 'cleaner' frontmatter design will be proposed and silently break validation. _(e.g. Both `Plan` and `Step` models declared `ConfigDict(extra="forbid")`; the detail explicitly forbade adding `superseded_by:` and routed the pointer through `status_note`.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-20 via detail:reconcile-subcommand-split#1</sub>
- **Before writing any 'retire / replace / archive' banner or marker text by hand, grep the tool's own source for an existing helper that emits the same string (e.g. a `_replacement_notice()` or equivalent) and quote it byte-for-byte. Canonical-marker drift across in-place vs wizard-driven paths is a hidden bug that's costly to fix later.** — Most plan/doc tools have a create-time path that already emits the canonical supersession string. Hand-rolling a near-identical banner creates two slightly-different markers that future tooling has to parse as one. _(e.g. `plan_tool/create.py: _replacement_notice()` exists for the wizard-driven archive flow; reusing its exact two-line blockquote kept the in-place supersession marker identical to the wizard's output.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-20 via detail:reconcile-subcommand-split#1</sub>
- **Supersession steps face a 'move to archive/ vs leave in place' fork. Acceptance criteria must pin the chosen branch explicitly (assert path unchanged OR assert path under archive/), or a future reviewer will 'fix' the layout and silently break references from later steps in the new plan.** — In-place supersession is correct when the new plan's later steps still reference the old plan's `plan-details/<slug>/` folder; otherwise an archive move is cleaner. Either choice is defensible — only the un-pinned choice is dangerous. _(e.g. Step 6 of the new plan references `implementation-plans/plan-details/intent-preserving-reconcile/` artifacts; the detail pinned the file at its original path and asserted `git diff --stat` shows exactly one file touched.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-20 via detail:reconcile-subcommand-split#1</sub>

- **Steps that add new template/prompt files in a system using string interpolation must include the available placeholder set as an explicit acceptance criterion (grep for `\$\{` returns only known tokens).** — `string.Template.safe_substitute` and similar interpolators silently preserve unknown `${...}` tokens rather than raising — a typo in a placeholder produces a broken template at render time with no Python-level error to catch it. The placeholder set is the only mechanical contract between prompt author and renderer. _(e.g. Step 2 added `${plan_title}` / `${step_detail}` / `${lessons_section}` etc. to two new prompts; without the explicit acceptance check, a typo like `${step_files_}` would survive into claude's rendered input.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-20 via detail:reconcile-subcommand-split#2</sub>
- **When a step deletes a file or symbol whose callers won't be updated until a later step, the detail must explicitly name the 'broken window' and the step that closes it — phrased so reviewers understand the breakage is intentional and the batch must ship together.** — A detail that just says 'delete X' (with cleanup deferred) reads as a regression to reviewers and to anyone who runs the half-applied plan. Naming the window converts an apparent bug into a documented atomic-batch constraint. _(e.g. Step 2 deletes `prompts/reconcile.md` but `cli.py` / `auto_reconcile.py` still pass `mode="reconcile"` until steps 5/6 — the detail called this out as 'intentionally broken between landing step 2 and step 5' so the steps ship as one batch.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-20 via detail:reconcile-subcommand-split#2</sub>
- **When a step creates two near-mirror-image files (same skeleton, different parameter), put 'factor out the shared fragment' in the Deferred section by default — extract only after both files stabilize through one iteration.** — Mirror-image files look like prime DRY candidates but the extraction tax is real (an extra include layer, two readers must trace through indirection) and the two files often diverge in non-obvious ways once real use cases land. Premature extraction creates rework when one side has to break the shared shape. _(e.g. Step 2 produces `detail-reconcile.md` and `run-reconcile.md` with the same `## Scope and the post-apply guard (FYI)` and `## Output contract` sections; the detail explicitly defers shared-fragment extraction and flags mirror drift as a known risk.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-20 via detail:reconcile-subcommand-split#2</sub>
- **For 'delete file X' steps, before writing the detail, grep for every site that constructs the file's path (string literal, `Path(...) / "name"`, glob, dynamic mode-based lookup) and route each hit to either 'fixed in this step' or 'deferred to step N (named)' — do not rely on the proposed `files` list alone.** — Create's proposed `files` list captures the file being deleted but not its consumers. Renderer/dispatch layers that build the path dynamically (`prompts/<mode>.md`) hide consumers behind a variable, so they're invisible to a path-based search of `reconcile.md` and easy to miss. _(e.g. `prompts/reconcile.md` was loaded via `prompts/<mode>.md` from both `cli.py` and `auto_reconcile.py`; a literal grep for `reconcile.md` finds zero Python references — the detail had to grep for `mode="reconcile"` and `args.mode == "reconcile"` instead.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-20 via detail:reconcile-subcommand-split#2</sub>
- **When a system has a family of similar artifacts (multiple prompts, multiple resolvers, multiple handlers) and a plan touches one or two, the detail should explicitly enumerate the siblings it is NOT touching and explain why each is out of scope.** — Readers and downstream implementers assume 'reconcile prompts' means all of them. Naming the survivors (and the structural reason they're different) prevents scope creep in later steps and prevents follow-up plans from re-litigating the boundary. _(e.g. Step 2 retires `reconcile.md` but explicitly preserves `reconcile-deep.md` because it has a different dispatch path and an extra `acceptance_bullet` variable — without the explicit callout, a future step might 'clean up' both.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-20 via detail:reconcile-subcommand-split#2</sub>

- **When a step's `files:` list includes modules that are already abstraction-clean for the new dimension being added (mode/kind/source/literal), the real diff in those files collapses to docstring/comment enumeration of the new literal. Detail should explicitly label each file as 'behavior-change' vs 'doc-hygiene'; create should treat steps that are mostly doc-hygiene as one bucket smaller in complexity.** — Misleading 'medium' complexity ratings under-prepare reviewers for what's actually a routing-table touch in one file plus three enumeration updates. The reviewer expects N substantive changes; they get 1 substantive + (N-1) comment edits and lose trust. _(e.g. Step 3 here listed 4 files; launcher.py and review_persistence.py only needed MUTATING_MODES / SavedReview.kind docstring enumeration of the new 'detail-reconcile' literal because both modules already accept arbitrary kind/mode strings.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-20 via detail:reconcile-subcommand-split#3</sub>
- **CLI-flag-addition steps must specify mutual-exclusivity with every existing flag on the same subcommand as a first-class design decision in the detail (which existing flags are compatible, which are mutually exclusive, which are silently ignored). Default to enumerating them all in a small table.** — Create proposals routinely leave this implicit because the step title focuses on the flag's primary behavior. The next implementer then guesses, or worse, ships without an `add_mutually_exclusive_group` and the flag combination silently does the wrong thing. _(e.g. Step 3's --reconcile flag had to address compatibility with --step (allowed, required), --iterate (rejected via mutually-exclusive group), --dry-run (rejected at handler top), and --force (ignored as no-op). None of this was in the step title.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-20 via detail:reconcile-subcommand-split#3</sub>
- **When a step adds a new regex sibling to an existing routing-table regex family, the detail must explicitly address ordering precedence (which pattern is tried first and why) AND specify a test that guards the precedence — not just a test that the new pattern matches.** — Regex changes are a common source of silent mis-dispatch when a future loosening of either pattern makes them overlap. Without an ordering test, the bug surfaces only in production behavior, not in CI. _(e.g. _DETAIL_RECONCILE_PAT had to be tried before _DETAIL_PAT in recognized_action; the detail had to specify a `takes_precedence_over_detail` test in addition to the matches-correctly test.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-20 via detail:reconcile-subcommand-split#3</sub>
- **When multiple sibling steps in the same plan will introduce near-identical helpers (here: step 3's detail-reconcile save+walk, step 4's run-reconcile save+walk), the first step's detail should specify a parameterization of the existing helper rather than duplication. Calling this out at detail-time keeps the second step's diff small and prevents two-of-three-flows-updated drift.** — If step 3 ships a duplicate, step 4 inherits the choice and the third flow (the original) keeps drifting from the new two. Detail is the right layer to make this decision because it sees the dependency graph that create's per-step focus doesn't. _(e.g. Step 3 parameterized `_save_and_walk_reconcile` with a `kind` kwarg defaulting to 'reconcile' so steps 3, 4, and the existing reconcile path all funnel through one helper.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-20 via detail:reconcile-subcommand-split#3</sub>
- **For multi-step plans where later steps remove code that earlier steps touch, the detail's Deferred section must enumerate 'do NOT touch X — step N owns its deletion' with file:symbol pointers, not just 'X is deferred to step N'. The negative phrasing prevents an over-eager implementer from cleaning up the gate at the same time they add the new path.** — Sequential-step plans are routinely worked in parallel branches, and a step-3 PR that also rips out a step-6 symbol creates a merge conflict that doesn't surface until step 6 starts. Explicit don't-touch lists prevent the conflict at PR-author time. _(e.g. Step 3's Deferred section had to enumerate _REFUSED_VERDICT, _ALLOWED_PLAN_UPDATE_JUSTIFICATIONS, parse_decision_block, the ## Decision rollup, and the Direction enum as 'owned by step 6 — touching them here conflicts'.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-20 via detail:reconcile-subcommand-split#3</sub>
- **A new literal (kind/mode/source/status) that flows through a routing system needs the detail to grep for and enumerate every membership set, frozenset, Literal type, docstring, and inline comment that lists 'known' literals. Create can't anticipate the full set; the detail must inventory it.** — Missing one membership set produces a 'works in 4 of 5 places' bug that surfaces only when the missed code path runs. The inventory is mechanical (`rg "\"reconcile\""`) but easy to skip. _(e.g. 'detail-reconcile' had to land in _APPLY_AS_FINDING_SOURCES (frozenset), MUTATING_MODES docstring (enumeration prose), SavedReview.kind comment, _timestamp_filename docstring, RecognizedAction.kind docstring + inline comment, and the save_review kind parameter docstring — six call sites for one new literal.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-20 via detail:reconcile-subcommand-split#3</sub>
- **For test enumeration, detail should first identify the existing test file structure (which file the new tests slot into, alongside which existing test cluster/class), then list new cases with a one-line purpose each. 'Add tests' as a bullet is insufficient; reviewers need to know where the diff lands.** — Without test-file enumeration up front, implementers either create a new test file (breaking the per-module organization convention) or scatter tests across the wrong files. The location is part of the design. _(e.g. Step 3's Tests section listed tests by destination file (test_cli.py, test_launcher.py, test_review_persistence.py, test_review_walk.py) and by existing test cluster they extend — making it clear which test class each new case slots into.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-20 via detail:reconcile-subcommand-split#3</sub>

- **Treat `not X` / `not Y` qualifiers in a step title as load-bearing design decisions, not editorial asides — preserve them verbatim from create through detail, and re-state the contrast prominently in detail's Implementation.** — These qualifiers encode a chosen architecture by exclusion (e.g. 'audit applies inline, not per-finding apply') that determines the entire shape of the step. If the detail re-derives the design without anchoring on the exclusion, the next implementer or reviewer can drift toward the rejected alternative. _(e.g. Step 4's title said 'reuses run's file-write machinery (not apply_finding)' — that single 'not apply_finding' clause pinned the audit-applies-inline design and meant no new code-apply prompt was in scope.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-21 via detail:reconcile-subcommand-split#4</sub>
- **When a CLI surface formats an operator-facing string from model output (a `[REDIRECT]` line, a `next: ...` suggestion, an error message), construct the canonical form CLI-side from known invocation parameters; echo the model's text only as the human-readable diagnosis portion.** — Prompt wording drifts between runs and across model upgrades. If integration tests assert on a string that came from the prompt, they break on harmless prompt edits. CLI-constructed strings keep the test contract stable against prompt drift. _(e.g. The redirect short-circuit emits `next: plan-tool detail <slug> --reconcile --step <N>` (CLI-constructed from `args.name` + `rec.step_id`) instead of echoing claude's `**Suggested next**:` field verbatim.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-21 via detail:reconcile-subcommand-split#4</sub>
- **When a later step in the same plan deletes code wholesale (per `depends_on` ordering), the current step's detail should explicitly enumerate the symbols it is *not* touching, by name and file, with a one-line reason ('dies in step 6').** — Without an explicit 'Not touched' guard, the temptation during implementation is to bundle 'while-I'm-here' edits — which then conflict with the later step's deletion diff and leave the codebase in a half-removed state if the later step is delayed. _(e.g. Step 4's detail listed `apply_finding`, `_REFUSED_VERDICT`, `_ALLOWED_PLAN_UPDATE_JUSTIFICATIONS`, `_format_decision_section`, `parse_decision_block`, the `Direction` enum, `--strict-intent`, and `_format_decision_rollup` as 'not touched here, deleted in step 6'.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-21 via detail:reconcile-subcommand-split#4</sub>
- **`argparse.add_mutually_exclusive_group` does not work cleanly when one of the mutex arguments has a non-None default — argparse cannot distinguish 'user passed nothing' from 'user explicitly passed the default value'. Use a post-parse rejection check against `args.<name> != <default>` instead.** — Easy to over-promise the mutex via `add_mutually_exclusive_group` and end up with a flag combination that should be rejected slipping through silently (or rejecting the default-equivalent invocation that should be allowed). Spec'ing the post-parse check up-front saves a re-do during implementation. _(e.g. `--reconcile` had to be mutex with `--mode <anything-other-than-default-'run'>` and with `--iterate`. The `--mode` default of `'run'` meant the mutex had to be `args.reconcile and args.mode != 'run'`, not an argparse-level group.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-21 via detail:reconcile-subcommand-split#4</sub>
- **When a step's primary deliverable is 'extend the literal set' across docstrings, inline comments, and parameter descriptions (no signature or behavior change), call out each doc-hygiene edit explicitly in Implementation — name the file, the symbol, and the before/after — so reviewers do not dismiss the step as trivial and so the edits actually land.** — Documentation edits that 'just' add a string literal to an enumeration look like noise in a diff and get cut by an implementer trying to keep the change minimal. Explicit enumeration in the detail makes them part of the contract. _(e.g. `review_persistence.py` needed no signature changes but had four doc sites (`SavedReview.kind` inline comment, `SavedReview` docstring producer paragraph, `_timestamp_filename` docstring, `save_review` `kind:` parameter line) that all had to list `'run-reconcile'`.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-21 via detail:reconcile-subcommand-split#4</sub>

- **Deprecation-alias steps should explicitly specify the banner emission mechanism (`print(..., file=sys.stderr)` vs `warnings.warn`) in the implementation section — the choice is load-bearing for the test strategy, not a free implementer decision.** — `warnings.warn` is filterable, stack-frame-sensitive, and respects `PYTHONWARNINGS`, which fights `capsys`-style assertions and makes the banner non-deterministic across environments. Stating the mechanism up front saves the detail-drafting run from rediscovering it when writing the banner-content test. _(e.g. Step 5 here had to call out the choice because tests like `test_alias_prints_deprecated_banner_to_stderr` pin the banner as a single line via stderr capture; switching to `warnings.warn` would have silently broken those tests.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-21 via detail:reconcile-subcommand-split#5</sub>
- **When a CLI alias dispatches across two subparsers (e.g. `run --mode X` → `detail --X`), the detail must require extracting a shared helper that takes the *intersection* of attributes the two subparsers actually populate — never synthesize an `argparse.Namespace` from one to call the other's command function.** — Subparsers in real CLIs compose different parent parsers and produce different attribute sets; silently defaulting the missing attributes creates a fragile coupling that breaks the next time either subparser grows a flag. The detail surfacing this avoids a subtle bug class. _(e.g. Step 5's alias could not call `cmd_detail(args)` directly because `cmd_detail`'s parser populates `force`, `no_lessons`, `no_resume` that `cmd_run`'s parser doesn't — the detail mandated a `_run_detail_reconcile_path(...)` helper instead.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-21 via detail:reconcile-subcommand-split#5</sub>
- **For deprecation steps, the detail's Deferred section must explicitly enumerate what stays as dead code now vs. what gets deleted in the follow-up cleanup step, and confirm the cleanup step's `depends_on` points back at the alias step.** — Bundling the deprecation banner with dead-code deletion in one step couples a behavior change to a deprecation and produces a `git diff` that's hard to review for either half. Naming the deferred deletions by symbol routes scope to the right step. _(e.g. Step 5's Deferred section explicitly handed `_save_and_walk_reconcile`, `_walk_saved_reconcile`, `_prompt_reuse_saved_reconcile`, and the `if args.mode == "reconcile":` blocks to step 6, leaving the alias diff scoped to two files.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-21 via detail:reconcile-subcommand-split#5</sub>
- **When a step deprecates a value in an argparse `choices=` tuple (or any other parser-level allowlist), the detail must explicitly keep the deprecated literal in the tuple until the alias is removed — and route the removal to the cleanup step.** — Removing the literal pre-deprecation turns the soft warning into a hard `argparse: invalid choice` error before the alias's banner can fire, defeating the entire transition-window pattern. Easy to overlook because the alias guard fires inside the command function, not at parser construction. _(e.g. `AVAILABLE_MODES` in plan-tool's `cli.py` must continue to contain `"reconcile"` during step 5's window; step 6 owns its removal. The detail called this out as a deferred item to prevent a well-meaning implementer from over-cleaning.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-21 via detail:reconcile-subcommand-split#5</sub>
- **When an alias forwards an invocation into another subcommand's code path, audit/run-log emitters should record the verb the operator *typed*, not the dispatch target — typically via a phase event around the dispatch rather than opening a second logger.** — The operator-facing audit trail should reflect operator intent; opening a separate log under the target verb makes `runs list` confusing and obscures the deprecated invocation count, which is exactly the signal needed to decide when to remove the alias. _(e.g. Step 5 specified that the alias opens a `command="run"` `RunLogWriter` with a `deprecated_alias_dispatch` phase pair, not a `command="detail"` log — so `plan-tool runs list` still surfaces the deprecated invocation under its original verb.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-21 via detail:reconcile-subcommand-split#5</sub>
- **Compat-shim steps that introduce load-time value translation in a persistence layer should explicitly cite an existing precedent in the same loader (if one exists) and mirror its in-memory-only behavior — never rewrite the file on disk.** — Loaders frequently accumulate multiple legacy-value translations over time, and consistency between them (translate in-memory, leave bytes alone, document the rewrite in the dataclass docstring) is what keeps the pattern reviewable. Citing the precedent in the detail makes the reviewer's job mechanical instead of judgmental. _(e.g. Step 5's `load_review` rewrite of `kind == "reconcile"` → `"detail-reconcile"` was structured to mirror the existing `Annotation.status` rewrite of `"done"` → `"applied"` — same translate-on-load, never-write-back rationale documented in the same place.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-21 via detail:reconcile-subcommand-split#5</sub>
- **When a compat-shim step depends on a prior step's filename-filter narrowing, the detail must verify that the narrowed filter still admits the legacy artifacts the shim is supposed to keep working — and broaden it back if needed.** — A prior step that scoped a substring/glob filter to the new naming convention will silently exclude legacy files, making the compat shim untestable end-to-end. The interaction is invisible from the step description and only surfaces when writing the reuse-prompt test. _(e.g. Step 3 may have narrowed `_find_saved_reconcile_for_step`'s pre-filter from `"-reconcile" in p.name` to `"-detail-reconcile" in p.name`; step 5's detail had to broaden it back so legacy `*-reconcile.json` saves still reach the in-memory `kind` check that the load-time rewrite normalizes.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-21 via detail:reconcile-subcommand-split#5</sub>

- **For deletion / teardown steps, the create-time audit should grep every named symbol (constants, classes, functions, regex names, kwargs, flag strings) across the *whole* tree — including `prompts/`, `lessons/`, and operator docs — and surface every match as either an in-scope file or an explicit out-of-scope deferral, instead of relying on the heuristic's same-file already-implemented hits.** — The heuristic on this run flagged review.py and apply-finding.md, but the real blast radius included a `--strict-intent` mention in concept-completeness.md and several `Decision`/`Direction`/`REFUSED` references in lessons/general.md. Discovering them mid-draft cost reads and forced an ad-hoc 'cross-file ripples' subsection. _(e.g. Step 6 of Reconcile Subcommand Split: 6 files in scope per create; detail had to add prompts/concept-completeness.md and lessons/general.md as in-step ripples, plus defer reconcile-deep.md and the operator-doc trio.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-21 via detail:reconcile-subcommand-split#6</sub>
- **Complexity for deletion steps should scale on *distinct named symbols removed* and *fan-out call sites touched*, not file count. A 6-file edit that removes ~40 named symbols + threads through 8+ call sites is L, not M.** — Step decomposition that treats deletion as 'medium' because it touches 6 files under-budgets review attention. The same fan-out as a feature build deserves the same complexity bin. _(e.g. Step 6 removed _ALLOWED_PLAN_UPDATE_JUSTIFICATIONS, _REFUSED_VERDICT, Direction, Decision, parse_decision_block, _DECISION_*_RE (6 regexes), _decision_field_re, _DIRECTION_TOKENS, --strict-intent (+ ~8 strict_intent= kwargs), DecisionRollupEntry, _format_decision_rollup, _render_rollup_bullet, _build_decision_log_entries, decision_log, refused_ids, allowed_justifications, _justification_token, _format_decision_section, and decision_section across launcher/review/cli/auto_reconcile/renderer/apply-finding — rated 'medium' but had L-scale fan-out.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-21 via detail:reconcile-subcommand-split#6</sub>
- **Deletion-step details should include an explicit 'Survives' / 'What stays' section, not just 'What goes'. The implementer's mental load is half-driven by knowing where to stop — and over-deletion is the failure mode that breaks downstream alias contracts.** — On this run, the 'stays' list (triage code, parse_apply_verdict, MUTATING_MODES, `_run_deep_for_step`, surviving `mode="reconcile"` callsites used by the deprecation alias) was as load-bearing as the 'goes' list. Without explicit enumeration, the implementer is one careless `git grep | xargs sed` away from removing the alias's helpers. _(e.g. Step 6's detail had to spell out that `_walk_saved_reconcile`, `_save_and_walk_reconcile`, `_iterate_reconcile_loop`, and `cmd_run`'s `args.mode == "reconcile"` branch all stay because step 5's deprecation alias dispatches into them.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-21 via detail:reconcile-subcommand-split#6</sub>
- **When a step has `depends_on: N`, the detail should cite *what specifically* about step N is load-bearing for this step's surviving callsites — a one-sentence contract statement, not just the dependency edge.** — Dependency edges in the frontmatter say 'after step N'; they don't say 'because step N's deprecation alias dispatches into helper X, helper X stays.' That contract has to be explicit or the next implementer relitigates it. _(e.g. Step 6's Why section had to state: 'step 5's `cmd_run` alias dispatches into the new detail path while `review_persistence.py: load_review` translates legacy `*-reconcile.json` files to `kind="detail-reconcile"` on read' — without that, the surviving `mode="reconcile"` callsites look like an oversight.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-21 via detail:reconcile-subcommand-split#6</sub>
- **When deleting a symbol, grep for `from X import Y` patterns at *every* indentation level, not just module top. Lazy imports inside function bodies are silent symbol-lifelines that keep 'deleted' code reachable and produce surprise ImportError-at-import-time when the last lazy importer also goes.** — A symbol removed from review.py would have left the codebase importable as long as `_build_decision_log_entries` in cli.py kept its lazy `from plan_tool.review import Direction, parse_decision_block`. The deletion order matters: lazy importers must die before (or with) the symbols they pin. _(e.g. Step 6 caught two lazy imports: one in `launcher.py: apply_finding` (`from plan_tool.review import Direction, parse_decision_block`) and one in `cli.py: _build_decision_log_entries` (same line). Both had to be enumerated as explicit deletes or the 'removed from review.py' acceptance criterion would fail at module import time.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-21 via detail:reconcile-subcommand-split#6</sub>
- **Deletion-step details should enumerate the now-obsolete test cases by name in the Tests section so the test-removal hand-off (typically a later step) is mechanical. Don't leave it as 'step N owns tests'.** — Listing the doomed tests by file + test function name converts a 'discover and decide' problem into a 'delete this list' problem, which a different implementer (possibly under context-load pressure) executes correctly with no rediscovery cost. _(e.g. Step 6's Tests section listed ~25 test cases by name across test_review.py, test_launcher.py, and test_cli.py, partitioning them into 'delete' vs 'keep with legacy-fixture comment' — step 8's deletion pass becomes a checklist instead of an investigation.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-21 via detail:reconcile-subcommand-split#6</sub>
- **When the plan's acceptance criteria contradict a surviving call site discovered during detail-drafting, the detail should *flag and defer* the contradiction in a 'Deferred' subsection rather than silently honor one or the other.** — The plan author may not have known the surviving call site existed. Silently honoring the acceptance criterion deletes load-bearing infrastructure; silently honoring the call site leaves the criterion permanently unmet. A flagged deferral is the only safe move — it routes the decision back to the plan author. _(e.g. Step 6's plan-level acceptance criterion said 'prompts/reconcile.md and prompts/reconcile-deep.md no longer exist', but `launcher.py: _run_deep_for_step` still consumes reconcile-deep.md for the unrelated `verify --deep` pipeline. The detail deferred reconcile-deep.md to a follow-up and flagged the inconsistency in the PR description, leaving the decision to the plan author.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-21 via detail:reconcile-subcommand-split#6</sub>
- **Steps that touch a hand-curated 'lessons learned' or 'BACKLOG' file should treat the prune as part of the step, not a documentation-rewrite follow-up. Lessons systems re-inject their content into future tool runs; stale rules become future cost.** — Lessons captured by previous review runs are read by future detail-drafting / review prompts. If a deletion step retires a feature but leaves the lessons that name it in place, the feedback loop keeps re-asserting dead guidance — and the next plan inherits a confusing context. _(e.g. Step 6's detail explicitly listed `lessons/general.md` hand-prune as in-step work (not deferred to step 7's operator-doc rewrite), because the lessons system is consulted by every future `plan-tool detail` run.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-21 via detail:reconcile-subcommand-split#6</sub>

- **Doc-only steps (markdown rewrites, prose retirements, vocabulary cutovers) have no pytest target; default the Tests section to grep-invariants (negative patterns proving deleted vocabulary is gone, positive patterns proving the new surface is present in every affected file) plus anchor-resolution checks for any [text](#slug) link the rewrite touches.** — Without an explicit alternate-test pattern, detail-drafting drifts toward either inventing a fake test target or omitting Tests entirely. Grep invariants + anchor resolution are the only objectively verifiable signals available for prose churn. _(e.g. Step 7 here introduced 16 verifiable invariants (`grep_no_match_strict_intent`, `anchor_resolution_README_toc`, etc.) covering deleted vocabulary, new surface presence, and slug resolution across 3 files.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-21 via detail:reconcile-subcommand-split#7</sub>
- **When a step rewrites prose across multiple files that cross-reference each other (e.g. README's TOC links into RUNBOOK, SKILL's frontmatter quotes README terminology), the detail must enumerate the cross-file links explicitly and add them to acceptance criteria — not assume each file is independently editable.** — Rewriting one file in isolation breaks anchors in the others silently.
