Markdown renderers don't flag broken intra-repo `[text](other.md#slug)` links, so the failure mode is invisible until a reader follows the dead link. _(e.g. Step 7's README `## Table of contents` had a `[Intent preservation in reconcile](#intent-preservation-in-reconcile)` bullet; rewriting the H2 to `## Per-subcommand reconcile` required updating that TOC line in the same edit, and RUNBOOK/SKILL each carried their own cross-link that had to move in lockstep.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-21 via detail:reconcile-subcommand-split#7</sub>
- **When the step is 'rename / rewrite section X', cite every edit by its unique identifier text (H2 heading, H3 heading, frontmatter field name, table-row key cell, bullet lede phrase) rather than line number — prose edits shift line numbers underneath any subsequent edit in the same file, so line-number anchors invalidate themselves mid-run.** — A detail that says 'edit line 247' is wrong by the time the editor lands edit #1 above it. Heading-text anchors stay stable across the rewrite and let the implementing agent apply edits in any order. _(e.g. Step 7's per-file implementation block cited every target by heading text (`### apply-finding REFUSED — update-plan with non-allow-listed Justification`), table-row left cell (`Refuse every reconcile plan-edit this run`), and frontmatter field (`description:`), never by line number.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-21 via detail:reconcile-subcommand-split#7</sub>
- **When a step documents a deprecation alias (the old surface still works but prints `[DEPRECATED]` and dispatches to the new one), the detail's Deferred section MUST explicitly schedule the alias-removal cutover as a follow-up, naming the code references that will need editing.** — Deprecation aliases become permanent fixtures by default — the cutover never happens unless it's tracked somewhere. The detail is the right place because it's the document that introduced the alias to operators. _(e.g. Step 7's Deferred section named the alias-removal cutover, the specific code references (`args.mode == "reconcile"` branch in `cli.py: cmd_run`, the load-shim in `review_persistence.py: load_review`), and the doc surfaces that would need a follow-up pass when removal lands.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-21 via detail:reconcile-subcommand-split#7</sub>
- **When create's 'already-implemented signal' matches a heading or symbol that the step's title explicitly says to rewrite / replace / retire, treat the match as a false positive, not as evidence the work is done — the heuristic only knows the symbol exists, not whether it carries the old semantics or the new ones.** — Create's deterministic grep can't distinguish 'heading present and stale' from 'heading present and current'. A future detail that takes the signal at face value will under-scope the step or skip it entirely. _(e.g. Step 7's signal matched `### Reconcile` in RUNBOOK.md and `### Intent` in SKILL.md — both were the targets to rewrite, not evidence of prior work. A naive read would have concluded step 7 was a no-op.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-21 via detail:reconcile-subcommand-split#7</sub>

- **When a step is 'restore X that a refactor dropped', the detail audit must enumerate both producer call sites and consumer call sites — silent-degradation bugs hide where one half of a producer/consumer contract is missing and the other half fails open on default values.** — Default-`None` dataclass fields flowing through a default-permissive gate (or a fallback default in a downstream getter) mask the regression — no exception fires, tests stay green, only behavior drifts. Calling out producer+consumer at create time prevents the detail from rediscovering the failure mode mid-draft. _(e.g. Here `Recommendation.impact`/`.complexity` defaulted to `None`; `_triage_level` returns `-1` on `None`; `should_apply_by_triage` then returns `True`. The producer (`extract_reconcile_recommendations`) was the only broken half, but nothing alerted on it.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:complete-per-subcommand-reconcile#1</sub>
- **When the plan's Testing section calls out partial / reordered / one-sided variants of an inline tag as tolerated input, prefer multiple independent per-key regexes over a single combined block regex — and say so explicitly in the detail's Implementation section so a reviewer doesn't shorten it.** — A combined regex like `\(impact:\s*(...),\s*complexity:\s*(...)\)` couples the two captures and silently drops the entire tag whenever one half is missing or keys are reordered — which is exactly the regression that motivated the fix. Independent regexes degrade gracefully. _(e.g. This step's edge variants `(impact: medium)`, `(complexity: high)`, and reordered `(complexity: low, impact: high)` all require independent `.search()` calls; a single anchored regex would have failed all three.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:complete-per-subcommand-reconcile#1</sub>
- **Normalize parsed strings (lowercase, strip) at parse time even when downstream consumers already normalize — the parsed value becomes the source of truth for any other reader (loggers, summary renderers, saved-state diffs) that doesn't go through the consumer's normalizer.** — Relying on consumer-side normalization leaks unnormalized strings to any alternative reader. Parse-time normalization keeps `Recommendation.impact` directly assertable in tests and renders consistently wherever the field surfaces. _(e.g. `_triage_level` lowercases its argument, but `emit_triage_summary` and other downstream readers print `rec.impact` directly — mixed-case captures would surface to operators.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:complete-per-subcommand-reconcile#1</sub>
- **When a wrapper function exists over the function being fixed, dedicate one test to the wrapper specifically — even when the fix is trivially inherited. Otherwise a future wrapper edit (e.g. adding preprocessing) can silently break the contract.** — Inherited behavior is a load-bearing contract that doesn't survive refactors unless pinned. A single `test_wrapper_inherits_X` case is cheap insurance. _(e.g. `extract_run_reconcile_recommendations` is a thin wrapper over `extract_reconcile_recommendations`; the new triage-population fix lands in the base but `test_wrapper_inherits_population` pins the inheritance independently.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:complete-per-subcommand-reconcile#1</sub>

- **When a step lists multiple files for a 'scrub dead references' cleanup, the detail should verify each file actually contains the target pattern before drafting — declared files that produce no diff create reader confusion and should either be removed from `files:` or get an explicit 'no edits, included for cross-reference clarity' callout.** — Create heuristics often include 'natural sibling' files (e.g. cli.py + review.py) speculatively. If the sibling has no matches, the declared scope misleads reviewers into hunting for missing edits. _(e.g. Step 2 listed `plan_tool/review.py` in `files:` but grep confirmed zero `## Decision` / `strict_intent` matches there; detail had to add a 'no edits required, declared for scope clarity' paragraph to head off PR-review confusion.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:complete-per-subcommand-reconcile#2</sub>
- **For rename steps, the detail must enumerate three classes of references to update: (a) the `def`/declaration, (b) live call sites, and (c) **cross-reference comments in sibling functions** (`# Mirrors X`, `# See X`, `# Like X`). Class (c) is the most-missed and is where stale renames silently rot.** — Grep on the identifier alone catches (a) and (b); cross-reference comments only surface if you search the surrounding sibling function's commentary. Missing them leaves a 'phantom peer' that confuses the next reader. _(e.g. Renaming `_dispatch_reconcile` → `_dispatch_detail_reconcile_legacy` required updating the live call site *and* the `# Mirrors ``_dispatch_reconcile``` comment at the top of sibling `_dispatch_detail_reconcile` — without the third edit the cross-reference would point at a non-existent symbol.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:complete-per-subcommand-reconcile#2</sub>
- **For 'scrub stale references' steps, the detail must partition references into **live** (production code, runtime help strings, live test fixtures) vs **dormant** (skipped-test docstrings, intentionally-retained legacy parsers, deprecation comments) — and the acceptance criteria must explicitly carve out the dormant set.** — A naive grep-zero acceptance criterion forces deletion of references that are deliberately retained (e.g. parser comments describing legacy data-shape support), causing scope creep and loss of load-bearing 'why' commentary. _(e.g. Step 2's `grep ## Decision` acceptance had to carve out `plan_tool/launcher.py` (parsing still chunks legacy `## Decision` sub-blocks in replayed saves) and `@pytest.mark.skip`-decorated test docstrings in `tests/test_review_walk.py`.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:complete-per-subcommand-reconcile#2</sub>
- **When renaming a function in a `def X` / `def X_legacy` pair, the detail should explicitly state that the rename is **alias-only signaling** and that the body is byte-identical — and add a `git diff` / `inspect.getsource` acceptance check that the body did not drift.** — Renames can accidentally become rewrites; without a 'body unchanged' invariant, a future reviewer can't quickly confirm scope wasn't violated, especially when the rename's stated motivation is 'mark this as alias-only'. _(e.g. Acceptance check `inspect.getsource(_build_review_action_fn)` + 'body byte-identical to prior `_dispatch_reconcile` body' kept the rename from silently turning into a refactor.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:complete-per-subcommand-reconcile#2</sub>
- **When the detail removes kwargs from test fixtures because production code no longer reads them, explicitly note the language/library quirk that made the kwargs harmless (e.g. `argparse.Namespace` accepts arbitrary kwargs silently, dataclass `**kwargs` capture, Pydantic `extra='allow'`). This both justifies the cleanup as cosmetic and warns future contributors that adding fields to such fixtures won't be caught by type-checks.** — Without the explanation, a reader assumes either the kwargs were already broken (why didn't tests fail?) or the deletion is risky (will tests now fail?). The library quirk explains 'why nothing broke when production stopped reading the field'. _(e.g. Detail called out that `argparse.Namespace(strict_intent=False, …)` accepts the field silently, so the six fixtures had been carrying dead weight since the `--strict-intent` action was deleted, and removing them is purely cosmetic.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:complete-per-subcommand-reconcile#2</sub>
- **When a rename target lives in a closure (function-local `def`), the detail should explicitly state that the symbol's blast radius is the enclosing function only — no module imports, no monkeypatch targets, no cross-file references — so reviewers don't unnecessarily check downstream modules.** — Closure-local symbols look superficially identical to module-level symbols in a diff; without the scope callout, a reviewer wastes time grepping the broader codebase for callers that cannot exist. _(e.g. `_dispatch_reconcile` was a local inside `_build_review_action_fn`, so the rename's scope was that closure alone — the detail stated this explicitly to head off downstream-import audits.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:complete-per-subcommand-reconcile#2</sub>

- **"Prune ~N stale references" steps are not low-complexity — they require enumerating every reference, partitioning each into delete-vs-rewrite (and ideally keep-as-is), and for rewrites designing a concrete swap target. Rate them medium minimum and budget detail time accordingly.** — Create heuristics see "edit one markdown file, delete some bullets" and emit low. The real work is the partition table — every bullet is a judgment call about whether the rule generalizes beyond its dead example, and rewrites require finding a live artifact in the same plan to cite. A low rating under-budgets review and produces ad-hoc prunes that miss the rewrite/delete distinction. _(e.g. This step had ~29 candidate entries (plan body said ~23); 14 deletions, 14 rewrites, 1 keep-as-is — none of which were derivable without reading each entry's rule + example pair.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:complete-per-subcommand-reconcile#3</sub>
- **When editing accumulated-bullets files (lessons, changelogs, decision logs), anchor each edit by a stable in-line token (e.g. trailing provenance tag + first bolded phrase), not by line number. Apply edits top-to-bottom in file order.** — Line numbers shift with every edit; even surrounding-context anchors break when adjacent entries are deleted. A trailing tag like `<sub>added YYYY-MM-DD via …</sub>` plus the entry's lede is stable across the whole edit pass. The detail should explicitly state the anchoring convention so the next implementer doesn't open the file at L:1500 and start counting. _(e.g. Detail anchored each of the 29 candidate entries by `<sub>via detail:<slug>#<n></sub>` + the first bolded sentence; line numbers were never cited because every delete shifts every following entry.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:complete-per-subcommand-reconcile#3</sub>
- **When *rewriting* an entry in an accumulated-lessons-style file, preserve the rule's wording verbatim and swap **only** the example clause. Resist the urge to also "clean up" the rule's prose.** — Lesson/rule systems often run a de-duplication or equivalence pass over rule bodies. Rewording a rule can cause future consolidation to (a) treat the rewrite as a near-duplicate of itself and prune it, or (b) lose the entry's searchability for readers who remember the original lede. The example clause is the lossy, project-specific part; the rule is the durable part. _(e.g. Step 3 here swapped each `_(e.g. …)_` italic clause to cite a surviving artifact but left every bolded rule sentence byte-identical, explicitly to avoid colliding with the `find_lesson_equivalences` consolidation pass.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:complete-per-subcommand-reconcile#3</sub>
- **When rewriting (not deleting) an entry, append to its provenance trail rather than replacing it: keep the original `<sub>added YYYY-MM-DD via …</sub>` and add `; rewritten YYYY-MM-DD via detail:<slug>#<step>`.** — Provenance tags are how future review/detail runs trace why a rule exists. Replacing the tag orphans the rule's history; deleting+re-adding loses the original-add date. Append-only preserves audit trail and lets a positive grep invariant count the rewrites separately from the unchanged entries. _(e.g. Detail specified `<sub>added 2026-05-15 via detail:intent-preserving-reconcile#1 ; rewritten 2026-05-22 via detail:complete-per-subcommand-reconcile#3</sub>` and added a positive invariant `grep -cE 'rewritten 2026-05-22 via detail:complete-per-subcommand-reconcile#3' == 14` as an acceptance criterion.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:complete-per-subcommand-reconcile#3</sub>
- **Acceptance criteria for prune/scrub steps must include both negative invariants (dead-symbol grep counts == 0) AND positive invariants (rewrite-count, unchanged-anchor-count, single-file diff scope). Negative-only acceptance is satisfied by an empty file.** — `grep -cE 'DeadSymbol' file == 0` is true if the file is empty, byte-identical to base with no `DeadSymbol`, or accidentally scrubbed of unrelated content. Pairing it with "this specific marker appears exactly N times" and "git diff --stat shows one file, net-line-count drops by ≥ deletion count" gates against over-prune and under-prune in the same check. _(e.g. Acceptance combined six `grep -cE … == 0` invariants for dead symbols with `grep -cE 'rewritten 2026-05-22 …' == 14`, `git diff --stat` showing one file path, and a byte-identity check on the H1 header + lead paragraph + step-2's lesson entry.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:complete-per-subcommand-reconcile#3</sub>
- **Partition tables for delete-vs-rewrite-vs-keep should explicitly list keep-as-is entries that look like candidates but aren't. Reviewers need proof you considered them, not just the entries you touched.** — A reader inspecting the partition can't distinguish "deliberately left alone" from "missed" without a row for it. Listing one or two keep-as-is rows (with a one-line "already cites post-state correctly" note) shows the audit boundary and prevents a later "why didn't you also fix X" round-trip. _(e.g. The step's partition table included a `via detail:complete-per-subcommand-reconcile#2` row with verdict "keep as-is" — explicitly listed to confirm the entry added by step 2 of the same plan was inspected and intentionally left untouched.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:complete-per-subcommand-reconcile#3</sub>
- **Create-time "~N references" estimates are approximate; expect detail to find 1.2–1.5× more candidates after the partition pass. Don't flag the discrepancy as scope creep — flag it as accurate enumeration.** — The heuristic that produces "~23" probably counted occurrences of one canonical symbol; the partition surfaces adjacent entries that mention related-but-distinct dead vocabulary (enum members, helper names, flag names, prompt block titles). Detail should reconcile the actual count to the plan body in prose rather than silently treating the higher count as the truth. _(e.g. Plan body said "~23 stale references"; detail's partition table enumerated 29 candidates (28 in-scope + 1 keep-as-is) and called out the under-count in prose rather than quietly editing the body.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:complete-per-subcommand-reconcile#3</sub>

- **When a step extends an existing pattern to a sibling surface (e.g. adding flag X to subcommand B because it already exists on subcommand A), the detail must explicitly name the sibling's argparse shape AND its test class as the template, by file:line/class-name. Without that anchor the implementer re-derives the shape from scratch and may diverge subtly.** — Symmetry across subcommands is load-bearing for users and for grep-based ACs, but the 'follow the existing pattern' instruction is invisible unless the pattern is cited concretely. _(e.g. Step 4's detail had to point at `p_detail.--reconcile` (inside `p_detail_mode` mutex group) AND `p_run.--reconcile` AND `TestRunReconcileDispatch` as templates for the new `p_review_all_audit` group + `TestReviewAllReconcileDispatch` class — none of which create surfaced.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:complete-per-subcommand-reconcile#4</sub>
- **For argparse mutex-group steps, the detail should explicitly justify `add_mutually_exclusive_group(required=True)` vs post-parse mutex check — the choice hinges on whether either branch has a default value, not on style preference. Get this wrong and the parser either accepts no-flag invocations silently or rejects valid default invocations.** — Both shapes look interchangeable in passing but have opposite semantics for the no-flag case. A step title that says 'mutually-exclusive group' under-specifies which shape; the detail must pick deliberately. _(e.g. On `p_run` the legacy pattern is post-parse mutex because `--mode run` is the default; on `p_review_all` neither flag has a default so `add_mutually_exclusive_group(required=True)` is the right shape. The detail's 'design alternative considered' section made the trade-off explicit so the implementer doesn't second-guess.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:complete-per-subcommand-reconcile#4</sub>
- **When a step changes load-bearing banner / log text (e.g. `[NOTE]` → `[DEPRECATED]`), acceptance criteria should include both a positive grep for the new token AND a negative grep for the old token at a specific scope. Tests should assert on substring presence in captured stderr, not on exact-line equality.** — Banner wording is the contract — both users and tests grep for it. A test that pins the full line locks future copy-editing; an AC that only checks the new token leaves the old `[NOTE]` line dead-on-arrival in the file. Both directions need verification. _(e.g. Step 4 included `grep -n '\[NOTE\]' ... | grep review-all` returning no hits AND `capsys.readouterr().err` containing `"[DEPRECATED]"` to pin the swap from both sides.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:complete-per-subcommand-reconcile#4</sub>

- **Doc-only steps changing a canonical CLI/API surface should be sized by the count of distinct citations (greppable hits), not the count of files. Create-time audit should run the deprecated-string grep across the doc set and report the citation count alongside `files:`.** — Three files with seven citations behaves like a medium-complexity step, not a low one — each citation lives in different prose (code fence, table row, bullet, frontmatter YAML) with different surrounding constraints. File count hides that. _(e.g. Step 5 listed 3 files at `low` complexity, but `grep -nE 'review-all[^\n]*--mode reconcile'` returned hits in 7 distinct doc contexts plus 1 SKILL.md frontmatter field — each requiring per-context edit logic.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:complete-per-subcommand-reconcile#5</sub>
- **Acceptance criteria for canonical-spelling-change steps must pair two greps: a positive grep ('canonical spelling appears in each of these named contexts') and a negative grep ('deprecated spelling appears only inside the alias sub-section'). One-direction greps miss regressions where a reviewer deletes the canonical citation along with the deprecated one.** — A single grep for the new spelling passes even if the editor accidentally removed half the canonical citations; a single grep for the old spelling passes if the editor leaves a stale canonical citation alongside the new one. _(e.g. Step 5's acceptance pairs `grep ... '--mode reconcile'` (must only hit inside two named alias sub-sections + frontmatter) with `grep ... '--reconcile'` (must hit in nine named contexts), so neither direction can silently regress.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:complete-per-subcommand-reconcile#5</sub>
- **When a deprecation alias is being added for a verb, and a sibling verb already has a documented deprecation alias with its own sub-section in the docs, the detail should extend the existing sub-section in lockstep rather than create a new one — the alias-doc structure is template-load-bearing.** — Two parallel alias sub-sections drift independently if they're authored separately. Treating the existing sub-section as a template forces the new alias to inherit phrasing for the [DEPRECATED] banner, save-loader shim mention, and removal-is-follow-up sentence, which keeps the audit surface symmetric. _(e.g. README's `### '--mode reconcile' — one-window deprecation alias` already described `run --mode reconcile`; step 5 extended that same paragraph for `review-all --mode reconcile` instead of opening a new section.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:complete-per-subcommand-reconcile#5</sub>
- **CLI-usage code fences in docs should mirror the parser's argparse usage-string convention (e.g. `(--a | --b)` for `required=True` mutex groups, `[--a | --b]` for optional). Picking doc syntax to match the parser shape avoids drift when the parser changes.** — Doc readers and chat agents both rely on the usage fence as ground truth for the flag's contract. If the fence advertises `--mode <m>` as a bare optional but the parser enforces a `required=True` mutex with `--reconcile`, every reader infers the wrong shape and the next parser change desyncs further. _(e.g. Step 5 replaces `... --mode <m> [--max-steps N]` with `... (--reconcile | --mode <m>) [--max-steps N]` because step 4's parser uses `add_mutually_exclusive_group(required=True)`.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:complete-per-subcommand-reconcile#5</sub>
- **A doc-only step that follows a code-change step should explicitly enumerate which surfaces belong to which step ('argparse help text → step 4, frontmatter description → step 5') in a dedicated 'Non-edits worth calling out' section.** — Without this, the implementer of the doc step over-edits into the parser step's territory (or vice versa) and the diff fails review for scope creep. Naming the boundary makes the cut auditable. _(e.g. Step 5 lists `Do not touch the argparse help-string text on p_review_all — that belongs to step 4` and verifies it with a grep invariant on `cli.py` that should return identical matches before and after the step.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:complete-per-subcommand-reconcile#5</sub>

- **When a step IS the testing step (its whole deliverable is new tests), the Implementation section should fully enumerate the test methods and the Tests section can recapitulate them tersely rather than duplicate content — avoid the trap of writing 'tests for the tests'.** — Otherwise the detail either ships two near-identical lists (Implementation + Tests) or hides the test methods inside the Tests section where Files/Implementation reviewers won't look. Naming the test methods as the deliverable in Implementation keeps the seams visible. _(e.g. Step 6 here had three test methods (`test_canonical_reconcile_stdout_routes_through_extract_and_triage`, `test_run_reconcile_path_inherits_triage_population`, `test_triage_disabled_returns_all_recs_in_apply_bucket`); they're spec'd in Implementation, and the Tests section just lists them with one-line purpose each.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:complete-per-subcommand-reconcile#6</sub>
- **For regression-pinning integration tests, the detail's acceptance criteria should name two or more *independent* assertions that flip under the targeted regression, and walk the silent path that would otherwise pass.** — A single assertion can be flaky; two independent assertions both failing is the load-bearing 'fails loudly' signal a future reviewer can check against. Naming the silent path (which short-circuits, which fields go `None`, which downstream helper no-ops) also makes the acceptance criterion verifiable by reading code rather than re-running tests. _(e.g. Step 6's deliberate-regression criterion calls out that BOTH `len(open_deferred) == 1` and the `"deferring 1 finding(s)"` StringIO substring check flip under a step-1 revert, because `should_apply_by_triage` fails open on `None` and `emit_triage_summary` short-circuits on empty `deferred`.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:complete-per-subcommand-reconcile#6</sub>
- **When `depends_on` is set for plan-sequencing reasons (so the test lands last) rather than a real technical dependency, the detail should call out the distinction explicitly.** — Otherwise a reviewer or implementer assumes step N's code is required to write step M's test, and either introduces an artificial coupling or blocks step M on unrelated step-N churn. Naming it as a 'plan-shape choice' lets the test be written first if needed. _(e.g. Step 6's Summary spelled out: 'Depends on step 5 only because step 5 is the last per-step item that ships code/docs; the test itself depends *behaviorally* on step 1 (the regex that this test exists to pin) and runs cleanly even if steps 2–5 hadn't landed.')_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:complete-per-subcommand-reconcile#6</sub>
- **Pick the test seam where the regression actually lives, not the broadest seam the marker permits — driving the full CLI from an integration test re-validates argparse plumbing already covered elsewhere and dilutes the failure signal.** — Integration markers tempt the broadest possible coverage, but a test that fails with 'something in main() broke' is harder to diagnose than one that fails at the exact module boundary the regression crosses. Detail should justify the seam choice explicitly when an integration test stops short of `main([...])`. _(e.g. Step 6's Implementation section explicitly justifies not driving `main([...])`: the producer→consumer chain crosses two modules, but argparse plumbing is covered by separate CLI tests and including it here would muddle the failure signal.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:complete-per-subcommand-reconcile#6</sub>

- **When a step requires inventing a heuristic (selection rule, threshold, ordering), create should mark it design-required and the detail must commit to the precise rule before writing tests; mid-draft rule-corrections in the body are a smell that the algorithm wasn't pinned down upfront.** — Iterating on the rule mid-document leaves contradictory text (initial statement, then 'Correction:', then updated test assertions) that future readers have to reconcile. Pinning the rule before writing tests also catches counter-examples earlier — here the 'outermost shared ancestor' rule was wrong for the seed-under-inner case and only surfaced when writing the third unit test. _(e.g. Step 7 of plan-tool-reconcile-redesign had a mid-draft correction from 'outermost member of intersection' to 'first cwd-walk .git that is also a seed ancestor' inside TestPickRepoRootForSeed.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:complete-per-subcommand-reconcile#7</sub>
- **When a step changes the observable behavior of a CLI subcommand, include the CLI dispatcher/wiring file in `files:` even if the new code's main weight is in a separate helper module — CLI wiring is load-bearing and easy for create to overlook.** — create's heuristic anchors on 'where does the new helper live?' and stops there, but a helper that nothing calls is dead code. Detail then has to invent the wiring section without create having budgeted for it; complexity ratings under-count as a result. _(e.g. Step 7 proposed `create.py`, `locator.py`, `tests/test_create.py` but the heuristic only fires once `cli.py`'s `_resolve_*_repo_root` is rewired — a fourth edited file that create missed.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:complete-per-subcommand-reconcile#7</sub>
- **When the new code is a validator/gate adjacent to an existing advisory helper (warns vs raises, same diagnostic surface), the create-time codebase audit should locate the adjacent helper and detail should explicitly justify keep-vs-collapse rather than discovering the overlap mid-draft.** — Adjacent helpers that look like dupes often differ on fail-fast-vs-advisory semantics. Discovering the overlap during detail-drafting forces a rationale paragraph that should have been a create-time decision; missing the overlap entirely produces two helpers with drifting diagnostics. _(e.g. `advise_path_issues` (advisory, post-write) and the new `check_manifest_prefix_consistency` (fail-fast, pre-write) share vocabulary (`PathStyleHint`-style examples capped at 3) but coexist intentionally — detail had to invent that rationale on the fly.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:complete-per-subcommand-reconcile#7</sub>
- **Complexity rating should count *distinct surfaces touched*, not LOC or 'one helper, looks simple'. A step that adds a new helper + a new validator + CLI wiring + ~8 tests is at the top of M, and a reviewer should consider whether it should split.** — create-medium often gets read as 'a couple of small edits' by both reviewers and the detail-drafting pass. When the surface count is high, the detail balloons and individual surfaces get short-shrifted. Surface count is a more honest signal than estimated LOC. _(e.g. Step 7 was rated `medium` but touches: a new locator helper with a non-obvious algorithm, a new create-time validator with threshold logic, a new CLI sibling resolver, an informational stderr notice, and two new test classes.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:complete-per-subcommand-reconcile#7</sub>

- **When a step says 'reuse existing primitive X with a small extension', create's `files` list must include the file containing X — parameterizing it is part of this step's diff, not 'pre-existing infrastructure'.** — Create proposed five files but the implementation also had to edit `sweep.py` (to add `archived_suffix` kwarg on `archive_completed_plan`). The detail had to surface this as a sixth file; reviewers reading the original step would miss the cross-file edit. _(e.g. Step 8 reuses `sweep.archive_completed_plan` but extends it with `archived_suffix`. `sweep.py` belongs in `files:` alongside `launcher.py`.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:complete-per-subcommand-reconcile#8</sub>
- **When a step introduces a new module-level constant that is conceptually a sibling of an existing constant (suffix pairs, dirname pairs, enum aliases), put the file holding the existing sibling in `files:` so the new constant lands next to its partner.** — Discoverability erodes when sibling constants live in different files. The detail naturally chose to place `COMPLETED_SUFFIX = '.completed'` next to `REPLACED_SUFFIX = '.replaced'`, but create's files list did not include that file — a less-careful implementer might invent a new home for the constant. _(e.g. `COMPLETED_SUFFIX` belongs next to `REPLACED_SUFFIX` in `plan_tool/create.py`; create's proposal omitted `create.py`.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:complete-per-subcommand-reconcile#8</sub>
- **A proposed file can legitimately end up as 'verification only — no code edit' when the existing primitive already satisfies the new contract. The detail should call this out explicitly (with the invariants the implementer verifies) rather than invent a code edit to justify the file's presence.** — Forcing a token edit into a file just because create listed it produces noise and obscures the real review surface. Better to be honest that the file is in scope as a verification touchpoint. _(e.g. Step 8 listed `parser.py` in files, but `parser.write_plan` already preserves bodies and re-validates frontmatter — no code edit needed; the detail flagged it as a verification-only touchpoint.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:complete-per-subcommand-reconcile#8</sub>
- **When wiring a new flag or context field into a command, grep for ALL call sites of nearby setup/logging helpers (e.g. `RunLogWriter.start`, `subparser.add_argument`) within that command — long command bodies often have multiple parallel call sites that all need the same edit.** — Single-call-site assumptions cause silent omissions: the deprecated alias path or fallback branch keeps logging the old schema while the happy path gets the new field, producing inconsistent run logs that are hard to debug later. _(e.g. `cmd_run` calls `RunLogWriter.start(...flags={...})` from both the standard path and the deprecated `--mode reconcile` alias path; both needed `no_archive` added to the flags dict.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:complete-per-subcommand-reconcile#8</sub>
- **When detail introduces new cross-module imports (especially upward from one helper file to another), explicitly verify no import cycle is introduced and record the dep-graph order in the detail itself.** — Import cycles are easy to introduce when adding `from plan_tool.sweep import ...` to a file that sweep might transitively touch. A one-line 'sweep imports create+schema; launcher imports sweep+create+schema; no cycle' note in the detail saves the implementer a debug round. _(e.g. Step 8's detail had to confirm launcher → sweep → (create, schema) is acyclic before adding `archive_completed_plan` / `classify_plan` imports to launcher.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:complete-per-subcommand-reconcile#8</sub>
- **For steps that add a CLI flag, propose a dedicated parser-level test (`build_parser().parse_args([...])` returning a Namespace with the expected defaults) in addition to dispatch-level tests. Parser tests catch argparse wiring regressions independently of mocked subprocess plumbing.** — Dispatch tests can pass while the flag is wired with the wrong `dest=` or default; a 3-line parser-shape test pins the contract cheaply. _(e.g. `test_no_archive_flag_parses_to_namespace_default_false` asserts `args.no_archive == False` on bare `run rl` and `True` on `run rl --no-archive` — independent of `cmd_run` mock plumbing.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:complete-per-subcommand-reconcile#8</sub>

- **For 'fix stale references' or 'clean up dead test fixtures' steps, detail must re-run the failing command at HEAD before writing scope — a BUG_BACKLOG entry catalogues failures at the time it was written, and partial cleanup often lands in unrelated PRs between the BACKLOG entry and the detail. The actual remaining scope is frequently a small remnant of the originally cataloged list.** — Without this, the detail mis-states scope, acceptance criteria pass trivially against work already done, and the reader can't tell which classes/files still need attention. _(e.g. This step's BUG_BACKLOG entry listed ~36 failures across 6 test classes and ~100 refs in test_cli.py. By detail time, only one skipped method, one fixture-string scrub, and six dead kwargs across one file remained — the other test classes had already been deleted by sibling PRs.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:bug-backlog-drain#1</sub>
- **A file declared in a step's `files:` list may legitimately need zero edits after detail-time audit. When every grep hit on the deleted symbol turns out to be a live fixture or a post-deletion shape-pin test, declare the file 'audit-only' in the Files section and add an explicit acceptance criterion that `git diff` against that file is empty.** — Without an explicit 'declared but no edits' callout, reviewers will treat an empty diff on a declared file as a missed edit. The acceptance criterion turns reviewer doubt into a verifiable invariant. _(e.g. test_launcher.py was in this step's files list but every `## Decision` / `Direction` / `Impact` match belonged to TestSummarizeFindings, TestParseFindingBullets, or TestApplyFindingNoGate — all live or shape-pinning fixtures. Detail documented the audit and added a 'git diff against test_launcher.py is empty' acceptance criterion.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:bug-backlog-drain#1</sub>
- **For test-cleanup steps, every grep hit for a deleted symbol must be categorized into (a) dead fixtures to remove, (b) live fixtures that pin current behavior, or (c) post-deletion shape-pin tests that confirm the deletion — before any are removed. The same string can appear in all three categories in the same file.** — Removing category (b) silently regresses live coverage; removing category (c) loses the only assertion that the deletion stayed deleted. A blanket sweep based on grep is unsafe. _(e.g. The string '## Decision' appears in 7+ tests in test_launcher.py: 4 pin bullet-chunking-parser behavior that still glues sub-blocks to severity bullets, 1 pins the post-deletion 'previously-REFUSED justifications now apply' shape, and the rest are unrelated. None were deletable.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:bug-backlog-drain#1</sub>
- **When the codebase has multiple skip/xfail decorators in the same file, check each `reason=` string for ownership before claiming any of them. Skip decorators are typically owned by specific sibling plan-steps that will land separately.** — Sweeping all skips in a file as 'cleanup' will cross plan boundaries and pre-empt sibling steps. The `reason=` text is the ownership marker. _(e.g. test_cli.py has 5+ `@pytest.mark.skip` decorators; one was in-scope for this step (its reason cited the strict-intent deletion), and the other four cited 'Step 6 of reconcile-subcommand-split deletes the dead loop' — owned by a sibling plan. Detail had to enumerate which were in-scope vs Deferred.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:bug-backlog-drain#1</sub>
- **Pre-existing test failures unrelated to the step should be surfaced as a Deferred bullet with a count, the affected test-class names, and a one-line categorization — not silently inherited. Running the failing test command at HEAD before writing detail is what surfaces them.** — Without this paper trail, the next implementer running the full suite is surprised by red, can't tell what's new vs pre-existing, and may try to fix the pre-existing failures as side scope. The Deferred bullet creates a triage handle. _(e.g. Running `uv run pytest tests/test_cli.py tests/test_launcher.py` at HEAD surfaced 29 pre-existing 'no plan matching' manifest-resolution failures across 11 test classes — all unrelated to strict-intent. Detail logged them as Deferred with class names so they can be filed as a fresh BUG_BACKLOG entry.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:bug-backlog-drain#1</sub>
- **Dead kwargs in keyword-flexible constructors (Python `argparse.Namespace(**kwargs)`, JS `Object.assign`, Ruby `OpenStruct`) linger silently in test fixtures after the consumer stops reading them — they produce no test failures and no runtime warnings. A grep for the dead name is required to find them; they will not surface as red tests.** — After deleting an attribute from production code, the test fixtures that supplied it stay green and look correct. A reviewer reading the fixture later will assume the attribute is still consumed and may add new code that reads it. Explicit cleanup is the only signal. _(e.g. Six `argparse.Namespace(..., strict_intent=False, ...)` calls across TestIterateReconcileLoop* classes were green at HEAD even though `_iterate_reconcile_loop` no longer references `args.strict_intent`. Only a grep for `strict_intent` exposed them.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:bug-backlog-drain#1</sub>

- **When a step's body is 'X is captured but never read,' the create-time audit should grep for the read sites of X (not just the write site) and list them all in `files`. The fix is at the consumption sites, not where the data is produced.** — These bugs look small (low complexity) because the plumbing exists, but the real surface is the set of consumers. Missing one consumer in `files` makes the detail invent it later and complicates the Deferred section. Listing them up front sets correct scope and complexity. _(e.g. Step 2 here: `stats.is_error` is populated in `_parse_stream_line` (already covered by `test_result_is_error_true_surfaces`); the fix is at three reader sites — `_emit_claude_call_end`, `run_step`, and `apply_finding`. Only the first two were in scope; create should have surfaced all three so the boundary was a decision, not an oversight.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:bug-backlog-drain#2</sub>
- **Detail for state-coercion / branching fixes should enumerate the input × output truth table in prose before describing tests, and the tests should be `pytest.mark.parametrize` rows that mirror the table 1:1.** — A coercion fix has a small number of corner cases (often 2×2 or 3×2). Writing the table once gives the reviewer a single source of truth and forces the detail to think about each corner — including the 'raw rc wins over synthesized rc' style asymmetries that are easy to miss when describing the fix narratively. _(e.g. Step 2's `(exit_code, stats.is_error) → effective_rc` table made the asymmetry (raw rc=2 + is_error=True still yields rc=2, not the sentinel) visible before any test was written.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:bug-backlog-drain#2</sub>
- **When a fix has a clean schema-extension form AND an overload-an-existing-field form, the detail should pick one explicitly and route the rejected option to Deferred with the reason. Don't silently choose; state the trade-off.** — Schema extensions cascade into downstream parsers / dashboards. Overloading an existing field is cheaper but loses information (synthesized vs. native is collapsed). Future readers and review-time reconcile need to know this was a deliberate scope decision, not an oversight. _(e.g. Step 2 used `ClaudeCallEnd.rc` as the signal channel rather than adding a new `is_error` field, because adding the field would have pulled `run_log.py` and the `runs profile`/`runs diff` parsers into scope. The Deferred section names this explicitly so a future plan touching `run_log.py` for other reasons can revisit it.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:bug-backlog-drain#2</sub>
- **For steps whose value lies primarily in unblocking a later step, the detail's Why section should name the dependent step and what signal it consumes — not just the bug being fixed.** — A bug-fix step in isolation may look optional ('we've shipped fine without it'). Tying it to a downstream step's contract (e.g. 'step 8 needs a reliable success/error signal before reordering writes') makes the priority defensible at review time and prevents the step from being deferred out of scope. _(e.g. Step 2's Why explicitly states that step 8's `write_plan`/`append_lesson` reorder depends on the coerced exit_code reaching the gate; without that pointer the fix reads as nice-to-have rather than load-bearing.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:bug-backlog-drain#2</sub>
- **For low-complexity coercion / branching fixes, the user-facing message produced by the failure path should be reviewed in the detail and updated if the synthesized failure would be misleading.** — When a fix synthesizes a non-zero exit code from a logical condition, the operator-facing string ('exited with code 1') becomes inaccurate — no process exited 1. The reviewer rarely catches this; the detail must call it out and add a test pinning the alternate message. _(e.g. Step 2 changed `handle.fail(f'claude exited with code {exit_code}')` to a distinct string when the rc was synthesized from `is_error`, and added a test asserting the substring.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:bug-backlog-drain#2</sub>

- **When a step's files include a test file (`tests/test_*.py` etc.), create should also propose any fixture files the test will need — or at least name the conventional fixtures directory — instead of leaving the detail to invent the path.** — Test fixtures are almost always new files with project-specific layout conventions (e.g. `tests/fixtures/<subdir>/`). Detail surfacing this mid-draft costs reads to find a sibling test's `FIXTURES = ...` pattern, and shipping a test that hard-codes a path inconsistent with that pattern is a silent style-break the reviewer has to catch. _(e.g. Step 3's files: list was `create.py` + `tests/test_create.py`, but the detail also created `tests/fixtures/create/vendor-sftp-source.md` to anchor the real-world regression. A hint like `tests/fixtures/create/<vendor>-source.md (new)` in the create proposal would have made this explicit.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:bug-backlog-drain#3</sub>
- **Steps that add a pre-write / pre-side-effect check need the detail to enumerate the target function's call order and pinpoint the insertion line — and the acceptance criteria should include a 'no on-disk side effects on failure' invariant verifiable by reading the temp dir after a forced failure.** — The whole point of these checks is atomicity: a check inserted after archival, lock acquisition, or partial writes corrupts state on failure exactly the way the original bug did. Without a written call-order trace in the detail, the next implementer can land the check in the wrong spot and the test will still pass on the happy path. _(e.g. The step 3 detail explicitly traced `create_plan`'s sequence (resolve metadata → seed_sections → references → collision check → archive → write → manifest → iteration loop) and pinned the new check between references-resolution and collision-archival, with `output_dir.iterdir() is empty` + 'pre-existing collision file is byte-identical' as graspable acceptance criteria.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:bug-backlog-drain#3</sub>
- **When the step adds a new hard-stop check inside a function already covered by tests, the detail should require an explicit audit of pre-existing test cases for incidental trippage and budget for updating any that trip.** — A hard-stop refusal can break tests that exercise the legacy behavior intentionally (e.g. blank-skeleton creation). Without an audit clause, the implementer adds the check, runs only the new test class, sees it pass, and ships — discovering the regressed tests in CI or, worse, post-merge. _(e.g. Step 3's acceptance criteria explicitly named the six sibling test classes (`TestCreatePlanBlankSkeleton`, `TestCreatePlanFromDescription`, etc.) and required them to continue passing, with an 'if any case trips the new check, update + document' clause. Cheap to write, prevents a real regression class.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:bug-backlog-drain#3</sub>

- **For 'fix bug X in module Y' steps, the create-time codebase audit should explicitly check whether the fix's infrastructure (flag, env var, parameter, helper) already exists from prior work — the real gap is often a fallback / guard / diagnostic, not the threading the step title implies.** — Step titles like 'thread X through Y' frame the work as plumbing when the plumbing is already in place; the actual deliverable is a different shape (here: a manifest-anchored fallback helper plus a fail-loud guard). Mis-framing inflates the perceived blast radius and obscures what to test. _(e.g. Step 4's title 'Thread --repo-root through sweep manifest path resolution' implied threading work, but `--repo-root` / `PLAN_TOOL_REPO_ROOT` / `resolve_repo_root` / `build_plan_tool_context(repo_root_override=...)` / `cmd_sweep → _load_context → sweep_plans(repo_root=...)` were all already wired. The real deliverable was `find_repo_root_from_manifest` + an all-missing-entries `ManifestError` guard.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:bug-backlog-drain#4</sub>
- **When create lists test files in `files:`, audit which source modules are actually being modified and include the per-module unit-test file for each — not just the integration test file for the user-facing surface.** — Test conventions are usually per-module (one test file per source module). A step that modifies module A's behavior but only lists module B's test file forces the detail to add the missing test file explicitly and breaks the create→detail file-set contract. _(e.g. Step 4 modified `locator.py` substantively but `files:` only listed `tests/test_sweep.py`. The detail had to add `tests/test_locator.py` (where the existing `TestResolveRepoRoot` and `TestBuildContextWithRepoRootOverride` classes live) and explicitly call out the divergence from create's frontmatter.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:bug-backlog-drain#4</sub>
- **When a step's `files:` list includes a source file but the actual change there is docstring-only / no behavioral change, the detail must say so explicitly and the acceptance criteria should pin the no-behavioral-change invariant (e.g. grep that finds zero new uses of a forbidden pattern).** — Reviewers reading `files:` expect a substantive edit per entry. Silent docstring-only edits look like incomplete work; explicit no-behavior-change framing plus a grep-based invariant makes scope verifiable. _(e.g. Step 4 listed `sweep.py` but the only change was a docstring breadcrumb — the detail pinned `grep -nE 'Path\.cwd\(|os\.getcwd|find_repo_root_from_cwd' sweep.py` returns zero hits as an acceptance criterion to prove the file's behavioral surface didn't regress.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:bug-backlog-drain#4</sub>
- **Bug-fix steps that pin a reproducer should include hermetic-env requirements (env-var unset, CWD pinned) in the test plan as load-bearing fixtures, not as afterthoughts — the env vars that mask the bug in developer shells are exactly the ones that will mask the regression in CI if a test author forgets them.** — Bugs caught only when a specific env var is unset are subtle; if the author's shell has that var set, the bug is invisible during repro and the test passes even without the fix. _(e.g. The 2026-05-21 nested-`.git` divergence was masked when `PLAN_TOOL_REPO_ROOT` was set; the detail's test plan requires `monkeypatch.delenv('PLAN_TOOL_REPO_ROOT', raising=False)` per test class as a load-bearing hermeticity rule, not a polish item.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:bug-backlog-drain#4</sub>

- **For 'make X atomic via stage-then-rename' steps, the detail must explicitly argue the same-filesystem precondition for `os.replace` / `rename` (staging is a sibling of the destination), not assume it.** — `os.replace` is only atomic on the same filesystem. If staging is built under `/tmp` or a different mount, the atomicity claim is silently false. Naming the precondition in Implementation makes the claim falsifiable by a reviewer. _(e.g. Step 5 of bug-backlog-drain put staging at `details_dst.parent / f"{details_dst.name}.tmp"` (sibling of destination), so the cross-filesystem hazard doesn't apply — but only because the detail spelled it out.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:bug-backlog-drain#5</sub>
- **When a step must preserve an exact log/error message string (because tests, log-greps, or operator dashboards depend on it), quote the literal string in the Implementation section and say 'preserve verbatim'.** — Without an explicit preservation note, a future cleanup commit will helpfully 'improve' the wording and silently break downstream consumers. The string is part of the contract, not incidental text. _(e.g. Step 5 had to keep `f"could not archive plan-details for slug {slug!r}: {exc}"` exactly so existing log-grep patterns and sibling-suite assertions stay intact.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:bug-backlog-drain#5</sub>
- **When a step adds a new module-level import to a file that doesn't currently have it, call out the import addition explicitly in the Implementation section (or a 'Symbols added' sub-bullet) rather than burying it in the prose.** — It's a real diff item that's easy to omit when describing function-body changes, and reviewers expect imports to appear in the symbol-change summary. _(e.g. Step 5 added `import os` to `plan_tool/sweep.py`, which previously used only `json` / `shutil` / `pathlib`. Without the call-out, the import is easy to forget when the implementer writes the patch.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:bug-backlog-drain#5</sub>
- **Regression tests that pin a specific implementation choice (e.g., a deterministic staging directory name) should either be flagged in the test docstring as 'pins this internal choice' or be rewritten to assert the contract (no half-state) without naming the choice.** — A test that asserts on `not (path / 'done.tmp').exists()` breaks the day someone refactors to `tempfile.mkdtemp` — even though the atomicity contract still holds. The test must declare which level it's pinning so future refactors know whether breaking it indicates a regression or just renaming. _(e.g. Step 5's `test_pre_existing_staging_dir_is_cleared_before_use` pins the `<name>.tmp` convention; the Deferred section flagged that switching to `tempfile.mkdtemp` would require updating that test.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:bug-backlog-drain#5</sub>
- **When a plan applies the same pattern at multiple sibling steps with an explicit 'inline don't extract' architecture decision, each step's detail should cite that decision in the Why section so reviewers don't flag missing helper extraction as an issue.** — Reviewers reading step N in isolation will reasonably ask 'why is this inlined instead of extracted into a helper?'. Citing the plan-level threshold (e.g., '4th call site triggers extraction') makes the inlining a documented choice instead of an oversight. _(e.g. Step 5 cited the plan-level decision that steps 5/7/8 each inline the stage-then-rename pattern until a 4th call site appears.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:bug-backlog-drain#5</sub>

- **When a step's logic requires the called function to accept a new parameter the function doesn't currently expose, the kwarg-threading is part of that step's work — not a follow-up — and create's `files:` set must include every caller of the changed function. **Why:** A guard / redirect whose decision depends on a value the function can't see is just architecture, not code; detail then has to invent the kwarg and thread it through callers that may live outside the declared `files:` set, either expanding scope or deliberately punting. **How to apply:** When create proposes a step like 'branch X's behavior on Y', grep callers of X for Y's availability. If Y isn't in scope at X today, list both X and X's caller in `files:` and bump complexity.** — Step 6 here required `plan_path` inside `extract_and_append_detail_lessons` so the in-scope check could resolve canonical sinks; create didn't surface this signature change, so detail had to thread it from `generate_detail` and also delete a now-orphaned `_plan_slug_from_path_hint` helper. _(e.g. Create's `files:` listed `detail.py` and `lessons.py` but not the call site inside `generate_detail` that needed to pass `plan_path=plan_path`; detail had to thread the kwarg and prove the fallback helper had zero remaining callers.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:bug-backlog-drain#6</sub>
- **When a flaw has a symmetric twin at a parallel call site, create's scope decision (fix-both vs fix-one) must be explicit at step-proposal time — naming the included site in `files:` and routing the excluded twin to `## Deferred` with rationale. **Why:** Without this, detail discovers the twin mid-draft and either silently expands scope or buries the gap in prose where future review can't find it. The originating bug-backlog wording usually mentions one site but the defect lives at all of them. **How to apply:** When the defect is 'function X does the wrong thing', grep every caller of X's underlying primitive; if siblings share the flaw, declare them in `files:` (fix all) or in `## Deferred` (intentional scope).** — Step 6's bug-backlog entry named `detail`'s extractor, but `review.py:extract_and_append_review_lessons` calls `lessons.append_lessons` with the same scope-blindness; detail had to invent a Deferred entry for the review-side fix rather than have it pre-declared. _(e.g. `append_lessons` has at least two callers (detail + review); create's `files:` covered detail.py only, so detail's Deferred section had to call out the symmetric review-side gap.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:bug-backlog-drain#6</sub>
- **When the plan body's 'Testing' subsection names a test file that isn't in the step's frontmatter `files:`, detail should anchor that as a deliberate addition (mirroring the plan body) under a marked subheading and note it in the commit message — not silently expand scope and not omit the test surface. **Why:** Plan body and step frontmatter drift; the body usually represents more recent thinking. Treating frontmatter as authoritative under-tests; treating body as authoritative looks like scope creep at review. **How to apply:** After parsing `files:`, re-read the plan body's Testing block. Any test file named there but absent from `files:` belongs in detail's Files section under a marked subheading with a one-line rationale citing the plan body.** — Step 6's plan-level architecture named both `tests/test_detail.py` and `tests/test_lessons.py` as test surfaces; the step's `files:` listed only `test_detail.py`. Detail had to invent an 'Addition outside the declared files: set' subheading to keep the `test_lessons.py` additions auditable. _(e.g. `TestAppendPendingLessons` in `tests/test_lessons.py` pins the new `append_pending_lessons` writer — required by the plan body but not in step frontmatter.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:bug-backlog-drain#6</sub>
- **For 'quarantine / pending / scratch' destination patterns, pin the dedupe-scope decision in Implementation with explicit rationale: dedupe within the quarantine, against the canonical sink, or both. **Why:** Both scopes look reasonable in isolation; choosing wrong silently drops bullets the user wanted retained (cross-file dedupe re-triggers the very suppression quarantine exists to dodge) or silently re-appends ones they wanted suppressed (no within-file dedupe). The rationale is the load-bearing part. **How to apply:** When adding any 'appended-to scratch file' pattern, write one sentence in Implementation naming what counts as a duplicate (e.g. 'case-insensitive substring within `pending/<slug>.md` only — canonical-file presence does not suppress').** — Step 6's `append_pending_lessons` had to make a deliberate call: a bullet whose `rule` text already lives in `general.md` still lands in `pending/<slug>.md` — cross-file dedupe would defeat quarantine's purpose. Without anchoring this in Implementation, the reviewer would reasonably ask 'why doesn't this dedupe against canonical?' _(e.g. Detail's Implementation section explicitly stated dedupe scope is 'within the pending file's own body' and noted that mirroring `existing_lower` case-insensitive substring matching from `_append_to_file` is the chosen primitive.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:bug-backlog-drain#6</sub>

- **When the already-implemented heuristic matches a module's namesake symbol (e.g. `def foo` in `foo.py`), treat the signal as unreliable until verified — the regex can't distinguish 'the step's target function' from 'any function whose name overlaps the module'. The detail should explicitly disambiguate in the Why section so a reviewer doesn't re-investigate.** — Wasted time: the signal pointed at the module's public context-manager, not at any partial atomic-write implementation. A single Grep against `tempfile|os\.replace` in the target files resolved it in one call but the detail still had to spell out why the signal was a false positive. _(e.g. Step 7's signal flagged `iteration_lock` in `iteration_lock.py` matching `def iteration_lock`; the namesake context manager has nothing to do with atomic writes. Detail's Why section explicitly grepped for `tempfile|os\.replace` against HEAD to disprove the signal.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:bug-backlog-drain#7</sub>
- **(Outcome recorded 2026-08-02: in plan-tool this deferral ran to four copies before anyone extracted, and the "trigger" was never pulled by the process that set it — a duplication *test* was what finally held the line. Treat "defer extraction until N call sites" as needing an enforcement mechanism at the time you write it, not a note.)** **When an architecture decision is 'inline the pattern, defer extraction until N call sites', the detail should anticipate a per-module-private helper compromise once the call sites span more than one module — strict inlining of an identical 10-15 line block in 3+ places is rarely what the architecture actually intended. Capture the byte-identical-duplication invariant in acceptance criteria so the extraction trigger signal stays legible.** — Two byte-identical helper definitions across modules is the explicit signal for the future extraction step; if they drift, the signal evaporates and the extraction trigger gets blurred. Encoding the invariant in acceptance criteria turns reviewer attention to it. _(e.g. Step 7 added a module-private `_atomic_write_text` to both `review_persistence.py` and `iteration_lock.py`. The plan's 'no shared helper until fourth call site' rule was preserved, but raw inlining at 3 sites would have meant 45+ lines of triplication.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:bug-backlog-drain#7</sub>
- **When a step replaces a 'delete-then-write' sequence with an atomic-write (`os.replace` or similar), the detail must explicitly identify the now-redundant delete and drop it in the same diff — otherwise a reviewer asks why both exist, and a future maintainer may reintroduce the delete on autopilot. Mention it under Implementation and pin it via a grep in acceptance criteria.** — The delete is silently load-bearing today (it's how the overwrite case is handled). After `os.replace`, it's redundant but not obviously wrong; leaving it as-is bloats the diff and confuses the contract. _(e.g. Step 7's `acquire_lock` rewrite dropped a `try: lock_path.unlink() except OSError: pass` block immediately above the new atomic write. Acceptance criteria grep `lock_path.unlink` and assert it only matches inside `release_lock`.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:bug-backlog-drain#7</sub>
- **When a step modifies persistence/state files that integration tests in other modules round-trip through, the detail's Tests section should name those integration test classes (by file + class) even though they aren't in the step's `files:` list. The create heuristic only flags direct test siblings; cross-module integration coverage is the detail's job to surface.** — The plan's testing matrix often calls out these consumer test classes at plan level, but the per-step detail needs to repeat the link so a step-level reviewer can verify them without re-reading the parent plan. A regression that passes step-level tests but breaks integration consumers is exactly the failure mode atomic-write changes invite. _(e.g. Step 7 modifies `iteration_lock.py`'s on-disk JSON, which `TestIterateReconcileLoopDriftPhase` and `TestCmdRunReconcileIterationLoop` in `test_cli.py` round-trip through. Neither file was in the step's declared `files:` list, but the detail named them as 'must stay green' under acceptance criteria.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:bug-backlog-drain#7</sub>
- **Grep-based acceptance criteria like 'no remaining `write_text` callers' need scope qualifiers (which function, which class) to exclude legitimate unrelated uses. A bare `grep -n write_text path` matching three locations isn't a clean pass/fail signal when only some of them should be migrated.** — Without scope, the acceptance grep either over-fails (catches an unrelated write the step didn't touch) or under-fails (passes when the migration is incomplete because a legitimate exception masks the regression). Adding the function name or surrounding context makes the criterion mechanically checkable. _(e.g. Step 7's acceptance: `grep -n 'lock_path.write_text\|lock_path.unlink' iteration_lock.py` must match only inside `release_lock` (the legitimate delete path) — not inside `acquire_lock`. The qualifier 'inside release_lock' is what makes the criterion binary.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:bug-backlog-drain#7</sub>
- **When migrating writes through a helper, preserve the exact pre-existing on-disk byte shape (trailing newline, indent style, key ordering). Even when tests use `json.loads` and are byte-agnostic, an unnecessary byte-shape change churns `git diff` on existing saved state and complicates downgrade-paths.** — Operators sometimes diff saved-state files across runs to debug. Preserving byte shape means a step's diff stays focused on the behavior change rather than incidental formatting drift. _(e.g. Step 7's `save_review` rewrite preserved the trailing `+ "\n"` (which `path.write_text` previously emitted); `acquire_lock`'s rewrite preserved the no-trailing-newline shape. Two callers, two different existing shapes, both pinned.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:bug-backlog-drain#7</sub>

- **When a step title contains a compound conjunction ("X + Y", "reorder + partial-state log"), the detail should enumerate every named part in the Summary AND explicitly surface any unnamed-but-required sub-fix the title doesn't mention — and create should treat compound titles as a signal that low/medium complexity is probably under-rated.** — Step 8's title was "reorder + partial-state log" but the correct fix required a third sub-part (idempotency guard on `append_lesson`) that the title elides — without surfacing it up front, a reviewer of the create proposal would have accepted the step as a simple reorder. _(e.g. Step 8's Summary explicitly leads with "three changes: (a) reorder, (b) make append_lesson idempotent, (c) wrap both writes in a helper with a partial-state log" instead of mirroring the title verbatim.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:bug-backlog-drain#8</sub>
- **`depends_on` edges should be classified as hard (one step's output is the next's input) or soft (serial-ordering convention) in the Why section, so a reader doesn't assume an implementation order that doesn't actually exist.** — Step 8 `depends_on: 7` is purely serial — step 7's changes don't unblock step 8 — but the only step the fix *truly* depends on is step 2 (which makes `run_step` honor `stats.is_error`). Without the classification, a future planner could safely reorder these and miss the real prerequisite. _(e.g. Step 8's Why says: "Step 2 is the load-bearing prerequisite … steps 3–7 do not interact with this surface; the `depends_on: 7` edge is the plan's serial-ordering convention rather than a hard dependency.")_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:bug-backlog-drain#8</sub>
- **When the source bug/backlog entry offers multiple fix flavors ("swap the order OR wrap in try/finally"), the detail's Summary must state whether one, the other, or all flavors are being landed — never leave it implicit in the step title.** — The BUG_BACKLOG entry for this gap offered two alternatives; the step title implied both, but a reviewer skimming only the title could reasonably assume "reorder" was the entire scope and push back on the helper + log. _(e.g. Step 8's Why quotes both BUG_BACKLOG flavors verbatim and commits to "both" before the implementation section.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:bug-backlog-drain#8</sub>
- **Plan-level "Risks" entries that explicitly accept a residual hole should be cross-referenced from the detail's Deferred section, so reviewers don't push to close the hole inside the current step.** — Without the cross-reference, a reviewer (correctly) sees an outstanding failure mode in the detail and asks why it isn't being fixed — even when the plan has already deliberated and accepted the risk. The reference saves the round-trip. _(e.g. Step 8's Deferred section names the residual SIGKILL window and points at the plan's *Risks* section as the source of the accept-and-defer decision.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:bug-backlog-drain#8</sub>
- **When a step touches a function that has another call-site nearby with similar-looking code that should NOT be changed, the detail should call out the untouched sibling by name — pre-empting the "every call-site got the new treatment, why not this one" reviewer push.** — Reviewers scan for consistency. A second `write_plan(plan_path, plan)` call in the same function that isn't routed through the new helper looks like an oversight unless the detail explains why it's deliberately untouched. _(e.g. Step 8's Implementation has a "Deliberately untouched" sub-section pointing at the test-gate-failure `write_plan` call in `run_step` and explaining why the reorder does not apply to it.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:bug-backlog-drain#8</sub>

- **When a step title uses a loose noun phrase (e.g. "add n= column") that doesn't match the shape described in the plan's Risks / Tests sections, detail must reconcile the two and state the chosen interpretation explicitly under Why. Future create runs should treat such mismatches as a complexity-rating signal (low → medium), not a wording detail.** — Step 9's title read as 'one new column' but the Risks/Tests sections actually required (a) a sentinel format inside two existing cells AND (b) two genuinely new columns (min/max). Resolving that ambiguity took a careful read of three plan sections; a create-time flag would have surfaced it up front. _(e.g. Plan step 9 of Bug Backlog Drain: title 'add n= column on tiny `runs profile` buckets' vs Risks 'count and min/max remain visible' vs Tests 'cover that count/min/max remain visible' — three sections, three slightly different scopes, all reconciled in detail.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:bug-backlog-drain#9</sub>
- **For steps that change statistical / aggregate output (quantiles, percentiles, rollups), the create-time audit must grep the proposed test file for assertions that pin specific numeric values from the affected function and list each such test as a must-re-seed alongside the proposed files. "Add the new test class" is rarely the only test-side change.** — Existing tests often pin the wrong-but-stable old behavior as a contract. Re-seeding them is real scope (changing fixture data + recomputing every dependent assertion: total_ms, mean_ms, p50_ms, p95_ms, tokens, cost), but easy to miss when create only thinks 'add a test'. _(e.g. Step 9 had to re-seed `test_profile_aggregates_by_mode_with_p50_and_total` and `test_profile_json_emits_single_line_envelope` — both pinned p50/p95 values computed from n=2 fixtures that the new threshold would suppress.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:bug-backlog-drain#9</sub>
- **Threshold-change steps ("raise the cutoff to N", "suppress below X") almost always replace an existing too-loose carve-out, not just add net-new logic. The detail's Why section should locate and quote the existing wrong threshold so the diff is understood as a replacement, not an addition.** — Without naming the existing carve-out, the reviewer reads the change as 'new gating logic appears out of nowhere' and may miss the single-sample fallback being removed (here: the `else: p50 = float(durations[0])` branch). Quoting the prior code in Why makes the deletion auditable. _(e.g. Step 9's `_summarize_group` already had `if len(durations) >= 2:` with a single-sample fallback that returned `float(durations[0])` as p50. The detail had to explicitly note the fallback was being deleted, not just gated harder.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:bug-backlog-drain#9</sub>
- **When proposing a sentinel format for missing/suppressed numeric values, distinguish text-mode (operator-facing, sentinel string OK) from JSON-mode (machine-facing, must stay typed-null). The detail should pin both conventions and forbid mixing.** — Machine consumers branch on `null`; mixing `null` and `"n=3"` in the same JSON field forces every consumer to handle two types. The text-mode sentinel is unambiguous in context (renderer always emits strings); JSON-mode consumers can derive N themselves from existing count/incomplete fields. _(e.g. Step 9 emits `n=<N>` in the text-mode p50/p95 cells but keeps `p50_ms: null` / `p95_ms: null` in JSON, deferring 'JSON-side sentinel strings' to the Deferred section.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:bug-backlog-drain#9</sub>

- **When a step changes a helper's return contract (e.g. `None → bool`), the detail must inventory every existing exit path in that helper and pin which branch returns which value — `files: [cli.py]` doesn't surface the ripple, and skipping the inventory leaves the caller's new conditional under-specified.** — Return-type changes propagate to every call site; without an explicit per-exit-path mapping, the detail's behavioral guarantees become ambiguous and tests can't pin the boundary cases. _(e.g. Step 10 had to enumerate ~7 exit paths in `_maybe_offer_split_for_create` (not-TTY guard, source-missing, under-threshold, propose_split SplitError, empty sections_to_extract, declined prompt, execute_split SplitError, success-with-empty-written-files, success-with-writes) and decide each returns False except the last.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:bug-backlog-drain#10</sub>
- **The 'already-implemented' heuristic that regex-matches symbol declarations (`class X`, `def X`) flags presence-of-symbol, not presence-of-behavior. A step that adds a new branch *raising* an existing exception class will look 'already implemented' but isn't — the detail should confirm by reading the actual call sites, not the class definition.** — The heuristic produces false positives on behavior-inversion steps that reuse existing types. Trusting it without verification leads to under-scoped detail. _(e.g. Step 10's already-implemented signal pointed to `class CreateError` in create.py, but the work is not 'add CreateError' — it's 'add a new oversize-raise branch'. The class has existed since the file was first written.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:bug-backlog-drain#10</sub>
- **When a step inverts existing behavior, the detail should explicitly name which existing tests must be **deleted** (not just which new ones are added). `files: [test_x.py]` is ambiguous between 'add tests' and 'rewrite tests', and a reviewer can't tell from create's output which it is.** — Behavior-inversion steps that leave the old assertions in place will fail green, but reviewers won't know to look for deletion until they read the diff. Pre-naming the doomed tests prevents drift. _(e.g. Step 10 had to delete `TestReadExistingDescription::test_truncates_oversized_with_marker` and the entire `TestReadExistingDescriptionWarnsOnTruncate` class — both pinned the old truncate-and-warn behavior. The create step's `files` list gave no signal.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:bug-backlog-drain#10</sub>
- **When a step title encodes a state-machine change (contains 'flag', 'mode', 'switch', or 'X-landed bool'), the detail should include an explicit table mapping every input state to the function's return AND the caller's branch — narrative prose alone hides cases.** — State-machine changes have an exponential number of edge cases. Without a tabular enumeration, the detail will miss combinations (e.g. `--yes` + non-TTY + no-extractable-sections) that tests need to cover. _(e.g. Step 10's title 'CreateError + split-landed flag' encoded a 2x4 matrix (split landed: True/False × invocation mode: --no-split-offer/non-TTY/declined/under-cap). The edge-cases section had to enumerate all 8 before tests could be written.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:bug-backlog-drain#10</sub>
- **When the parent plan has an architecture or theme block that names multiple steps by number (e.g. 'Steps 2, 3, 10 invert ...'), the detail's Why should cite the block verbatim instead of re-deriving justification — the reviewer already approved the theme at plan-level.** — Re-deriving justification bloats the detail and risks the detail's reasoning diverging from the plan's. A direct citation keeps the chain of authority intact and shortens the Why. _(e.g. Step 10's Why cited the plan's 'Fail-loudly over silent fallback' block verbatim. Without that anchor, the detail would have had to re-argue why hard-stop > truncate-and-warn from first principles.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via detail:bug-backlog-drain#10</sub>

- **Every concrete file path mentioned in a step's detail body should also appear in that step's `files:` manifest (and every file in the manifest should appear in the prose). When the two drift, downstream tooling has to reconcile two disagreeing sources of truth, reviewers can't trust either, and reconcile heuristics get noisy.** — This review surfaced 40+ files-coherence findings across 10 steps — detail prose named handlers, tests, fixtures, and docs that the machine-readable manifest didn't list. It was the single most common drift mode in the review and dwarfed every other finding category. _(e.g. Step 6's detail referenced `plan_tool/lessons.py`, `tests/test_lessons.py`, `lessons/pending/<slug>.md`, `lessons/general.md`, and `<repo_root>/.agents/skills/plan-tool/lessons/pending/<plan-slug>.md`, none of which were in the step's `files:` array.)_  <sub>added 2026-05-22 via review:bug-backlog-drain</sub>
- **When a step intentionally creates files under a directory that doesn't yet exist, annotate each new path with `(new)` in the step's `files:` array and call out the directory-creation responsibility in the acceptance criteria. Don't rely on the file-existence heuristic to infer intent from context.** — The review's only HIGH finding fired because step 3's referenced fixture path had no existing parent.
